# Determ Security Posture

## Current C99 experiment status — 2026-09-22 (updated 2026-09-23)

The ledger rows below concern the C++ `determ` implementation unless explicitly
marked otherwise. C99 `determ-node` is a separate local pair experiment. C++ proof
closures, including S-044, do not establish C99 consensus safety or liveness.

**Development plan (2026-09-24):** [ADR-004 §8.4](decisions/ADR-004-Fault-Model.md#84-adopted-development-plan-2026-09-24)
adopts the combined two-co-creator direction and starts with receiver/resource and
recovery proofs. Adoption closes no security-ledger item or H proof obligation.
The freestanding target and its compiler/platform/crypto qualification remain
separate from the current hosted reference and local experiment described below.

| C99 item | Current disposition |
|---|---|
| Payload admission | Both commitments and both matching SHA-256 openings are required. Missing/invalid input terminates the local attempt; explicit retry is caller-controlled. No 1-of-2 completion or authenticated membership is claimed. |
| Timeout and storage behavior | The network/CLI propagate attempt failure. Evaluation output is not persisted as a validated block. This removes the misleading finality behavior; it does not implement a chain. |
| Portable time and bounded assembly | QPC conversion uses overflow-safe, saturating arithmetic. The unused, unchecked socket stream bundler is removed; the capped in-memory bundler remains. |
| Difficulty | Corrected helper uses eleven timestamps for ten intervals and checks predecessor-derived work before mutation. Production timestamp validation, block ingestion and reorganization are **open**. |
| Alleged evidence/slashing/fork choice | Unused arbitrary-byte evidence and unvalidated work-comparison APIs are removed. Authenticated evidence and production fork choice remain **unimplemented**. |
| Delay/security model | Custom AES/SHA-256 evaluator is verified by reevaluation. Sequential hardness, hardware advantage, challenge freshness, selective-abort bias and adversarial chain-growth bounds are **unproved**. |
| Sharding and settlement | Authenticated election, state ownership, data availability and reorganization-safe receipts are **open**; ADR-005 is proposed only. |
| C99 routing query | Matches the existing canonical anonymous-address map using bounded, local startup configuration. Strict read-only RPC and live node tests enforce its declared inputs. No genesis authentication or transaction ownership enforcement is claimed. |
| C99 HTTP framing | Portable header-line parsing replaces the undeclared GNU substring search that broke Linux POST requests. A single positive decimal Content-Length is bounded against remaining receive capacity before arithmetic; ambiguous/unsupported framing refuses before RPC dispatch. This is a bounded POST contract, not complete HTTP hardening. |
| C99 pending inbox | Opt-in receiver validates canonical signed anonymous TRANSFER frames, exact local chain identity and intra-shard routing before duplicate/conflict/capacity decisions. At most eight occupied shard buckets with four entries each; smaller data hash resolves pending same-sender/nonce alternatives. No balance/nonce readiness, execution, durability or fair-admission guarantee; arbitrary signing keys can fill the finite inbox. Disabled unless `--pending-genesis` is supplied. |
| C99 recovery model | Finite anchored sibling selection, original-parent replay and requeue use fixed eligibility/receipt oracles. Valid conflicting root bodies retain count/header ranking; smaller transaction data hash applies only during assembly/requeue and cannot override the chosen block or revive stale nonces. The model implements requeue, not production assembly. Competing descendant branches remain unsupported; production recovery and cross-shard dependencies remain open. |
| C99 RPC exposure (2026-09-23) | The HTTP RPC server bound `INADDR_ANY` and answered `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` although its config and the node banner said loopback, so `submit_pending_transfer` was reachable from the network and from any web page. It now binds the configured IPv4 address (default 127.0.0.1, malformed address refused) and serves no OPTIONS/CORS; the test asserts both. **Open:** the server checks no `Host`, `Origin` or `Content-Type`, so a DNS-rebinding page gets same-origin access to the loopback RPC and a cross-origin "simple" POST still reaches `submit_pending_transfer` blind (reproduced 2026-09-23 with a live request); idle-connection slot exhaustion (16 sockets → 503); the mesh's missing handshake timeout. The duel aggregator (`k2_net.c`) and the mesh listen on all interfaces without authentication. A failed `http_rpc_server_init` no longer leaves descriptor 0 in the client table (fixed and gated 2026-09-23). |
| C99 build discipline (2026-09-23) | Every C99 migration target (the 22 `--c99` targets and `determ-c99-node-core`) builds as ISO C99 without extensions and with `-Wall -Wextra -Werror -pedantic` on GCC 13 and Clang 18 (`determ_c99_strict`); `usleep` and silently truncated paths were removed. The shared `determ-crypto-c99` library keeps its own settings (three of its files do not pass `-pedantic -Werror` under GCC). CI also runs the targets under ASan + UBSan with GCC, the crypto library instrumented (`--c99-sanitize`). The root Makefile, never run by CI, was removed. `determ-dsso` and `d5rp` are C99 (`c_std_99`) targets outside this strict set; the code keeps `__attribute__`/`__asm__` behind `__GNUC__` guards. |
| Removed non-OPAQUE wrapper (2026-09-23) | `src/crypto/opaque_dsso.c` claimed to be OPAQUE but implemented an OPRF plus a static, replayable identity proof with no AKE; nothing in production used it and it is removed. The shipped DSSO (`dapps/dsso`, OPAQUE-3DH) is unaffected. |
| C99 transport and codec fixes (2026-09-23) | A mesh frame is queued whole or not at all (a stranded length header used to swallow the next message); the listener accepts until EAGAIN; kqueue `mod()` drops unrequested filters; the transaction decoder returns the whole payload and rejects nonzero padding; header decoders require exact length; gossip dedup covers the message type. These are experiment-level fixes, not a network security claim. |
| C99 accept loop under descriptor exhaustion (2026-09-23) | The event loop is level-triggered since 2026-09-23; the mesh accept loop returns on `EMFILE`/`ENFILE` without draining, so a ready listener makes every poll return at once (a CPU spin) until descriptors free up. The reactor's accept loop (used only by tests) stops on the same errors, and the HTTP RPC accept loop, `poll()`-based and level-triggered before this change too, spins the same way inside `determ-node`'s service loop. **Open.** |
| Standalone ledger boundaries | Self-transfers debit the fee once and advance the nonce once. Nonces cannot wrap after exhaustion; the last representable nonce remains usable. Accumulated-fee overflow rejects before account creation or balance writes. Verifier/apply regressions check rejection without mutation. Roots commit the leaf count and use big-endian leaves (corrected 2026-09-23). |

The [local contracts and counterexamples](proofs/K2_VDF_Soundness.md) define the
scope of the C99 gates. Local timeout termination does not establish network
progress, and no claim of absolute security, enforced blindness or MEV immunity
is made. This correction does not close any unrelated C++ security-ledger row.
The existing historical status notes below retain their original dates and scope.


**Doc status:** Canonical. Reconciles the rev.7 security audit findings with the in-tree predecessor `OPEN-VULNERABILITIES.md` (now superseded and removed) against current code at rev.8 + rev.9 sharding through B6.basic.

> **STATUS 2026-09-17 — O-1 step 3c landed: the claims that rested on equivocation slashing and the abort deduction are re-derived on the shipped mechanism.** The 58 untiered documents that carried a `STATUS 2026-09-16` banner have been re-derived and the banners are removed; the ledger rows change as follows. **S-006** — the closure is restated honestly: ContribMsg-level detection still closes the observability gap (the second contrib's signature no longer vanishes at the receiver) and now yields a V11-verified on-chain RECORD which is the declared L2 input (D22); the apply-path half of the old closure is void. Stays ✅ Mitigated on the narrower claim; NOT reopened. **S-011** — the ⚠ is retired and replaced by a derivation whose answer is partly negative (`S010S011SybilEconomics.md` §6.7 + T-4-R): with no forfeiture and no abort deduction, a cartel's marginal per-round protocol cost is the opportunity cost of RECOVERABLE capital plus bandwidth, while its per-round revenue is its share of `block_subsidy + fees` — so the recorded "per-round cost > chain subsidy ⇒ economic infeasibility" claim is **FALSE at every parameterization with a positive subsidy**, and under `DOMAIN_INCLUSION` (where `min_stake = 0`) BOTH legs of the old closure are zero. The row stays ✅ Mitigated on the surviving, narrower ground — the S-010 floor prices ACQUIRING `M−1` seats under `STAKE_INCLUSION`, and `Censorship.md` T-2.1 plus `Safety.md` T-1 clause 1 cap what the seats are worth. Named residuals: BFT escalation can seat a ZERO-honest committee at `|pool| == K` (one abort, `bft_escalation_threshold` default 1, `bft_committee_size(3) == 2`; S-086 OPEN, D5a/R-4 authorized-not-landed) and the suspension window is renewable at zero cost. **S-013** — the ⚠ is retired: the OOM closure was always layers 1–2 (per-signer cap 2, pre-filter ≤ K ⇒ `|buffered_block_sigs_| ≤ 2·K`), which are counting arguments that consume no economic term; layer 3's "the second signature costs the signer its stake" is void, so the cap BOUNDS the flood but no longer PRICES it. **S-029** — the ⚠ is retired because the refutation is now settled rather than pending, and it is STRONGER than recorded: the Level-3 block-hash tiebreak is grindable not only by the trailing signer (one Ed25519 sign per trial over a fixed digest) but by an unauthenticated RELAYER at one SHA-256 per trial, because `initial_state` is inside `Block::signing_bytes`, outside `compute_block_digest`, ignored by apply above genesis and read by no validator rule (recorded in the S-102 adjudication entry). S-029's own finding — fork-choice DETERMINISM in BFT mode — is unaffected and stays ✅ Mitigated; the grinding hole is stated as OPEN in `S029ForkChoiceSoundness.md` §5.3/F-1 and its fix is an unwritten accept rule. **S-095** — ✅ CLOSED: `RoundStallValveSoundness.md` C-2 is corrected (the empty-abort-tail case produces an honest same-height same-`gen` contrib pair that passes every V11 clause, so the valve CAN fabricate evidence against the recovering node; validation safety C-1 is unaffected and the L1 harm is zero since D4). **`BFTSafety.md`** — assumption B2 ("equivocation slashing enforced") is DELETED (T-5's proof never consumed it) and Corollary T-5.1 is WITHDRAWN and replaced by T-5.1-R: a B1 violation now yields exactly classical BFT failure with an evidence record and **no recovery**. What is still owed, by name: the consumer of every evidence record (D22, v1.1 DApp scope, NOT DESIGNED), the evidence cap + in-block dedup (step 3b), evidence completeness (S-090) and shard event identity (S-089). DECISION-LOG 2026-09-17 "O-1 step 3c".
>

**Methodology.** Each finding is verified against current source before classification. Findings the rev.7 audit raised that have since been mitigated are listed in §5. New issues visible in rev.9 code (sharding, snapshots) that the audit predates are included as first-class findings. Severity follows the audit's CVSS-style framing.

**What checks this table, and what does not.** `tools/test_security_ledger_coherence.sh`
derives the "Open (untouched)" and "Reopened" rows from the §2 triage-row STATUS cells — the
id set and that row's Total — and checks that nothing listed as mitigated carries an
OPEN/REOPENED status. It reads no other row, and it never reads a per-severity NUMERAL: it
compares id MEMBERSHIP. So the "Partially mitigated" row above — every cell of it, including
its Total — is unguarded, and on 2026-09-18 it was measurably wrong: the Medium cell read
**2** while naming four ids (S-110, S-111, S-114, S-115), because S-114/S-115 were added to
the parenthetical and to the row Total (6 -> 8) without the cell numeral moving. 1+1+2+2 = 6,
not 8. Corrected the same day to **4**, which sums. A guard on a proxy for the thing is the
failure this project has now recorded three times (wave doctrine 2026-09-18 §2); the numeral
check is not written, and until it is, this row is maintained by hand and by reading.

(M-F removed iterated SHA-256 delay-hash and its supporting infrastructure — `delay_T` field, worker thread, `RUNNING_DELAY` phase, `EVP_MD_CTX` per-iteration alloc — in commits `14bf3d6` and `1b9b086`. T-001 through T-004 are operator-facing trade-offs of `sharding_mode = EXTENDED`, not bugs — see §6.5.)

**Open Critical findings (2026-09-23): one open (S-055) and three partially mitigated (S-057 — the empty-pq_auth rule landed, the block-byte cap was reverted; S-063 — delivery gating landed with open residuals; S-030 — the apply-layer framing, see the table) — see the triage rows; as of the 2026-09-14 status note: S-056/S-058/S-059/S-061/S-062 were closed the same day (producer-side admission + eviction; committee-completeness trigger) and S-060 (with it, the reopened leg of S-052) on 2026-09-15 by the owner-authorized create-only REGISTER, S-071 (Zeroth pool drain through a COMPOSABLE_BATCH inner transfer) the same day; the rest of this paragraph is the 2026-08-12 state.** **S-049 (newly discovered CRITICAL — inflation) closed in this session** — the tx apply path computed the debit `cost = amount + fee` in unchecked uint64 at every value-moving site (TRANSFER/PQ_TRANSFER, SHIELD, STAKE, DAPP_CALL); an attacker choosing `amount ≈ 2^64 − fee` wrapped `cost` to ~0, passed the `balance < cost` gate on a near-zero debit, and had the recipient credited the FULL amount (mint-from-nothing, CVE-2010-5139 class); the always-on A1 supply assertion was blind because A1 is a mod-2^64 identity and the injected discrepancy is exactly a multiple of 2^64. Found by the D1 shielded-pool adversarial-audit workflow; closed by a `checked_add_u64` debit (skip-on-overflow, no state change) at all four sites + a fail-closed validator reject + checked `total_fees`/`charge_fee` accumulation; byte-invariant for all legit txs (unchanged goldens + a 2-lens adversarial re-verify with zero surviving findings); regression-locked by `determ test-value-overflow-mint`. Only S-030 remains partially mitigated (D1 effective-closed via S-033; all three D2 pool-fed dimensions — inbound / equivocation / abort — now consensus-closed via the **shipped** v2.7 F2 view-reconciliation + digest binding (commits `850d2c3` / `c16d6c3` / `a727cb2` / `48c4b45`); `partner_subset_hash` is now bound into `compute_block_digest` (commit `8585a50`; deterministic from merge state, so no view-reconciliation needed) and `timestamp` is now bound via deterministic median reconciliation (commit `f99eeb8`; the §5 mechanism — Phase-1 `proposer_time` commit → lower-median at `build_body` → digest binding → validator re-derivation), so S-030-D2 is now fully digest-closed). S-031 is now fully closed (6 architectural layers shipped). **Open High findings: zero — S-044 and S-045 (the abort-cascade / escalation-reachability liveness pair) both closed this session (see below and §3).** The previously-open High items are all closed: S-006 via `on_contrib` equivocation detection; S-010 via operator stake-pricing formula + DOMAIN_INCLUSION availability; S-011 via S-010 stake floor + FA6 equivocation-slashing economic-infeasibility bound. **S-042 (newly discovered HIGH) closed in this session** — light-client trustless reads previously bound `state_root` to a daemon-reported header field (the committee digest excludes `state_root`, and stripped headers can't recompute `block_hash`), so a malicious daemon could swap `state_root` post-signing and have it reported as committee-verified; closed via the `committee_bound_state_root` helper (full-block recompute + committee-signed successor `prev_hash` binding; head fails closed), with every trustless reader routed through it and the consensus digest unchanged. **S-043 (newly discovered CRITICAL-liveness) fixed in this session** — the f99eeb8 S-030-D2 timestamp binding shipped its sender + validator halves but `Node::on_contrib`'s gossip-side recompute kept the 7-arg call (defaulted `proposer_time=0`), so EVERY production contrib failed signature verification at every peer and **no multi-node cluster could mint a single block** (total liveness outage; zero safety impact — the failure direction was reject-everything); fixed by re-binding `msg.proposer_time` in the two receiver recompute sites, validated live on a local 3-node cluster (37 blocks minted + committee-verified post-fix), and regression-locked by the new in-process `determ test-contrib-wire-verify` (the sign → wire → receiver-recompute → verify chain no prior test exercised). **S-037 closed in this session** — added serialize / restore handling for `dapp_registry_` so DApp-active chains survive snapshot bootstrap intact; new regression `tools/test_dapp_snapshot.sh` (12/12 PASS) exercises register → snapshot → restore → `dapp-info` end-to-end. **S-038 closed in this session** — `Node::try_finalize_round` now populates `body.state_root` via a tentative-chain dry-run before broadcast, so the S-033 verification gate at apply time actually fires (pre-fix the field was zero on every gossiped block, short-circuiting the gate). S-033's documented "shipped" status is now genuine end-to-end; the apply-layer mitigation of S-030 D1/D2 is no longer dormant. **S-041 closed in this session** — the three R7 merge-threshold scalars (`merge_threshold_blocks` / `revert_threshold_blocks` / `merge_grace_blocks`) contribute `k:`-namespace state_root leaves but were omitted from `Chain::serialize_state` / `restore_from_snapshot` (S-037-class gap, invisible under genesis defaults); now emitted + restored with genesis-default fallbacks, and `test-snapshot-roundtrip` / `test-snapshot-full-determinism` carry non-default thresholds through the JSON round-trip to guard regression. **S-044 + S-045 (the abort-cascade / escalation-reachability liveness pair) both closed in this session** via three composable changes derived in `docs/proofs/AbortCascadeLiveness.md` (FB67): **(F-a)** the abort-claim quorum is now `max(2, K−1)` via the shared `chain::abort_claim_quorum()` helper (routed through by the producer-formation, gossip-adoption, and validator paths) — a no-op for K≥3 but unsatisfiable at K=2, so no single-claim abort event can form and the K=2 wedge-by-cascade becomes a crash-stop halt-by-single-death (FB67 §4.1); **(θ)** the genesis-default `bft_escalation_threshold` is now **1** (was 5) — reached by the *first* abort event (which always forms), so the escalation counter can never freeze below it (killing the S-045 counter-freeze), while gate 1 (`avail < k_target`) still bars premature escalation whenever MD margin exists and the F-a floor keeps a single Byzantine node from forcing BFT mode (S025 `A_premature` stays closed); **(profile)** the `web` init-default profile is retuned M=3/K=2 → **M=4/K=3**, so the default deployment has K≥3 (no S-044) plus MD margin 1 (the common single-fault case runs a full K-of-K MD round among survivors, never even needing escalation). Validated live (`tools/test_bft_escalation.sh`: 34 MD blocks then BFT escalation on node-kill with θ=1) + in-process (`test-timing-profiles`, `test-required-block-sigs`) + the rewritten `tools/test_s044_gate_surface.sh` (now pins the FIXED shape; SELFTEST 10/10 regression classes). The only remaining permanent wedge is `avail < k_bft` (past escalation headroom) — the *designed* R7/operator-recovery boundary (FA9), not a defect. Candidate fix F-b (wall-clock abort decay) was analyzed and **rejected** on consensus-non-determinism grounds (FB67 §4.2); F-c (formation-failure certificate) proved unnecessary once θ=1 makes the counter reachable. **S-047 (newly discovered HIGH-liveness — found by the new in-process FA4 failover harness `test-fa-liveness-virtual`) closed in this session** — every consensus round message was a ONE-SHOT broadcast (abort claims never re-sent; hash-chained abort events whose single miss permanently splits `aborts_gen` generations that then mutually drop each other's messages; contribs/sigs a crashing committee member delivered to SOME peers only), so one lost/mistimed message wedged the chain PERMANENTLY even at 4/5 honest-alive — three distinct wedge modes reproduced in-process; closed by the S-047 retry tick (`Node::rebroadcast_round_state_locked`: the phase timers re-arm — stale fires phase-gated — and each tick relays the FULL stored, author-signed round state: abort tail in chain order + all stored contribs + all stored sigs; byte-identical re-serialization so every receiver dedup path drops repeats instead of reading S-006 equivocation; zero wire/digest change). **S-048 (newly discovered MEDIUM, node-local liveness) CLOSED this session — owner-authorized head-reorg wired (A4)** — the abort-vs-finalize race can leave one node on a validly-signed minority SAME-HEIGHT block; `Chain::resolve_fork` (S-029) defined the deterministic winner but (pre-fix) was not wired into Node and sync was append-only, so the forked node could never reorg. Closed by a depth-1 head reorg wired into the live accept path (`Chain::revert_head` A4.1 + `Node::maybe_reorg_to_locked` A4.2, design `docs/proofs/BoundedReorgDesign.md`): a node that applied the minority block now reverts→validates→adopts `resolve_fork`'s deterministic winner, fail-closed and byte-neutral for normal operation (the reorg is a strict no-op on the gossip-duplicate stream). Direct evidence: the live 5-node `test-fa-liveness-virtual` cluster is 10/10 with `[node] S-048 REORG … replaced head` firing and zero "cannot reorg" markers; `test-node-reorg-s048` is the deterministic WINNER/REPLAY/LOSER/INVALID repro (commit `e22d857`). The running-node fork is closed, and A4.4 now closes the sync-path rejoiner (a restarted node holding a saved minority tail fetches from `height()-1` and reorgs to `resolve_fork`'s winner over the CHAIN_RESPONSE sync path — `test-fa-liveness-virtual` REJOIN 10/10, commit `dcb49be`); and A4.5 closes the reorg-during-save crash-consistency — a reorg rewrites the tail block file in place, so `save_incremental` now shrinks the manifest to `persisted_count_` BEFORE the rewrite (tracked via `persisted_manifest_height_`), making every crash window reload a consistent chain instead of bricking on a head_hash mismatch (commit `d8265c0`; adversarial-review GO, `test-chain-reorg-save-crash`). **S-048 is now closed end to end.** **Open Medium findings: zero** (S-045 was briefly registered Medium and upgraded to High the same day per AbortCascadeLiveness.md T-4). The previously-open Medium items are closed: S-018 via `json_require<T>` / `json_require_hex` / `json_require_array` helpers (`include/determ/util/json_validate.hpp`) across every attack-relevant wire-format consumer (gossip envelope, Transaction/Block/AbortEvent/EquivocationEvent/GenesisAlloc, ContribMsg/AbortClaimMsg/BlockSigMsg, GenesisConfig, node_key.json loader); new regression `tools/test_s018_json_validation.sh` (10/10 PASS) exercises field-name diagnostics for missing-field / wrong-type / wrong-hex-length / non-array errors. S-016 is now closed (the v2.7 F2 Option 1 inbound-view intersection commitment + admitted-set digest binding shipped — commits `850d2c3` / `c16d6c3` / `a727cb2`; Option 2's 3-block soak retained as a liveness pre-filter). **Open Low/Op findings: zero fully-open** — S-035 Option 3 (path portability) shipped this session via `tools/common.sh` helper + sed conversion across all then-49 tests (now 53; new tests added in-session use the helper from the start); Options 1 (gtest seed) + 2 (deterministic-simulation framework) remain as v1.x quality work but don't block deployment (2026-09-23: Option 2 has shipped except its production dependency injection — see the S-035 row).

**Three findings registered from the cluster-test-suite remediation (2026-06-11); the S-044/S-045 liveness pair CLOSED this session.** **S-044 (✅ Mitigated, was High-liveness)** — K=2 committees wedged permanently under ordinary timing skew: the abort-claim quorum at K=2 was K−1 = 1, so a single phase straggle abort-excluded a member with ONE claim; the resulting `aborts_gen` divergence dropped contribs between peers, cascading further single-claim aborts (`current_aborts_` cleared only on block accept then, `node.cpp:2454`) until the eligible pool fell below K and `check_if_selected` returned forever; BFT escalation could not rescue because k_bft = ⌈2K/3⌉ = 2 = K. Reproduced live on three independent cluster tests (K=2-of-3 twice, K=2-of-4 once) even at 2000 ms test timers. **Closed by fix F-a** (`chain::abort_claim_quorum` = `max(2, K−1)`, applied at the producer-formation / gossip-adoption / validator quorum sites): the floor is a no-op for K≥3 and makes the K=2 quorum unsatisfiable, so no single-claim abort can form — the cascade is eliminated and K=2 becomes a crash-stop 2-of-2 (a genuinely dead member halts the height, no cascade under healthy-node skew; FB67 §4.1). **Additionally the `web` init-default profile was retuned M=3/K=2 → M=4/K=3**, so the default deployment is K≥3 with MD margin 1 and is not exposed at all. **S-045 (✅ Mitigated, was High-liveness — had been upgraded from Medium per `docs/proofs/AbortCascadeLiveness.md` T-4/T-2.1)** — BFT escalation was structurally unreachable at the historical genesis-default `bft_escalation_threshold` = 5 on the nominal pool (N = M) of every shipped profile: rounds generate abort events and `check_if_selected` silently returns once `avail < k_use`, so at M=K a single dead member yielded at most K−1 = 2 events, frozen below θ=5 forever — the common one-dead-node halt. **Closed by lowering the genesis-default θ to 1** (`max(2,K−1)`-floored F-a keeps single-node forced escalation out): θ=1 is reached by the first abort event (which always forms), so the counter can never freeze below the threshold — escalation is reachable whenever it is *viable* (`avail ∈ [k_bft, K)`), and gate 1 still bars it when MD margin exists. Validated live (`tools/test_bft_escalation.sh` at θ=1-default: 34 MD blocks then BFT escalation on node-kill) and pinned by the rewritten `tools/test_s044_gate_surface.sh` (FIX-shape guard). Candidate fix F-b (wall-clock decay) rejected on determinism grounds; F-c (formation-failure certificate) proved unnecessary. **S-046 (FIXED, Low)** — `determ start` silently ignored every argument other than `--config`/`--data-dir`, so a launcher invocation carrying e.g. `--rpc-port`/`--genesis` "started" an unconfigured node (init-default ports, no genesis pin) with no diagnostic — exactly how a broken test recipe shipped unnoticed; now strict-argv (unknown argument → usage error + exit 1).

**Top-of-list priorities** (updated after in-session closures — see §3 bodies for closure details):

Currently genuinely outstanding:

- **S-055 (open Critical), S-057 and S-063 (Criticals, partially mitigated) + S-065, S-069, S-080, S-085, S-117 (open High), S-067 (partial) and the S-054 runtime bound `2K > N(h)` (D5a decided, not landed; the 2026-09-20 attempt was reverted 2026-09-23).** Recorded in DECISION-LOG 2026-08-13..16 and 2026-09-14. Their ordering against the D2 front is an owner decision (CLAUDE.md DECISION CLOCK). The shared root of S-056/S-059/S-061/S-062 (the producer included what the verifier rejects, and nothing evicted it) was node-local and is CLOSED 2026-09-14 (`determ test-producer-admit`, `test-mempool-admit-eviction`).

- **S-030 D2 — v2.7 F2 SHIPPED; now fully digest-closed.** The three pool-fed D2 dimensions (inbound / equivocation / abort) are consensus-closed: the F2 view-reconciliation + admitted-set digest binding landed (commits `850d2c3` / `c16d6c3` / `a727cb2` / `48c4b45`; design in `docs/proofs/F2-SPEC.md`), so committee signatures gather only on view convergence and the reconciled sets are bound into `compute_block_digest`. D1 is effective-closed via S-033 state_root binding. The non-pool field `partner_subset_hash` is now bound too (commit `8585a50`; deterministic from merge state, so it needed no view-reconciliation — see `docs/proofs/S030-D2-Analysis.md` item 9). **`timestamp` is now bound too (commit `f99eeb8`):** via the §5 mechanism — each committee member commits its `proposer_time` in the Phase-1 `ContribMsg`, `build_body` computes the deterministic lower-median of the K committed times, that median is bound into `compute_block_digest`, and the validator re-derives it before signing (it cannot be raw-hashed because honest clocks differ within the ±30s window; the median reconciliation resolves that). With `timestamp` bound, S-030-D2 is now fully digest-closed — the only digest-excluded field left is `cross_shard_receipts`, which is deterministically derived from the bound tx set rather than an independent divergence vector.
- **v2.10 threshold randomness aggregation** — ⏸️ **block-beacon application DE-SCOPED.** Decision: retain the v1 MPDH commit-reveal block beacon. Per `docs/proofs/FROST_DEVIATION_NOTICE.md` §9 (rationale absorbed from the deleted `V210-PhaseD-RandomnessWiring.md` §9), a FROST aggregate is **not** a bias-resistance upgrade over the existing FA3 commit-reveal guarantee (which already neutralises grinding, information-theoretically under SHA-256 preimage resistance), and — unlike threshold-BLS — FROST is **not** unbiasable-by-construction; so it does not justify the DKG/PSS ceremony for the block beacon. Residual selective-abort remains **accepted** under MPDH and handled by re-roll + suspension (not a gate; the round-1 stake deduction was retired 2026-09-16, D13). FROST is **removed from the v1.1 chain consensus path entirely** per `FROST_DEVIATION_NOTICE.md` (2026-06-07) — the FROST C99 code is retained **only as a library** (audit history + possible DApp-layer use), not in the chain path or the v1.1 formal-verification surface. Cross-shard randomness uses **commit-reveal aggregation**; DSSO uses **threshold-OPAQUE** (t-of-n T-OPRF on the shipped P-256 RFC 9497 VOPRF stack — `docs/proofs/v2.25-DSSO-DAPP-SPEC.md`, DECISION-LOG 2026-07-15), not FROST. Authority: Stoyan Denev (`docs/proofs/FROST_DEVIATION_NOTICE.md`).

Closed in-session (retained here for audit trail; see §3 bodies):

- ~~S-001 RPC authentication missing~~ — closed via `rpc_localhost_only=true` default + HMAC-SHA-256 (v2.16).
- ~~S-002 Mempool accepts unverified signatures~~ — closed via mempool sig-verify + paired `binary_codec.cpp::decode_tx_frame` fix.
- ~~S-003 Block timestamp window ±5s~~ — closed via ±30s window in `BlockValidator::check_timestamp`; spec and code now agree.
- ~~S-004 Plaintext private keys~~ — closed via AES-256-GCM passphrase envelope keyfile (v2.17).
- ~~S-006 ContribMsg same-generation equivocation~~ — closed via detection in `on_contrib`; reuses existing `EquivocationEvent` channel (no new wire format).
- ~~S-007 Subsidy/fee overflow~~ — closed via `checked_add_u64` on every credit path.
- ~~S-008 Unbounded mempool~~ — closed via `MEMPOOL_MAX_TXS = 10000` + `MEMPOOL_MAX_PER_SENDER = 100` with fee-priority eviction.
- ~~S-010 Sybil via under-priced MIN_STAKE~~ — closed via operator stake-pricing formula + DOMAIN_INCLUSION availability (see §3 S-010 calculator).
- ~~S-011 Abort claim cartel via M-1 quorum~~ — closed via S-010 stake floor + FA6 equivocation slashing bounding the attack to economically infeasible.
- ~~S-012 Snapshot trust~~ — closed via state_root verification on restore.
- ~~S-013 BlockSigMsg buffer flood OOM~~ — closed via per-signer cap (2 entries) in `try_buffer_block_sig`; total buffer bounded at 2·K through existing pre-filters.
- ~~S-014 No rate limiting on gossip + RPC~~ — closed via shared `net::RateLimiter` helper used by both `RpcServer` and `GossipNet` (per-peer-IP token bucket, HELLO exempt).
- ~~S-017 Producer/chain UNSTAKE divergence~~ — closed via Option 2: validator + producer both gain the `unlock_height` check; apply-time refund retained as belt-and-suspenders.
- ~~S-018 JSON parsing without schema validation~~ — closed via `json_require<T>` / `json_require_hex` / `json_require_array` helpers in `include/determ/util/json_validate.hpp` applied to every attack-relevant wire-format consumer (gossip envelope, Transaction/Block/AbortEvent/EquivocationEvent/GenesisAlloc, ContribMsg/AbortClaimMsg/BlockSigMsg, GenesisConfig, node_key.json); new regression `tools/test_s018_json_validation.sh` (10/10 PASS).
- ~~S-020 Rejection sampling O(K²) at K/N → 1~~ — closed via hybrid selector (rejection sampling at 2K ≤ N, partial Fisher-Yates shuffle at 2K > N — bounded O(N) regardless of ratio).
- ~~S-021 Chain file integrity not cryptographically verified~~ — closed via wrapping chain.json with `head_hash` + load-time recompute + mismatch reject (O(1) tampering detection before replay).
- ~~S-022 Permissive 16 MB message cap~~ — closed via per-message-type body-size limit applied after deserialize in `Peer::read_body`; 1 MB for consensus chatter, 4 MB for blocks/headers/bundles, 16 MB only for SNAPSHOT/CHAIN responses. Hardened again in round 12 (WIRE-2/WIRE-3) once measurement showed the post-deserialize position let a 16 MB frame expand ~52× in heap *before* the cap was consulted; WIRE-2 retired with the last JSON payload in D2 inc7c (2026-09-16 — every payload is a fixed frame whose decoder bounds each count before allocating); two residuals stay owner-gated (see the S-022 row).
- ~~S-026 No connection timeout / keepalive~~ — closed via `SO_KEEPALIVE` on every peer socket in `Peer::Peer`; dead connections reaped via OS-level keepalive probes through the existing on_close path.
- 🟢 S-016 Inbound-receipts pool non-determinism — CLOSED, including its S-030-D2 dimension. Option 1 (v2.7 F2 Phase-1 view intersection) IMPLEMENTED + ENFORCED at block validation (commits 850d2c3 + c16d6c3): the admitted inbound set is the deterministic committee-wide intersection of the members' committed views. The full inbound chain is now K-of-K-attested end-to-end: the per-creator view roots are bound via `creator_ed_sigs ∈ compute_block_digest`, AND the admitted set itself is bound — `compute_block_digest` appends a root over the sorted `hash_cross_shard_receipt` keys of `inbound_receipts` (commit a727cb2), closing the subset-only removal gap (a relayer can no longer STRIP an admitted receipt post-signing). Option 2's 3-block soak retained as a liveness pre-filter. The broader S-030-D2 residual (the equivocation/abort view dimensions + optional contrib-time V21-V26 passes) is a separate item tracked under S-030.
- ~~S-024 Deregistration timing predictability~~ — formally accepted per auditor's reclassification; 1-10 block grind window deemed acceptable in v1.x; v2.X enhancement noted.
- ~~S-027 Info leakage in logs~~ — closed via audit-pass (no secret material in node/RPC logs; only chain-public state) + new `Config::log_quiet` flag for operator-side log-volume control.
- ~~S-028 Hex parsing only accepts lowercase~~ — closed via case-insensitive `is_anon_address` + `normalize_anon_address` helper + apply at RPC read boundaries (`rpc_balance`, `rpc_send`); `rpc_submit_tx` rejects non-canonical with clear diagnostic (sig is over signing_bytes so server can't mutate).
- ~~S-029 BFT-mode multi-proposer fork-choice undefined~~ — closed via `Chain::resolve_fork`: heaviest sig set / fewer aborts / smallest hash, deterministic across peers.
- ~~S-031 Global mutex serialization~~ — closed via 6 architectural layers (shared_mutex + A9 Phase 1-2D + async chain.save + gossip-out-of-lock).
- ~~S-032 O(N) registry rebuild~~ — closed via incremental registry cache.
- ~~S-033 No state commitment~~ — closed via Merkle root in `Block.state_root` + signing_bytes binding. (S-038 closure makes the gate actually fire on production blocks.)
- ~~S-037 dapp_registry snapshot serialize/restore gap~~ — closed via `dapp_registry` field added to `Chain::serialize_state` + `restore_from_snapshot` (with `if (snap.contains(...))` guard for pre-v2.18 backward compat); new regression `tools/test_dapp_snapshot.sh` (12/12 PASS) exercises register → snapshot → restore → `dapp-info` verification end-to-end.
- ~~S-042 Light-client trustless reads bound state_root to a daemon field~~ — closed via `light/trustless_read.cpp::committee_bound_state_root` (full-block recompute via the `"block"` RPC + committee-signed successor `prev_hash` binding; head fails closed); all trustless readers routed through it; consensus digest / signing_bytes unchanged. New finding (HIGH), fixed same session. Liveness follow-up: because the daemon's `state_proof` always serves the current head (whose root has no committee-signed successor yet), the readers fail closed on every live current-state read; an opt-in `--wait <seconds>` flag (default off; `committee_bound_state_root` poll loop) on EVERY head-anchored composite — the reader handlers plus the `verify-and-submit` writer flow and the `verify-unstake-eligibility` verdict, both of whose embedded reads anchor at the head too (`tools/test_light_wait_flag.sh` pins the contract) — restores usability by blocking for the next block then binding the ALREADY-HELD proof — sound (never re-fetches, so no state race), default-off path byte-identical. Regression-locked by `tools/test_light_state_root_binding_guard.sh` (static guard + `SELFTEST=1` liveness: asserts no `!= vc.head_state_root` field-trust anchor survives, every reader keeps routing through `committee_bound_state_root`, and the helper's full-block fetch + successor binding check stay intact). See §3 S-042.
- ~~F-7 Light-client fail-closed on F2 / cross-shard blocks (capability gap)~~ — closed by completing `light/verify.cpp::light_compute_block_digest` to a FULL byte-for-byte mirror of `producer.cpp::compute_block_digest` (binds the three F2 view roots — inbound-receipt / equivocation / abort — on the SAME data-driven gates as the node) plus a full-block fallback in `light/trustless_read.cpp::verify_chain_walk` that re-fetches the unstripped body for any header whose stripped-header digest fails to verify. Light-client-local **CAPABILITY GAP**, not a vulnerability: pre-fix the verifier could only *error* / report UNVERIFIABLE (`verify_chain_to_head` threw before any verdict) — **never a false INCLUDED** — so it was false-negative-safe (not deployment-blocking), but it made `verify-receipt-inclusion` and every trustless read structurally unusable on any shard chain that had applied a cross-shard transfer (the receipt is applied IN an F2 block). Fix is entirely light-client-side; `producer.cpp` UNCHANGED. The static parity guard `tools/test_block_digest_xbinary_parity.sh` was tightened from "light == producer MINUS the 3 F2 roots" to "light == producer EXACTLY" (full byte-parity — strictly STRONGER). See §3 F-7 + `proofs/LightClientThreatModel.md §7 F-7`.
- ~~S-038 state_root gate dormant on production blocks~~ — closed by populating `body.state_root` in `Node::try_finalize_round` (tentative-chain dry-run, same pattern as the digest dry-run above it) before `apply_block_locked` + `gossip_.broadcast`. The S-033 verification gate at `chain.cpp::apply_transactions` (which already correctly compared `b.state_root` to `compute_state_root()` when non-zero) now fires on every produced block, so peer nodes reject any divergent body. K-of-K BlockSig signatures unaffected (`compute_block_digest` already excludes `state_root` per §4.3); `block_hash` (compute_hash via signing_bytes) now binds the field. Backward-compat: pre-fix chains with `state_root = 0` in stored blocks remain valid (the gate skips zero per the S-033 backward-compat shim). The S-038 fix is the "actually shipped" half of the S-033 mitigation — pre-fix, the data layer worked but the gate was bypassed.

- ~~S-050 Idle-follower re-sync gap (the stall valve's structural blind spot)~~ — closed via a `b.index > chain_.height()` trigger in `apply_block_locked` that arms the existing tolerance-0 catch-up, guarded to fire once per stall episode so it cannot become a `STATUS_REQUEST` amplifier. Owner-authorized in-session 2026-08-12. Regression-locked by `determ test-straggler-resync`.
- ~~S-052 Equivocation evidence not height-bound (forged slash)~~ — reopened 2026-08-14 via S-060 (the verifier resolves the accused key through the registry, `validator.cpp:471` / `committee_pool.cpp:43-55`, and REGISTER let any key replace that entry) and **re-closed 2026-09-15 by V-REG-1** (REGISTER is create-only, so no key can be rebound to a domain it does not hold). Closed by making both digest families two-level and openable (`SHA256("DTM-BLKDIG-v3" ‖ index ‖ gen ‖ body_root)` / `SHA256("DTM-CONTRIB-v3" ‖ block_index ‖ gen ‖ body_root)`), carrying `kind` + per-side `{index, body_root}` openings instead of opaque digests, and asserting `index_a == index_b == block_index` with signatures verified against the DERIVED digests. Rank-1 hole authorized 2026-07-31, landed 2026-08-12. Regression-locked by the 8-arm EQV block of `determ test-abort-cert-validation`.
- ~~S-053 Empty / under-K beacon header accepted (vacuous K-of-K)~~ — closed by extracting `verify_committee_sigs` as the ONE committee-signature core (non-empty + size match + membership + verify + `signed_count >= required_k`) and routing BOTH `on_beacon_header` and `verify_shard_tip_committee_sig_root` through it. Rank-1 hole authorized 2026-07-31, landed 2026-08-12. Regression-locked by `determ test-beacon-header-committee`. **Does not** authenticate `cumulative_rand` — see the row.
- S-054 Genesis accepted `2K <= M` (disjoint quorums — the unattributable fork) — **◐ PARTIAL (2026-08-14, log `ddfe877`)**: no accept rule reads `m_creators` (`validator.hpp:423` write-only, `producer.cpp:1254` `(void)m_pool_size`); the committee is drawn from the eligible pool `N(h)` (`validator.cpp:100-157`, `registry.cpp:88-90`), which REGISTER leaves uncapped, so the invariant that prevents two disjoint same-height committees is `2K > N(h)` and it is OPEN. Landed part: enforcing QUORUM INTERSECTION in `GenesisConfig::validate()`: the band is now `M/2 < K <= M`, so a genesis whose two K-subsets can be disjoint is rejected at load on BOTH the JSON view and the DGC1 binary container. `K == M` (the default) stays legal — unanimity, zero liveness margin. Regression-locked by leg GB-8 of `determ test-genesis-binary-codec`.

**Production-readiness bar (revised 2026-09-15; rows S-078..S-103 carried from the log 2026-09-16 — see the status note; every open row now carries an owner disposition):** one Critical finding is OPEN (S-055) and three are partially mitigated (S-057, S-063, S-030); S-054, S-067 and S-073 are partial, and thirteen High items are open (2026-09-23 state, after the reverts recorded in DECISION-LOG 2026-09-23): S-064, S-065, S-069, S-080, S-085, S-117 and the seven rows carried on 2026-09-16 that are still open (S-081, S-086, S-088, S-089, S-093, S-094, S-096); the remainder of this paragraph describes the 2026-08-12 state and is retained for history. The v2.7 F2 view-reconciliation closure (the last consensus-layer architectural item) is now **shipped** — it binds the reconciled inbound / equivocation / abort views into the block digest and rejects divergence at signature-gathering time (commits `850d2c3` / `c16d6c3` / `a727cb2` / `48c4b45`), so the three pool-fed S-030 D2 dimensions are consensus-closed. The two non-pool digest fields are now bound as well — `partner_subset_hash` in commit `8585a50` and `timestamp` via median reconciliation in commit `f99eeb8` — so S-030-D2 is now fully digest-closed; what remains between current state and "permissionless-deployment-ready" is operational polish or new-feature work.

Struck: "per-round cost > chain subsidy". With the equivocation forfeiture removed (D4) and the Phase-1 abort deduction retired (D13), the cartel's marginal per-round protocol cost is the opportunity cost of RECOVERABLE locked capital plus bandwidth — abort claims are unfee'd gossip — while its per-round revenue is its share of `block_subsidy + fees`, so the inequality is reversed. Named residuals: BFT escalation can seat a ZERO-honest committee when the eligible pool equals K (S-086 OPEN; D5a/R-4 authorized, not landed), the S-032 suspension window is renewable at zero cost so the honest member's SEAT is not safe though its stake now is, and under `DOMAIN_INCLUSION` both legs are zero and this row does not apply. Residual: the cap BOUNDS the flood (2·K entries, bounded verify cost) but no longer PRICES it — a Byzantine committee member pushes exactly 2 entries per height forever for free. Layer 3 survives as evidence PRESERVATION, which D4 makes more load-bearing, not less (the pair is the L2 input). At `2K <= M` a committee contains two **disjoint** K-subsets; each can sign a different block at the same height, each independently reaches the K-signature finalization threshold, and **both finalize with no member ever signing twice**. | S-068 | ✅ Mitigated | **✅ Mitigated 2026-09-15 (V-REG-1 companion, same owner authorization).** The verifier's REGISTER case rejects a payload key of small order: `determ_ed25519_point_has_small_order` (`src/crypto/ed25519/ed25519.c`) decodes the point, doubles it three times and tests `[8]P == O` on the extended coordinates (one decode, no ladder, no inversion); the check runs AFTER the signature so an unauthenticated REGISTER pays nothing beyond the verification it already paid (the S-065 lock-time surface), and the ingress mirror (`verify_tx_signature_locked`) applies the same test after its own verification. Under the neutral element a single `(R = O, S = 0)` verifies every message; under the other seven torsion points `[k]A` takes at most 8 values, so the same pair verifies one message in at most eight — either way the proof of possession is vacuous. This decoder also accepts the two sign-bit variants of the `x = 0` encodings (`01 00..80`, `ec ff..ff` — RFC 8032 §5.1.3 step 4 is not enforced; recorded in `ed25519.c`), so ten encodings decode to torsion points; all ten are rejected. The gate first DEMONSTRATES the forgery (for every encoding a REGISTER whose `(R = O, S = 0)` signature verifies is found within a few domain-name trials), re-derives each encoding's order with the library, keeps a normal-key REGISTER accepted, rejects every forged REGISTER with the S-068 reason, rejects an undecodable key (`y = 2`), and pins the ingress mirror (dropped at gossip, rejected at RPC, normal-key control admitted). Residual (genesis-tool hygiene, not consensus): genesis creators' `ed_pub` are not checked — a creator that pins a small-order key at genesis forfeits only its own identity. The same primitive on the ANONYMOUS-account path (the address is the key, `parse_anon_pubkey`) is a separate finding: S-071 (Zeroth pool drain through a COMPOSABLE_BATCH inner transfer) and S-072 (small-order anonymous addresses are anyone-can-spend). Gate: `determ test-register-small-order-key` (M1 delete the verifier rule / M2 helper returns 0 / M3 delete the mirror rule — all RED). **Medium (vacuous possession proof; recorded 2026-08-14 `1c0a61d`, carried 2026-09-15 as the V-REG-1 companion).** `determ_ed25519_verify` rejects a non-canonical `y` and `S >= L` but performs no small-order (torsion) check, so a REGISTER whose payload key is one of the eight small-order points admits a `(R, S)` pair that verifies EVERY message — the REGISTER's proof-of-possession is vacuous and, under create-only, permanent: anyone can then sign as that domain. Only a registrant can inflict it on its own domain (a fresh domain, its own key), so the exposure is a permanently forgeable identity rather than a takeover. Fix: reject a small-order payload key in the verifier's REGISTER arm (`[8]P == identity`), companion increment to V-REG-1. | `src/node/validator.cpp` (REGISTER arm), `src/crypto/ed25519/ed25519.c` | done |

The validator set is therefore closed at genesis under the default inclusion model, while README §"registers" and the REGISTER/STAKE tooling present joining as supported. With V-REG-1 and terminal DEREGISTER the set can only shrink. Owner decision (DECISION CLOCK R-12): closed-by-design (document it; retire the misleading tooling) or accept STAKE from a registered-but-unstaked domain (accept-rule change, genesis-frozen). **2026-09-23:** D6 landed 2026-09-20 (063ad603) and was REVERTED 2026-09-23 as unsound: with the per-transaction 2K cap it let two STAKEs in one block reach N = 2K (a permanent halt with the D5a assertion), it admitted any pre-`inactive_from` registrant including suspended ones, eligibility was not deferred to the next epoch, and the cap read the STAKE amount big-endian (review CXA-1, CXA-8, CXA-10). D6 stays DECIDED, not landed (DECISION-LOG 2026-09-23). | `src/node/validator.cpp::check_transaction` (sender registry rule), `include/determ/chain/eligibility_floor.hpp` | owner-gated |
| S-070 | ✅ Mitigated | **✅ Mitigated 2026-09-16 (D19a backlog item 2, with S-079 — the same defect with `fee` in place of `amount + fee`): the S-079 ingress affordability rule rejects `REGISTER(name, 0, fee = UINT64_MAX)` from a zero-balance domain on both channels (`mempool: unaffordable (S-079)`; a fresh domain pays its fee from whatever was sent to that name — by balance, not registration) and the build-time predicate evicts an unaffordable resident REGISTER; a fee the name can pay is admitted as before. Gate `determ test-mempool-admit-affordability` (S-070 arms: the fee-MAX REGISTER dropped at gossip ingress and rejected at RPC, the fee-0 one admitted as the control); mutant M1 RED.** History: **Medium (mempool-layer name squatting; pre-existing, found by the V-REG-1 review 2026-09-15).** The verifier has no `balance >= fee` rule for REGISTER (`validator.cpp` REGISTER arm), the honest producer skips an unaffordable one WITHOUT evicting it (`producer.cpp` `if (sb < tx.fee) continue;` precedes nothing that evicts), and apply skips it without advancing the nonce — so a zero-balance `REGISTER(name, 0, fee = UINT64_MAX)` passes ingress and the verifier, is never included and never charged, cannot be outbid (replace-by-fee ties favour the incumbent and nothing exceeds it), and squats `(name, 0)` in every mempool it reaches: whoever sees a pending REGISTER for a name can deny that name at zero cost, and since V-REG-1 a successful squat of a name the victim later needs is permanent for as long as it stays resident. Fix (node-local): mempool admission requires `fee <= balance(from)` for REGISTER, or the producer-side predicate treats an unaffordable REGISTER as rejected (evicting it). | `src/node/node.cpp::mempool_admit_check`, `::tx_admit_locked` | done |
| S-071 | ✅ Mitigated | **✅ Mitigated 2026-09-15 (same day it was found).** The verifier's COMPOSABLE_BATCH arm rejects an inner tx with `from == ZEROTH_ADDRESS` (`validator.cpp`, before the inner signature) and the apply loop mirrors it (`chain.cpp`, belt-and-suspenders alongside its other inner checks). The false premise "no key can sign over the all-zero pubkey" is retracted everywhere it was stated (`params.hpp`, `validator.cpp`, `chain.cpp`, `main.cpp`, WHITEPAPER §8.5, proofs NefPoolDrain / EconomicSoundness / SubsidyDistribution / DAppRegistryLifecycle, UNIT-TESTS, CLI-REFERENCE, docs/README): the guard, not the key, is what makes the pool a pseudo-account. The ingress mirror gained the outer E1 check (node-local; a forged-signature pool tx is no longer mempool-resident; a pool-draining batch still passes the mirror on the outer signature and is evicted at the first build). What this closes is the FORGED-SIGNATURE route; the economic route — the shipped NEF hands `pool / 2` to every fee-0 fresh REGISTER — stays open as S-073. Residual (pre-existing, Low, deterministic): the verifier does not pre-check the apply loop's no-cross-shard-inner rule (nor does apply mirror the verifier's inner payload ≤ 32 rule), so such a batch is accepted, wholly rolled back at apply, and pays its outer fee. Gate: `determ test-zeroth-pool-inner-batch` (demonstrates the forged inner signature first; M1 delete the verifier check / M2 delete the apply check — both RED). **Critical (theft of protocol funds — remote, cost one outer fee; pre-existing, found by the S-068 review 2026-09-15).** The Zeroth pool (E1, the NEF subsidy seed `zeroth_pool_initial`) is an ordinary `accounts_` entry at the all-zero anon address, and an anon address IS its Ed25519 key (`parse_anon_pubkey`). The all-zero key is a small-order point (order 4), so a signature under it is forgeable: `(R = O, S = 0)` verifies one message in four. The E1 guard `tx.from == ZEROTH_ADDRESS` (`validator.cpp`) saw only the OUTER tx; a COMPOSABLE_BATCH inner TRANSFER from the pool passed the inner signature check with a forged signature and `apply` debited `accounts_[inner.from]` — anyone could sweep the pool for the price of an outer fee. | `src/node/validator.cpp` (COMPOSABLE_BATCH arm), `src/chain/chain.cpp` (batch apply) | done |

| S-074 | ✅ Mitigated | **✅ Mitigated 2026-09-15 (same day it was found).** An AbortEvent's identity is CANONICAL: `timestamp` = the parent block's timestamp (chain time), `event_hash` = `chain::canonical_abort_event_hash(event, tail predecessor, committee_seed, height)` = `SHA256("DTM-ABORT-ID-v1" ‖ 0 ‖ committee_seed ‖ block_index ‖ round ‖ aborting_node)` for the first event at the height and `SHA256("DTM-ABORT-ID-v1" ‖ 1 ‖ prev.event_hash ‖ round ‖ aborting_node)` thereafter, seeded by the §5.2 committee seed (`epoch_committee_seed(epoch_rand, shard_id)`) that every verifier — validator, beacon, light auditor — already derives, so no parent block is needed anywhere — the timestamp is deliberately NOT a hash input (the parent committee chooses the block timestamp inside the ±30 s window as the lower median of its proposer times, which would otherwise be a free ~60-way re-draw of the post-abort committee; review finding). Recomputed and enforced by the validator (`check_abort_certs`, before the fold), by the assembler (`on_abort_claim`) and by the gossip adoption path (`on_abort_event`: checked BEFORE the claim signatures so a re-sent variant costs one hash, not `max(2, K−1)` verifications under the lock; adopts only the canonical NEXT event of the local tail; and requires the accused to be in the CURRENT committee, so a replay of an excluded member's public claims — canonically chained, previously adoptable and yielding a tail no block can carry — is dropped, which also closes the empty-committee zero-claim adoption window). Both abort handlers now return on an empty chain (fail-closed; unreachable in production). Side effects on liveness: every survivor of an aborted round assembles the byte-identical event, so the C1 same-abort-different-timestamp tail fork of `AbortCascadeLiveness.md` no longer exists and `total_aborts` grows by one per aborted round for honest assemblers (unchanged at the shipped default `bft_escalation_threshold = 1`); on the concurrent-quorum wedge the foreign event is dropped rather than appended, so the S-050 valve trips at its 5 s soft window. Stated precondition of the "re-arrives in chain order" recovery: the S-047 relay runs only on committee members with an armed round timer, so a tail-holding node that is not re-selected does not relay it. What a participant can still choose: the victim (which quorum-ready bucket is assembled) and the round (1 or 2) — two draws, visible in the block. Gates: `determ test-abort-cert-validation` (four S-074 arms: chosen hash, chosen timestamp, chained control, chained-as-first) and `determ test-abort-event-canonical` (M=5/K=3 so the post-abort draw is 3 of 4: the formula is pinned independently of the helper, a chosen hash is shown to seat a different committee and is dropped, timestamp variant dropped, canonical first + chained adopted, replay against the excluded member dropped, and a NON-member node assembles exactly the canonical event under a wall clock ≠ chain time). Mutants M1 validator hash / M2 validator timestamp / M3 adoption canonical check / M4 assembler wall clock / M5 adoption accused-membership / M6 helper formula (timestamp added) — all RED, reverted. **Closed the same day (second increment):** the beacon's shard-tip verification (`verify_shard_tip_committee_sig_root`, which both `on_shard_tip` and the beacon's `check_shardtip_witnesses` accept rule route through) and the light auditor's witness path (`light/main.cpp`, the byte-for-byte mirror) recompute every witness abort event's identity from the same committee seed and the tip height before folding it, so a K-colluding source committee cannot present a chosen hash that seats a committee of its choosing at the beacon while its own chain rejects the block — the two derivations agree again. Gate: `determ test-shardtip-witness-verify` (a canonical-abort witness signed by the committee it derives is ACCEPTED; the same witness with a chosen hash, signed by the committee THAT hash derives, is REJECTED); mutant (beacon skips the check) RED, reverted. The light mirror is covered by the mirror discipline of that gate (its daemon-side twin), as every other light re-verification is. **High (liveness / targeting; pre-existing, found by the per-block-committee design analysis 2026-09-15).** The hash folded into the post-abort committee re-selection (`rand = SHA256(rand ‖ event_hash)`, `validator.cpp` `check_creator_selection` / `check_abort_certs`, `node.cpp` `check_if_selected`, the beacon and the auditor) was whatever the assembling node put in the event: nothing recomputed it (`compute_abort_hash` / `chain_abort_hash` had one production caller, the assembler), `timestamp` was the assembler's wall clock and was checked by nothing, and `on_abort_claim` had no receiver-membership gate while `current_creator_domains_` is populated on every in-sync node — so whichever peer first held the `K−1` public claims assembled the event and chose the re-round committee among all `C(N−1, K)` sets, at zero seats; a Phase-2 withholder (unslashed, unsuspended) could pre-grind its own replacement; and a re-assembly of the same claims under a different hash split the abort tail across adopters (the validator rejects any block built on the variant tail), wedging every adopter until the S-050 valve. `AbortCertificateSoundness.md` §1.1 called the value "the chain's deterministic fingerprint of the abort" — it was not. | `src/chain/block.cpp` (`canonical_abort_event_hash`), `src/node/validator.cpp` (`check_abort_certs`), `src/node/node.cpp` (`on_abort_claim`, `on_abort_event`) | done |
| S-075 | ⚠ OPEN | **Medium (node-local availability — a snapshot-bootstrapped node cannot follow the chain; pre-existing, reproduced 2026-09-15).** `Chain::height()` is `blocks_.size()` (`chain.hpp`) and `Chain::at(i)` indexes `blocks_` positionally (`chain.cpp`), but `decode_state` restores only the snapshot's header tail into `blocks_` (`chain.cpp`, DSN1 `headers`). Reproduced with a 7-block chain encoded with `header_count = 2` and decoded: `height() == 2` while `head().index == 6` (`at(height()−1)` is the head, so the per-block seed happens to read the right block; the epoch seed's `epoch_start > height()` branch silently reads the wrong anchor). Consequence for the node: `start_contrib_phase` uses `block_index = chain_.height()` and `apply_block_locked` routes `b.index > height()` to the straggler path, so a node bootstrapped from a snapshot with a partial header tail never validates the next block — it is stuck on `STATUS_REQUEST`s until it is resynced from genesis. Node-local, no accept rule, no fork. Fix shape: an absolute base index on `Chain` (so `height()`/`at()` are absolute) or requiring the snapshot's header tail to reach back to genesis. Reproduction: a standalone probe linking the daemon's objects (session artifact); a permanent gate lands with the fix. **2026-09-23:** A fix landed 2026-09-20 (34271027) and was REVERTED 2026-09-23 as unsound: a snapshot-bootstrapped node wrote blocks at positional indices and failed to restart ("block index mismatch"), and on SINGLE/CURRENT chains the epoch-rand fallback derived a different committee than a full node (review CXB-2) (DECISION-LOG 2026-09-23). The restart failure is not specific to that fix: at HEAD too, `save_incremental` writes `blocks_[i]` to `i.blk`, and S-084's load check (`b.index == i`) rejects the first file of a node bootstrapped from a snapshot whose header tail does not start at genesis ("block index mismatch … expected 0 got 5", reproduced 2026-09-23 with a 2-header snapshot of a 7-block chain), so such a node cannot restart. Same fix shape as above. | `src/chain/chain.cpp` (`height`, `at`, `decode_state`), `src/node/node.cpp` (snapshot restore) | open |
| S-076 | ⚠ OPEN | **Low (design bound, stated for the record; owner decision — DECISION CLOCK R-15).** At any height, in any committee-selection cadence, no abort can form against a silent committee member once **two** members are silent: claims are admitted only from committee members and only against committee members, the quorum is `max(2, K−1)`, so a bucket against a silent member holds at most `K−2` claims; escalation counts aborts and the S-050 valve re-derives the identical committee (the height, hence the seed, is frozen). The round is re-run by the same committee until a silent member returns — a permanent, un-attributed, un-slashed halt for two flooded nodes or two colluders, at every shipped K. `PROTOCOL.md` §12.1 states the positive half ("one straggler is tolerated"); this row states the bound. Per-block committee selection does not change it (design analysis 2026-09-15). The levers that would: a lower claim quorum or claims from non-committee pool members when `N(h) > K` (accept-rule changes with the S-044 cascade/attribution trade-off), or a stall re-selection (the refuted time-bucket / round-marker family — do not revive). | `src/node/node.cpp` (`on_abort_claim`, `maybe_stall_reset_locked`), `include/determ/chain/params.hpp` (`abort_claim_quorum`) | open |
| S-077 | ⚠ OPEN | **Low (randomness bias residual; pre-existing, found by the design analysis 2026-09-15; owner decision — DECISION CLOCK R-16).** The committee member that receives the other `K−1` Phase-2 reveals first knows `cumulative_rand[H]` — hence the E3 jackpot outcome and, under per-block selection, the next committee — before revealing, and can reject that sample by withholding its own reveal. A Phase-2 abort is neither recorded nor suspended (`chain.cpp`, only round-1 aborts are recorded; no abort deducts stake since D13, 2026-09-16), so the veto costs only the seat at that height; the re-round is a fresh draw the withholder cannot choose. Rejection sampling, not choice: it lifts the withholder's own next-committee inclusion probability from `K/N` to at most `K/N + (1−K/N)·K/N` per veto and cannot seat a chosen set (S-074 removed the assembler's choice). The M-F trade-off text below ("backed by … slashing machinery") is not true of Phase-2 withholding and is corrected there. | S-078 | ✅ Mitigated | **✅ Mitigated 2026-09-16 (D19a backlog item 1) — `Chain::load` now seeds EVERY genesis-pinned parameter before the replay.** **High (restart — any non-default genesis was unrestartable; recorded by the 2026-08-13 sweep under two lenses, carried 2026-09-16).** Before the fix `Chain::load` constructed the replay chain and seeded only six fields (`block_subsidy_`, `shard_count_`, `shard_salt_`, `my_shard_id_`, `epoch_blocks_`, `k_block_sigs_`) before `apply_transactions` over every stored block; `min_stake_`, `suspension_slash_`, `unstake_delay_`, the merge thresholds, `crypto_profile_`, `subsidy_mode_`, `subsidy_pool_initial_` and `lottery_jackpot_multiplier_` kept their in-class defaults and are all `k:` state-root leaves, so the first restart of a chain whose genesis differed from the defaults threw S-033; the `tactical` / `cluster` profiles mandate FIPS and were therefore unrestartable independent of economics. Closed: `Chain::Params` (`include/determ/chain/chain.hpp`) carries all sixteen replay-relevant fields; `Chain::load(path, const Params&)` calls `Chain::set_params` on the replay chain BEFORE the first stored block re-applies; `Node::Node` (`src/node/node.cpp`) builds one `chain_params` from the parsed genesis, hands it to `load`, and seeds the genesis-bootstrap chain from the same struct, so the replay chain and the live chain are seeded identically; the post-load setters are gone (they also reset a governance-activated MIN_STAKE / SUSPENSION_SLASH / UNSTAKE_DELAY to the genesis value on restart). No L1 fix is proposed: a predicate separating an honest re-sign from a splitter is refuted (`EquivocationSlashing.md` §2 Case (c)) and re-using the pre-reset `dh_secret` would break the S-009 commit-reveal hiding FA3 rests on. Original defect: C-2 concluded a valve-induced re-sign cannot fabricate equivocation evidence because "the fresh contrib carries `aborts_gen = 0` while peers hold `≠ 0`" — which silently requires a non-empty abort tail; with an empty tail the soft-restart deferral never fires (`src/node/node.cpp:1615-1651`) and the valve re-enters `start_contrib_phase` at the same height with a fresh `dh_input`, producing an honest same-height same-`gen` pair that passes every V11 clause. Every other throw in `apply_transactions` (S-007 credit overflow, S-049 fee accumulation, the A1 assertion) needs a committee-produced body and is covered by the same guard. (b) Lost at HEAD: the head block from memory (the on-disk chain shrank at the NEXT `save_incremental` through the A4.5 shrink-first manifest, so a restart recovered it only inside that window), the retained A4 snapshot (consumed — no further reorg until a new head), the head's transactions from the node's mempool; recovery was by peer re-sync (S-050) and the attempt repeats at will — for a committee member a forced Phase-1 abort each round.
