| S-108 | ✅ Mitigated | **✅ Mitigated 2026-09-17 — the DSSO login was SINGLE-FACTOR and its online-guessing bound was undefined; both are closed in `dapps/dsso/authn.*`, gate `determ-dsso selftest-authn` (114 assertions), mutants M1..M7 each RED.** **High (DSSO identity role; off-chain, no consensus surface).** (a) `v2.25-DSSO-DAPP-SPEC.md` §3-§5 derives everything the user needs from the password: the threshold OPRF output unseals `envelope = AEAD_{HKDF(y)}(cred_sk)`, so the credential key is a stored secret RECOVERED FROM KNOWLEDGE, not an independently held object, and `t`-of-`n` servers are ONE factor evaluated in a distributed way rather than `n` factors. CIR (EU) 2015/1502 Annex §2.2.1 requires two factors from DIFFERENT CATEGORIES at level substantial and §2.3.1 requires a dynamic authentication, so the shipped login could claim at most level low. Closed by a device-resident P-256 possession factor generated from device-local entropy, never transmitted, not derivable from the password or from anything the servers store (they hold only the public point plus enrolment metadata), which must answer a fresh challenge binding the login session nonce, the server-set digest, a timestamp, the device and the knowledge factor's own response at EVERY login — the possession proof is the shipped RFC 9497 VOPRF DLEQ used as a Chaum-Pedersen signature of knowledge, so ZERO new primitive and zero new hardness assumption. Enrolment of a later device needs a two-factor session PLUS the current device; recovery may only REDUCE assurance on one factor and needs a fresh subject-matched PID presentation to restore it, with the downgrade visible to the assertion layer; revocation bumps an `auth_epoch` every session carries, so live sessions stop verifying. (b) §6 left the aggregate attempt cap to the deployment, which leaves the online bound undefined and is walked around by rotating which `t` servers serve each guess. Closed by a per-account grow-only counter (one slot per server, per-slot-max merge — a state-based CRDT, no consensus rule, no new primitive) capped on the SUM, giving `A <= floor(cap / (t − b))` for every subset rotation with `b < t` non-counting servers; a stale view refuses to serve (fail-closed), counters do not leak across accounts, and a successful two-factor login clears the budget. **Scope of the closure, stated exactly:** what ships is the MODULE and its gate — `determ-dsso` has no network listener and no persistent store, the containers are fixed-capacity reference bounds, the EUDI PID verifier behind the narrow `dsso_pid_verify_fn` seam is a separate track (absent verifier ⇒ `DSSO_E_UNAVAILABLE`, never allow-on-outage), and no certification, notification or phishing-resistance claim is made. The §0.0 OPAQUE findings (claims C2/C6) are untouched and remain owner-gated. Derivation `docs/proofs/DssoAuthenticationAssurance.md`. | `dapps/dsso/authn.c` (`dsso_authn_login` / `dsso_authn_enrol_device` / `dsso_authn_recover` / `dsso_authn_cluster_meter`); `determ-dsso selftest-authn` | done |
| S-109 | 🟠 Partially mitigated | **Medium (wallet key material reaches disk world-readable, and a failed narrowing is silent).** Two defects on the wallet output surface. **P-1** — every wallet output file was created by `std::ofstream` at 0666 masked by the umask (0644 under the usual umask 022), the complete secret was written into it, and the mode was narrowed only afterwards; measured under `strace` on a plain successful run, no injection, no mutation. Any local process that can `open(2)` the path during that window reads the key material, and no after-the-fact check can see it — `keystore-audit`, the `mode_not_0600` hazard tag and the `tools/test_wallet_shamir_rotate.sh` section 28 assertion all observe the final 0600 and report a correct file. **P-2** — the narrowing call discarded its `error_code` (`(void)perm_ec`), so on a filesystem or platform where narrowing cannot happen (FAT/exFAT, some network and FUSE mounts, a path owned by another user) the command left a world-readable key file, exited 0 and printed nothing; reproduced with an `LD_PRELOAD` shim returning EPERM from chmod/fchmod/fchmodat. **Partially mitigated 2026-09-17 (first pass):** `write_bytes_file_0600` now creates with `::open(..., O_CREAT + O_TRUNC + O_CLOEXEC + O_NOFOLLOW, 0600)` AND `::fchmod(fd, 0600)` before the first write — both are needed, since the create mode applies only when the file is created and an existing 0644 file reopened that way stays 0644 — checks `perm_ec`, emits the diagnostic the daemon already uses and reports `"perms_narrowed": false` in the `--json` summaries; `backup-create --shares-out` and `keyfile-decrypt --out` are routed through it. Gate `tools/test_wallet_out_perms.sh` (FAST, 18 assertions, mutants M1-M5 RED). **Further mitigated 2026-09-17 (second pass — the two follow-up sites this row named). REPRODUCED independently at the pre-change binary before anything was touched.** `account-import-many` ran its own ofstream-then-narrow-then-discard sequence ONCE PER RECORD. Plain successful run, `umask 022`, nothing injected: a passive `LD_PRELOAD` observer that forwards every call to libc unchanged reported `want=0600 was=0644 size=68` at the instant the narrowing was attempted for rec1, rec2 AND rec3 — world-readable with the complete 68-byte DAK1 container already in it, on every iteration, and bytes 36..68 of that file are byte-equal to that record's `privkey_hex`. Under `strace` for record 2 the order is `openat(..., O_CREAT + O_TRUNC, 0666) = 3`, then `write(3, "DAK1...", 68)`, then `fchmodat(..., 0600)`; with narrowing denied by the EPERM shim the command left three 0644 key files, exited 0, wrote ZERO bytes to stderr and recorded `status: ok` for each. `keyfile-rotate` staged to `<out>_tmp.bin`, fsynced and renamed correctly, but created the temp with `ofstream` at the umask mode and narrowed it only AFTER `fs::rename` — and the mode travels with the inode, so the WIDE file existed under the FINAL name: an atomic publish that publishes a world-readable keyfile. The observer wrapped `rename(2)` and read the destination the instant it returned: `srcmode=0644 size=171 rc=0 destmode_after=0644`, on a pre-existing 0644 `--out` and on a fresh one alike. Both sites are now routed through the SAME `write_bytes_file_0600` — no third copy of the primitive was written — which gained one defaulted `err_out` parameter so the fault-tolerant per-record loop keeps a failure reason in THAT record's summary entry instead of aborting a 50,000-record batch; the per-record zeroization stayed exactly where it was, and a record whose keyfile could not be narrowed carries a failure-only `"perms_narrowed": false`. For `keyfile-rotate` the staging write goes through the helper, so the inode the rename publishes is already 0600 (`destmode_after=0600` measured after the fix); the fsync and the atomic rename are untouched and the post-rename narrowing keeps its belt-and-braces role with a CHECKED `perm_ec`. The rotated content is AEAD-wrapped so its severity is below the plaintext sites, but it was the one write-and-rename shape in the file. Gate: the existing `tools/test_wallet_out_perms.sh` was EXTENDED rather than duplicated — 18 to 44 assertions, FAST (already in the `ONLY_PATTERN`, so the FAST count is unchanged at 323), 0 sections skipped — with an ordering leg per site against PRE-EXISTING 0644 targets, asserted on records 2 AND 3 so a fix restricting only the first file of the loop goes RED, and for the rotate on the STAGED TEMP, requiring that a mode-setting call exists between its creating open and its first write, that the last one sets 0600, that nothing widens it before the publish and that the rename happened; path-based `chmod`/`fchmodat`/`fchmodat2` count alongside descriptor-based `fchmod` and both spellings are stripped in the non-vacuity self-check. Mutants M1-M6 each RED against the rebuilt binary. **One behaviour change, stated rather than discovered later:** the helper opens `O_NOFOLLOW`, and at the pre-change binary a symlink planted in `--out-dir` redirected a customer's plaintext seed into a file of the attacker's choosing — measured, 68 bytes landed at the link target and that record still reported `status: ok` with exit 0. That record now reports `status: error` with the `ELOOP` reason and the batch continues; NEITHER command's exit code changes. **NOT closed as of the second pass (2026-09-17), and this is why the row was OPEN — every site named in THIS paragraph except the Windows arm and the two `::chmod` exports was closed by the fourth pass below, 2026-09-18; the file:line references here are the pre-2026-09-18 ones and are kept as measured:** the fix is POSIX-only (on Windows the `_S_IREAD` / `_S_IWRITE` mode argument drives only FILE_ATTRIBUTE_READONLY and the ACL comes by inheritance — `S005PassphraseKeyfile.md` F-4); SIX independent `std::filesystem::permissions` sites in `wallet/main.cpp` still keep the old ofstream-then-narrow sequence with a discarded `perm_ec` — the `account-import-many` summary (`wallet/main.cpp:2319`), `backup-create --envelopes-out` (`:3223`), and the signed-transaction outputs of `cold-sign` (`:9801`), `sign-anon-tx` (`:10186`), `tx-batch-sign` (`:10803`) and `param-change-build` (`:23566`); and THREE further outputs narrow nothing at all, which this row had not previously named — `create-recovery` writes the DRS1 container of password-wrapped share envelopes with a bare `ofstream` and no permission call (`:6051`), and `encrypt-message` (`:7098`) and `decrypt-message` (`:7264`) write through a second unhardened byte-writer, `write_file_bytes` (`:6917`), which has no permission call either — `decrypt-message --out` is the RECOVERED PLAINTEXT. None of the six is plaintext key material, and that is the stated reason they were left: the P-2 reporting half is mechanical but is a rider across six commands and four `--json` contracts, and the P-1 ordering half is NOT mechanical — all six write through a TEXT-mode `std::ofstream` while the hardened helper is binary-mode, so routing them through it would change their on-disk bytes on Windows (LF becomes CRLF today) in files downstream tooling parses, on a platform the Linux branch cannot execute. The three unnarrowed outputs are a separate follow-up of their own and are NOT covered by any gate. The same is true of the two bare `::chmod(out_path, 0600)` audit/history exports (`:19051`, `:19518` as measured that day) — this sentence read "whose comments record a deliberate best-effort decision for non-key data" until 2026-09-18, when review established that one of the two sites has no comment at all and the other's says something else; see clause (c) of the fourth pass below for what was measured and why they are actually left. Same class as **S-091** at its limit — `save_node_key` writes the node identity seed with no permission call at all — which stays OPEN and is not touched by this increment. **Further mitigated 2026-09-18 (third pass — the shared primitive, and one error-path defect this row had not named).** The three hand-rolled restricted writers (`wallet/main.cpp::write_bytes_file_0600`, `src/crypto/keys.cpp::save_node_key`, `src/main.cpp::write_account_file_0600`) were extracted into ONE header-only primitive, `include/determ/util/restricted_write.hpp`. Every deliberate divergence between the three — refuse-versus-continue on a failed narrowing, whether the trailing `permissions` call happens at all, Windows text-versus-binary mode, and every diagnostic string — stays a parameter or stays at its call site, so no reporting policy, no exit code and no on-disk byte changes anywhere. **Reading the three for that extraction found a genuine defect in THIS one, reproduced against the binary built from HEAD before anything was touched:** `write_bytes_file_0600` was the only one of the three that did not remove the file after a failed `::write` or `::close`, so with `write(2)` forced to return 0 by an `LD_PRELOAD` shim, `shamir-split --out` and `account-import --out` each exited 1 with their named diagnostic and left a ZERO-BYTE key container behind under the final name — which a `--force`-guarded retry then refuses to overwrite. Both siblings unlink there and say why at the locus, and the wave doctrine's rule that no file may be left behind on a refusal path is binding on all three, so this was drift and the primitive now removes it for all three. Severity is bounded and stated rather than inflated: by the time a write can fail the file is already 0600, so this is not a permissions leak, and every container the wallet writes rejects a truncated file on its magic and length checks — which is why it is recorded here and in the DECISION-LOG rather than given a ledger id of its own. Gated by four new in-process assertions in `determ test-account-create-perms` (22 to 26) against a REAL write failure produced by lowering `RLIMIT_FSIZE` below the payload, with no injection hook on the shipped write path; mutants M6 (no unlink after a failed write) and M7 (no unlink on a refusal) RED. `tools/test_wallet_out_perms.sh` (50 assertions), `tools/test_node_key_perms.sh` (7 wrapper over 18 in-process) and `tools/test_account_create_perms.sh` (29) all stay green UNCHANGED — not one line of `tools/` was edited, and the FAST wrapper count does not move. **Further mitigated 2026-09-18 (fourth pass — the SIX independent `std::filesystem::permissions` sites and the THREE unnarrowed outputs this row named above). REPRODUCED at the pre-change binary, built from `372e863`, before anything was touched.** Every site was measured under `umask 000` with `strace` and with a PASSIVE `LD_PRELOAD` observer that forwards every mode-setting call to libc unchanged and reports the file's real mode and size at the instant the narrowing is attempted. The six P-1 sites, at that instant: `cold-sign --out` `was=0644 size=425`, `sign-anon-tx --out` `was=0666 size=432`, `tx-batch-sign --out` `was=0644 size=419`, `param-change-build --out` `was=0666 size=567`, `backup-create --envelopes-out` `was=0644 size=269`, `account-import-many --summary` `was=0644 size=351` — the COMPLETE artifact already on disk, world-readable and in two cases world-WRITABLE, before anything narrowed it. The `strace` order is `openat(..., O_CREAT + O_TRUNC, 0666)`, then `write(...)`, then `fchmodat(..., 0600)` at all six, and the FINAL mode is 0600 at all six — which is why every check in this tree, including this gate's own outcome section, was green throughout. The three unnarrowed outputs had NO mode-setting call naming them at all and ended at mode 0666 under `umask 000` — world-writable, permanently: `create-recovery --out` (the DRS1 container of password-wrapped share envelopes) and the `encrypt-message` / `decrypt-message --out` pair, which share one byte writer; `decrypt-message --out` is the RECOVERED PLAINTEXT. **The change.** All nine now go through the shared primitive `determ::util::write_restricted_0600` by way of ONE new renderer, `wallet/main.cpp::write_file_0600_quiet`, which exists for POLICY and is not a second copy of the mechanism: `write_bytes_file_0600` hard-wires Windows BINARY mode, its own diagnostic strings and the P-2 reporting shape, and six of the nine write through a TEXT-mode `std::ofstream`. That text-versus-binary difference is the reason this row previously gave for leaving them, and the primitive's `windows_binary` knob removes it — passing false reproduces each site's existing Windows stream exactly. The three that had no permission call also pass `final_narrow=false`, so no by-path chmod is ADDED on Windows where none existed; on POSIX it would be redundant with the `fchmod` and is the TOCTOU shape the primitive exists to avoid. **What this pass deliberately does NOT do:** the P-2 half stays open at all nine — `final_ec` is discarded exactly as `(void)perm_ec` discarded it and a failed pre-write `fchmod` still warns nobody — because adding it is a new stderr line and a new summary field across six commands and four `--json` contracts, which is a separate increment and not a rider on a window fix. **Measured, not asserted:** with fixed keys the pre- and post-change binaries produce BYTE-IDENTICAL `cold-sign`, `sign-anon-tx`, `tx-batch-sign`, `param-change-build`, `account-import-many --summary`, `account-import-many` per-record keyfiles and `decrypt-message` outputs, and identical exit codes and stdout on nine success paths and fourteen failure paths; identical STDERR on the nine success paths and on six of the fourteen failure paths, with the other eight differing ONLY by an appended `: <strerror>` reason — re-measured 2026-09-18 after review, **24 same / 8 different**, was 32 same / 0 different before that reason was rendered (see the fourth behaviour change below); the randomised containers (DBE1, DSS1, DRS1 and the AEAD blob) keep their exact lengths and a `create-recovery` container written by either binary is read back correctly by the other. **FOUR behaviour changes are stated rather than discovered later; the first three come with the primitive and the fourth was added after review:** `::close` is now CHECKED at the six sites that let the `ofstream` destructor close it, so an ENOSPC/EIO flush failure is no longer exit 0 on a truncated file; a failed write or close REMOVES the file instead of publishing a fragment under the final name; and `O_NOFOLLOW` makes a symlinked `--out` FAIL where it used to be written through — measured at the pre-change binary, 425 bytes of a signed envelope, 303 bytes of a DRS1 container and 68 bytes of message ciphertext each landed at an attacker-chosen link target with exit 0 and an empty stderr, and each now exits 1 with that site's EXISTING "cannot open --out for write" diagnostic and nothing written. **That third one is an OPERATOR-VISIBLE EXIT-CODE CHANGE ON NINE SHIPPED COMMANDS and it is not only an attacker scenario — spelled out 2026-09-18 after review, which is the same omission this repo corrected once already for `determ account create --out`.** Measured PRE vs POST at the two binaries across all nine: (1) `--out /dev/stdout` goes exit 0 to exit 1 on SEVEN of the nine — unconditionally on the four with no already-exists check (`account-import-many --summary`, `create-recovery --out`, `encrypt-message --out`, `decrypt-message --out`) and whenever `--force` bypasses that check on `backup-create --envelopes-out`, `cold-sign --out` and `tx-batch-sign --out`; `sign-anon-tx --out` and `param-change-build --out` do not move only because their exists-check already refused `/dev/stdout`. A pipeline that pipes `create-recovery ... --out /dev/stdout` into `gpg -c` loses 303 bytes of DRS1 and gets exit 1. (2) A DANGLING symlink — the legitimate `ln -s dated.json out.json` idiom — now fails on ALL NINE, including the five with an already-exists check, because `exists()` is false through a dangling link. (3) `--out` inside a SYMLINKED DIRECTORY is UNCHANGED (exit 0 at both binaries): `O_NOFOLLOW` guards the final path component only, so an attacker who controls a parent directory is not stopped by it. (4) `account-import-many --summary` now has two more ways to exit 1 AFTER both per-record keyfiles are already written at 0600 and NOT removed — a partial success reported as a failure. That shape is PRE-EXISTING, not new (a `--summary` naming a directory does it at both binaries, measured), so it is DOCUMENTED rather than restructured: making the summary fail first would mean opening it before the records it describes exist. All of this is now in `docs/CLI-REFERENCE.md` as one note that names all nine commands, plus the `create-recovery` row; the wallet USAGE TEXT still says nothing and is the remaining half, left because that half of `wallet/main.cpp` belongs to another track this wave. **FOURTH behaviour change, added 2026-09-18 after review: the refusal now says WHY.** `wallet/main.cpp::write_file_0600_quiet` (`:433`) discarded the `errno` the primitive captures, so an `ELOOP` on nine commands printed a bare "cannot open --out for write: <path>" on a path `ls -l`, `touch` and `test -w` all call fine. It now hands back the same `": " + strerror` SUFFIX its sibling `write_bytes_file_0600` (`:311`) has always rendered and that `include/determ/util/restricted_write.hpp` asks every caller for at its `err` field. A suffix on a line each site already printed — no new line, no exit code, no stdout, no `--json` field. Its one measured cost is the parity number above: the eight failure paths where the reason is now appended (three `: No such file or directory`, five `: Is a directory`). **Gate:** `tools/test_wallet_out_perms.sh` EXTENDED rather than duplicated, 50 to 101 assertions (83 on 2026-09-18, then 18 more in the new section G below), FAST (already in the `ONLY_PATTERN`, so the FAST wrapper count does NOT move), 0 sections skipped. New section F runs an ordering leg per site — seven of the nine against PRE-EXISTING 0644 targets; `sign-anon-tx` and `param-change-build` refuse an existing `--out` and have no `--force`, so their only path is a fresh create and the section says so in its own output rather than implying coverage it does not have. Nine new section-D outcome assertions run under `umask 000`, so a 0600 result means the mode was SET and not inherited from a friendly umask, plus a tenth that the table of nine was actually walked. New section E gates the ORDERING PARSER itself over synthetic traces with known answers — it needs no strace, no compiler and no binary, so it runs on Darwin and the BSDs where F cannot, though NOT on Windows, where this wrapper exits at its platform-skip marker before reaching E and the shared parser therefore has zero gate coverage. New section G, added after review, gates the `O_NOFOLLOW` exit-code change itself at all nine sites with two clauses each — a dangling symlink at the output path is REFUSED and nothing is written through it, and the refusal NAMES ITS REASON on stderr — and needs only symlink support, so it also runs where F cannot. Section F's eighteen assertions are nine verdicts about the wallet plus nine parser non-vacuity self-checks that are green against a defective wallet too (mutant M1 fails one assertion there, not two); the section says so in its own output so the count is not read as eighteen checks on `determ-wallet`. Mutants M1-M10 each RED: M1-M4 and M9-M10 against the REBUILT binary (one site reverted to create-then-narrow; the pre-write `::fchmod` dropped from the primitive; `chmod(path, 0666)` inserted between the narrowing and the first write, which leaves every final-mode assertion in the tree green and is caught only by the ordering parser; `write_file_bytes` reverted to the un-narrowed `ofstream`; M9 the `strerror` suffix dropped at one call site, which leaves every mode assertion green and is caught only by section G's diagnosability clause; M10 `O_NOFOLLOW` dropped from the primitive, caught by section G's refusal clause), M5-M8 against the mutated parser or wrapper the gate invokes (M8 plants a bare `except: pass` in `tools/strace_order.py` and turns `tools/test_gates_can_fail.sh` RED at 51/1, which is what proves the D4 ratchet now reaches the de-duplicated parser at all), with the harness refusing a verdict unless a marker grep proves the mutation reached the source AND the relinked binary. **The strace-ordering parser was de-duplicated in the same increment:** it existed in THREE copies (`tools/test_wallet_out_perms.sh` section A, the `ordered.py` that section C generated, and `tools/test_account_create_perms.sh`'s `order_verdict`) and had already drifted once — a descriptor-only spelling scores `create 0600; fchmod(fd,0600); chmod(path,0666); write; chmod(path,0600)` as CLOSED with the window fully open. One survivor, `tools/strace_order.py`, with the `chmod`/`fchmod`/`fchmodat`/`fchmodat2` enumeration; equivalence was measured by running each removed copy and the survivor over the SAME traces the originals were exercised with plus the two negative shapes — 12 comparisons, identical assertion outcome, 0 divergent. **STILL NOT CLOSED, and this is why the row does not move to Mitigated:** (a) nothing is closed on WINDOWS — the primitive's Windows arm is the caller's `std::ofstream` verbatim and the effective ACL arrives by inheritance from the parent directory (`S005PassphraseKeyfile.md` F-4); (b) P-2 — a narrowing that FAILS is still silent at all nine of the sites this pass migrated, per the paragraph above; (c) TWO sites remain that open their `--out` with a bare `ofstream` and call `::chmod(out_path, 0600)` by PATH afterwards — `tx-history-export` (`wallet/main.cpp::cmd_tx_history_export`, the open at `:19252`, the chmod at `:19263`) and `account-balance-history` (`wallet/main.cpp::cmd_account_balance_history`, `:19690` and `:19698`). **CORRECTED 2026-09-18 after independent review: the two sentences this clause carried until then were both false, and they were the stated reason the row did not close.** (i) It said the export is "readable for the duration of the write". It is NOT. Re-measured under `strace` at the committed binary under `umask 000`, both sites: `openat(AT_FDCWD, "hist.ndjson", O_WRONLY + O_CREAT + O_TRUNC, 0666) = 3` then `chmod("hist.ndjson", 0600) = 0` with NO `write(3, ...)` between them — final mode 600, final size 0. The chmod runs immediately after the open and before the sink is used, which is structural: the open is deliberately done before any RPC chatter so an unwritable destination fails fast, and every row is emitted after the RPC connect. The real residual is a short EMPTY-FILE window (an attacker who wins that race keeps a read descriptor and then sees everything streamed into it) plus a by-path chmod, which is TOCTOU. (ii) It attributed the decision to "their comments". `cmd_account_balance_history` has NO comment at that site at all — `wallet/main.cpp:19715-19732` is a `// -- Output sink --` banner, the stream declarations, the open, the failure arm, `sink = &out_f;` and the chmod, with nothing else between them. The only comment is at `tx-history-export` (`:19228-19230`) and it records that a chmod FAILURE does not fail the export, which is the P-2 question, not a decision about the P-1 window. The authority this row deferred to did not exist at one of the two sites. **The true reason they are left, and it is a reason about the code and not about a decision:** both sinks are a STREAMING `std::ostream` flushed per line (for `tail -f` friendliness) while `write_restricted_0600` is a ONE-SHOT blob writer that takes a pointer and a length — the primitive does not fit, and nothing in this tree bridges a restricted descriptor to a `std::ostream` (`grep` for `stdio_filebuf` / `fdopen` across the repo returns nothing). **The residual that IS worth naming, demonstrated 2026-09-18 and strictly worse than the window this clause used to describe: both sites FOLLOW SYMLINKS.** Neither open carries `O_NOFOLLOW` and the chmod is by path, so `--out` pointing at a symlink truncates and re-modes the TARGET: measured, a `victim.txt` at mode 644 with 27 bytes became mode 600 with 0 bytes after `tx-history-export --rpc-port <n> --accounts <addr> --out out.ndjson` where `out.ndjson -> victim.txt`, and `account-balance-history` did the same to a second file (27 bytes to 0). Transcript `/root/audit/z3/REMEASURE-th-sites.txt`, which also carries the `strace` of the ordering above. Anyone who can plant a symlink in the output directory gets an arbitrary truncate plus an arbitrary chmod-to-0600 on any path the invoking user can write. It is PRE-EXISTING — this increment neither caused it nor widened it — and it is NOT closed here: closing it race-free needs a restricted descriptor bridged into a streaming `std::ostream`, which on libstdc++ means `__gnu_cxx::stdio_filebuf` and on MSVC has no standard equivalent, so it is a portability decision plus a new exit-code change on two more shipped commands plus its own gate — a separate increment, not one small change. A pre-open `lstat` refusal would be four lines but is defeated by a race, and shipping a racy check as a fix for a TOCTOU is worse than naming the gap. Recorded here with file:line so the next increment starts from the measurement. The one remaining `std::filesystem::permissions` call in the file, `keyfile-rotate` at `wallet/main.cpp::cmd_keyfile_rotate` (`:4372`), is NOT of this class and is deliberately untouched: it runs AFTER an atomic rename that publishes an inode the staging write already created at 0600, its `error_code` IS checked, and on Windows it is the only narrowing there is. | `wallet/main.cpp::write_bytes_file_0600` + `wallet/main.cpp::write_file_0600_quiet` (the nine sites migrated 2026-09-18) + the two `::chmod` exports at `wallet/main.cpp::cmd_tx_history_export` (`:19263`) and `wallet/main.cpp::cmd_account_balance_history` (`:19730`) | authorized |
| S-110 | 🟠 Partially mitigated | **Medium (local key disclosure — client tooling; REPRODUCED 2026-09-17 before the fix).** Every `determ-light` subcommand that takes a private seed took it as a command-line ARGUMENT — `pq-sign-tx` / `pq-address` / `pq-transfer` (`--mldsa-seed`, `--ed-seed`) and `build-shield` / `build-unshield` (`--blind-seed`). A running process's argument vector is world-readable at `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`, `ps` prints it to every user on the host, and the operator's shell writes it verbatim into the history file; for an ML-DSA / Ed25519 seed that string IS the private key. **🟠 Partially mitigated 2026-09-17 — every one of those flags gained a `--*-seed-from <file:path or env:NAME or prompt>` twin** (`light/seed_source.cpp`, the convention `determ-wallet keyfile-create --passphrase-from` already uses; on 2026-09-18 that mechanism moved VERBATIM into the header-only `include/determ/util/secret_source.hpp` for S-114/S-115 and `light/seed_source.cpp` became a forwarder to it, with this row's gate green UNCHANGED as the proof), the raw flags now print a named `WARNING[seed-on-command-line]` to stderr, and resolved seeds are `determ_secure_zero`d. **NOT closed:** the raw flags still exist and still work unchanged (removing them is an owner decision; three shipped tests use them), so an operator who keeps using them keeps the exposure — the fix makes the safe path available and loud, it does not remove the unsafe one. Gate `tools/test_light_seed_source.sh` (28 assertions incl. reading the running child's `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`). | `light/seed_source.cpp`, `light/pq_sign_tx.cpp`, `light/main.cpp::cmd_build_shield` | done (raw flags: owner) |
| S-111 | 🟠 Partially mitigated | **🟠 PARTIALLY MITIGATED 2026-09-18 — the POSIX write window is closed on BOTH branches of the command and gated; nothing is closed on Windows and the P-2 policy is unchanged.** **Medium (private key reaches disk world-readable in the DAEMON binary; REPRODUCED 2026-09-17, re-reproduced 2026-09-18 before the fix).** `determ account create --out` wrote its plaintext branch with a bare `std::ofstream`, so the file was created at the umask-masked 0666 and the JSON — which carries `"privkey"` in clear — was written into it BEFORE `std::filesystem::permissions` narrowed it to 0600. Measured against the binary built from HEAD: the creating `openat` took mode 0666, a 243-byte write followed, and `fchmodat(..., 0600)` came last; an `LD_PRELOAD` observer on the narrowing call reported the file at mode 0644 with all 243 bytes already written, and with the narrowing denied user `nobody` read the privkey straight out of it. The final mode is 600, so every after-the-fact check saw a correct file and the window was invisible to all of them. This is the same P-1 defect S-109 records for the wallet, in the shipped daemon, on plaintext key material. **The change:** both file branches — the plaintext one and the `--passphrase` AES-256-GCM envelope a few dozen lines below it, which had the identical ordering defect — now go through one writer that creates with `::open(..., O_WRONLY + O_CREAT + O_TRUNC + O_CLOEXEC + O_NOFOLLOW, 0600)` AND `::fchmod(fd, 0600)` before the first write. Both calls are needed and neither implies the other: measured, `O_CREAT + O_TRUNC` with a mode does NOT narrow a file that already exists, so the create mode alone leaves the whole window open on every overwrite. The container bytes, the success lines and the exit codes are unchanged. **The P-2 half was already correct at this site and is untouched:** this is the one place in the tree that checked `perm_ec` and printed a named warning — wallet/main.cpp copied its diagnostic from here — so a narrowing that fails is still reported and the command still exits 0. Gate `determ test-account-create-perms` (26 assertions, in-process — 22 here plus the four the 2026-09-18 shared-primitive increment added for the leave-nothing-behind rule) plus the wrapper `tools/test_account_create_perms.sh` (29 assertions: strace ordering on both branches against a PRE-EXISTING 0644 target, an LD_PRELOAD EPERM leg asserting both the real harm and both named diagnostics, and the platform-skip arms); mutants M1-M6 RED. **NOT closed, and this is why the row does not move further:** (a) the fix is POSIX-only — on Windows `_S_IREAD`/`_S_IWRITE` drives only FILE_ATTRIBUTE_READONLY and the ACL arrives by inheritance from the parent directory (`S005PassphraseKeyfile.md` F-4), so that arm keeps the previous `std::ofstream` verbatim and the window there is exactly as wide as it was; (b) when the narrowing genuinely fails the command still writes the plaintext key into a file it could not protect and exits 0, warning — REFUSING there, the shape `src/crypto/keys.cpp::save_node_key` uses, is an exit-code change on a shipped command and is deliberately a separate increment. `O_NOFOLLOW` does make a symlinked `--out` (including `/dev/stdout`) fail ELOOP where it used to succeed; that is the one exit-code change here and the usage text and CLI reference say so. | `src/main.cpp` `cmd_account_create` (both `--out` branches) + `write_account_file_0600` | done |
| S-112 | ✅ Mitigated | **✅ MITIGATED 2026-09-18 — the operator's `--wait` now reaches `outbox enqueue`'s trust-minimized read, and every `outbox` verb that cannot act on a flag refuses it. REPRODUCED BY EXECUTION before the fix, not by inspection: against the pre-fix binary all seven verbs accepted `--wait 30` and returned in 12-19 ms, and, driven over an in-process committee-signed fixture chain whose next block lands on the third successor poll, `cmd_enqueue`'s four-argument call polled the successor ONCE and failed closed with a message that never mentions a wait, while the identical read with the wait forwarded polled three times and produced the hint** (`/root/audit/z1/REPRO-1-accept-and-ignore-prefix.txt`, `REPRO-2-enqueue-drops-the-wait-prefix.txt`). **Medium/Op (a trust-minimized reader silently drops `--wait`).** `determ-light outbox`'s shared argument parser accepted `--wait` for EVERY verb, `outbox reconcile` forwarded it, and `outbox enqueue` called `read_account_trustless` with FOUR arguments where the wait is the seventh parameter and defaults to 0 — so `outbox enqueue --wait N` was accepted and was silently a no-op. **Not a soundness defect:** per `WaitHoldAndWaitSoundness.md` WH-SN a wait never changes WHICH value a verdict carries, and the nonce hint is only floored into `max(local, hint)`. It was the LIVENESS gap `--wait` exists to close: the always-head readers fail closed on every live current-state read without a wait, so on a fresh outbox the observable symptom is the `cannot reserve a nonce offline` refusal. **The change.** The hint read is now `light/outbox.cpp::nonce_hint_trustless`, a named route whose `wait_seconds` parameter has NO DEFAULT — the omitted-defaulted-argument shape that caused this cannot compile any more — and `cmd_enqueue` passes `a.wait` into it. **Gates.** `determ-light selftest-outbox-hint-wait` (5 assertions over the real reader on a fixture chain: one extra successor poll per wait-second, the fail-closed diagnostic naming the wait, and the verified hint existing only when the wait carried through), wrapped by `tools/test_light_outbox_flag_surface.sh`; the `cmd_enqueue` call site is covered by `tools/test_light_wait_surface.sh`'s derived-helper check, whose KNOWN GAP entry for this defect is deleted and whose new RH and RJ selftest cases inject a literal 0 in the route and at the call site and require RED. **WH-6 is resolved:** its STATEMENT is authoritative and its enumeration was incomplete; `docs/proofs/WaitHoldAndWaitSoundness.md` §4.6 now lists `outbox enqueue` and `outbox reconcile`, and §8 counts nineteen binding consumers rather than seventeen. The wider accept-and-ignore surface this row named is S-113. | `light/outbox_cli.cpp` `cmd_enqueue`, `light/outbox.cpp` `nonce_hint_trustless`; `docs/proofs/WaitHoldAndWaitSoundness.md` §4.6 WH-6 | done |
| S-113 | ✅ Mitigated | **✅ MITIGATED 2026-09-18 — every `determ-light outbox` verb now accepts only the flags it reads and refuses the rest, fail-closed and by name; and a flag the verb does read but that nothing reads in the configuration it was given is refused there too.** **Low/Op (a shared parser gives every verb a silent accept-and-ignore surface).** `light/outbox_cli.cpp` has ONE `parse_args` for all seven verbs, so it RECOGNIZES the union of their flags — and it ACCEPTED that union for every verb. An ignored flag changes no output, no exit code and no file, so nothing in the tree could see it: that is why S-112 lived for weeks and why the other five verbs' `--wait` was never noticed at all. MEASURED against the pre-fix binary, not inferred: 133 (verb, flag) pairs were driven through the process and 133 were accepted, of which only 43 are read. The 43 that are, per flag — `--outbox` all seven; `--json` enqueue submit reconcile status prune; `--genesis` enqueue submit reconcile replace; `--keyfile` `--to` `--amount` `--fee` `--nonce` `--payload-hex` enqueue and replace; `--rpc-port` and `--timeout-ms` enqueue submit reconcile; `--wait` enqueue and reconcile; `--idempotency-key` and `--max-messages` enqueue only; `--older-than` and `--include-unlocated` prune only; `--state` and `--resume` reconcile only; `--now` submit only. Per verb that is enqueue 14, reconcile 8, replace 8, submit 6, prune 4, status 2, recover 1 — so 90 of the 133 pairs were accept-and-ignore, including `outbox status --wait 30`, `outbox recover --timeout-ms 1` and `outbox replace --json`. Two documented surfaces were also wrong in the other direction: `--timeout-ms` was READ by enqueue and reconcile and advertised on neither. **The change.** A per-verb accepted-flag table in `light/outbox_cli.cpp` (`verb_flags()`); anything outside a verb's set is refused before the branch that would parse it, with a diagnostic naming the flag and the verb and a non-zero exit, exactly as an unknown argument is. The union used to tell a wrong-verb flag from a typo is DERIVED from the same table, so the two can never disagree. The usage block and `docs/CLI-REFERENCE.md` now state the enforced sets. **The per-INVOCATION half** (`refuse_inert_flags`, added after the independent review found the first version false inside `enqueue`): a flag in a verb's set can still be read by nothing in the invocation given — `cmd_enqueue`'s nonce-reservation block is the only reader of `--rpc-port`, `--timeout-ms` and `--wait`, it is skipped whole under an explicit `--nonce` and opens no socket without `--rpc-port`, and `anchored_head` loads the cached anchor `--state` names only on the `--resume` path. Six (verb, flag, configuration) triples were MEASURED inert by driving every accepted pair in every configuration its verb admits and comparing exit code, output, the bytes of the outbox afterwards and wall clock; those six are refused, naming the flag AND the flag whose presence or absence made it inert, before anything is locked or written. Nothing else measured inert. One in-tree invocation was itself an instance and is corrected: `tools/test_light_outbox.sh` passed `--rpc-port 1` beside an explicit `--nonce 3`. **Gate `tools/test_light_outbox_flag_surface.sh`** (FAST, 65 assertions, nothing greps the source): the flag UNIVERSE is derived from the binary — each verb is provoked into its own wrong-verb refusal, which prints `accepted here: <its set>`, and the union of the seven printed sets is the universe — so a flag ADDED to the parser enters the measurement through the binary's own answer instead of being invisible to a list in the script. All 7 × |universe| pairs are driven through the process and classified four ways (accepted / refused by this verb / refused as inert in this invocation / not recognized at all), and three sets must agree per verb: what the binary accepts, what the binary says it accepts, and a hand-pinned contract. Every accepted pair must then observably change what the verb does (an exit code, a produced record, a JSON object, the acknowledged tx hash, a transport diagnostic naming the port, or a wall-clock window against a peer that never answers), each of the six inert triples must be refused in its inert configuration and NOT refused in its enabling one, and every pair the matrix measured as accepted must be probed or declined BY NAME — a coverage assertion that closes leg B over leg A's measurement. `determ-light help` must advertise exactly what the binary was measured accepting. **What is NOT closed, named rather than implied:** seven (verb, flag) pairs' EFFECTS cannot be observed without a daemon and are declined by name in the gate, banked as no pass — `--older-than` and `--include-unlocated` need a PROVEN-consumed slot; `--resume`, `--state`, `--wait` and `--json` on reconcile are read past `connect_and_pin`; and `--wait` on enqueue bounds a successor poll the gate's accept-and-never-answer peer never reaches (its EFFECT is leg D's, over a committee-signed fixture chain; its REFUSAL in both inert configurations is asserted directly). And the CLASS is closed only for `outbox`: a measured sweep found this is the tree's only parser shared across verbs, and `light/main.cpp` and `wallet/main.cpp` per-command parsers do refuse unknown arguments, but a per-command parser can still bind a flag it never reads and nothing gates that. One such site is confirmed by execution: `determ genesis-tool build <cfg> --wait 5 --older-than 9` exits 0 having ignored both, because the parser at `src/main.cpp:5511` scans only for `--json`. That file belongs to another track this wave and the site is left alone, named here. | `light/outbox_cli.cpp` `verb_flags` and `parse_args`; residual `src/main.cpp:5503` `cmd_genesis_tool_build` | done |
| S-114 | 🟠 Partially mitigated | **Medium (local key disclosure — operator tooling; REPRODUCED by execution 2026-09-18 before the fix).** Twelve `determ-wallet` argument parsers took private key material as a command-line ARGUMENT: `shamir-split --secret`, `envelope encrypt --password`, `envelope decrypt --password`, `account-derive-batch --seed` (the MASTER seed of a whole account set), `account-import --priv`, `backup-create --secret`, `keyfile-create --priv` (the validator key the passphrase-encrypted node_key wraps — that command's PASSPHRASE has been off argv since it shipped, its private key had not), `create-recovery --seed`, `create-recovery --password`, `recover --password`, `message-sign --priv` and `rpc-auth --secret`. A running process's argument vector is world-readable at `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` — measured `-r--r--r--`, and read there from an unrelated process in a different session — `ps` prints it to every user on the host, and the operator's shell writes it verbatim into the history file. This is S-110 in the second binary; the 2026-09-17 S-110 entry named two of these sites as deliberately out of scope. **🟠 Partially mitigated 2026-09-18 — every one of the twelve gained a `--<name>-from <file:path or env:NAME or prompt>` twin**, the raw flags now print the named `WARNING[seed-on-command-line]` to stderr naming the `-from` alternative, both forms of one secret at once are refused, and every malformed source (missing or empty file, unset or empty variable, empty path or variable name, unknown scheme) is refused with a named diagnostic and exit 1. The MECHANISM was promoted out of `light/` into the header-only `include/determ/util/secret_source.hpp`; `light/seed_source.cpp` and `wallet/main.cpp::passphrase_from_source` are now forwarders to it, so the convention exists once instead of the two copies that were already diverging. **The three sources are NOT peers, and every operator-facing surface this row touches now says so.** `file:<path>` with restrictive permissions (0600) is the STRONGEST: the value is never in the process table, never in `/proc/<pid>/environ`, and never in the shell history. `prompt` is next. `env:<NAME>` is the WEAKEST of the three — S-110's own row already said the value stays visible in `/proc/<pid>/environ` to the same UID, and, stated here because it was nowhere stated: the obvious way an operator reaches for it, `SECRET=... determ-wallet account-import --priv-from env:SECRET`, puts the secret back into the shell history file VERBATIM — one of the three leaks this row exists to close. `env:` is worth having only when the variable is set by a parent process, a CI secret store or a systemd credential, never when it is typed on the command line. An unrecognised source specifier longer than 16 characters is now ELIDED in the refusal rather than echoed: the likeliest way to reach that refusal is the typo `--priv-from <the key>`, one character from `--priv <key>`, and echoing it whole converts a transient `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` exposure into a persistent one in CI logs, ticket attachments and terminal scrollback. Gate `tools/test_secret_on_argv.sh` (102 assertions, FAST) — including the kernel read of the running child's `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` under both forms, taken while the child is BLOCKED in its `--out` FIFO open so the sample is genuinely after the secret has been used. **The mutant record, with its GREEN entries kept, because a record that reports a cleaner result than the harness measured is worse than the defect it hides:** M1, M2, M3, M5, M6, M7, M8, M7b, M9 and M10 RED; **M4 came back GREEN — a hole, not a pass** — and that is the increment's own finding: after the promotion `determ::light::seed_hex_from_source`, `warn_seed_on_command_line` and `zero_secret_string` have NO CALLER inside `determ-light`, so mutating one of them changes nothing; M4 was re-aimed as **M4b** (the `SEED_POLICY` binding both live light entry points share), which IS RED against the UNCHANGED `tools/test_light_seed_source.sh`. **M7 was also GREEN on its first run** — the password fixture carried internal spaces only, so trimming was a no-op on it — and the fixture had to gain LEADING and TRAILING spaces before the gate could see it. **NOT closed:** the raw flags still exist and still work unchanged (removing them is an owner decision; shipped scripts and fixtures pass them), so an operator who keeps using them keeps the exposure. Also NOT closed: the resolved secret is not `determ_secure_zero`d at the eighteen call sites (no worse than the raw path, which never scrubbed either, and the primitive exports `SecretScrub` for a later pass), and the header's `<windows.h>` include — which reaches `src/main.cpp` for the first time — carries a `NOMINMAX` guard that has NOT been executed on Windows, because no MSVC/MinGW toolchain exists on the box this was written on. Also NOT closed and named rather than dropped, each measured at HEAD: `determ-wallet shamir split <secret_hex>` — the LEGACY positional form of the same primitive, still dispatched — takes the secret as a POSITIONAL argument, and the `--<name>-from` convention does not map onto a positional without changing the command grammar; `envelope encrypt --plaintext <hex>` still takes the data being sealed on argv; and `determ-light rpc-auth --secret` is a same-class site in the third binary that S-110 did not cover. | `include/determ/util/secret_source.hpp`, `wallet/main.cpp` argument parsers | done (raw flags: owner) |
| S-115 | 🟠 Partially mitigated | **Medium (local key disclosure — the DAEMON binary; REPRODUCED by execution 2026-09-18 before the fix).** Six `determ` argument parsers took secret material as a command-line ARGUMENT: `account create --passphrase` and `account decrypt --passphrase` (the passphrase that seals / opens the at-rest account keyfile — the S-004 container), and the `--priv` sender key of `submit-param-change`, `submit-merge-event`, `submit-dapp-register` and `submit-dapp-call`. Reproduced by holding a real child in a FIFO-pinned window and reading the passphrase out of its `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`. **🟠 Partially mitigated 2026-09-18 — every one of the six gained a `--<name>-from <file:path or env:NAME or prompt>` twin** on the shared primitive `include/determ/util/secret_source.hpp`, with the same warning, the same both-at-once refusal and the same named refusals and exit 1 as S-110 and S-114. The `DETERM_PASSPHRASE` environment fallback of the two account subcommands is unchanged and is still consulted last. **The three sources are NOT peers, and every operator-facing surface this row touches now says so.** `file:<path>` with restrictive permissions (0600) is the STRONGEST: the value is never in the process table, never in `/proc/<pid>/environ`, and never in the shell history. `prompt` is next. `env:<NAME>` is the WEAKEST of the three — S-110's own row already said the value stays visible in `/proc/<pid>/environ` to the same UID, and, stated here because it was nowhere stated: the obvious way an operator reaches for it, `SECRET=... determ submit-dapp-call --priv-from env:SECRET`, puts the secret back into the shell history file VERBATIM — one of the three leaks this row exists to close. `env:` is worth having only when the variable is set by a parent process, a CI secret store or a systemd credential, never when it is typed on the command line. Gate `tools/test_secret_on_argv.sh` (102 assertions, FAST). **Its section D reads the daemon child's `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` while the kernel says that child is BLOCKED in the `--out` FIFO's `open(2)` — `/proc/<pid>/wchan` naming the FIFO rendezvous — which `account create` reaches only once Argon2id has run and the envelope has been sealed and serialized.** That synchronisation replaced a fixed `sleep 0.1` on 2026-09-18, after review measured 5 runs out of 5 in which the sample landed ~50 ms INSIDE the KDF (`state=R`, `wchan=0`, the `--out` FIFO not yet open) — so the window the gate actually measured was "100 ms after exec", not "after use", and every argv write in the remaining interval was invisible. The proof that this matters is mutant M9, which writes the resolved passphrase into the argv block AFTER the envelope is sealed: **RED against the synchronised gate and GREEN against a byte-for-byte replica of the old `sleep 0.1` one.** What the window still does not cover, stated rather than discovered later: an argv write AFTER that `open(2)` returns — a FIFO can pin a process at the open and nowhere else. **Mutants, GREEN entries kept:** M1, M2, M3, M5, M6, M7, M8, M7b, M9, M10 RED; M4 GREEN (a hole — three `determ::light::` forwarders have no caller after the promotion) and re-aimed as M4b, RED; M7 GREEN on its first run until the password fixture gained leading and trailing spaces. **NOT closed:** the raw flags still exist and still work unchanged. Three same-class sites in this binary are NOT converted and are named rather than dropped. `determ account address <privkey_hex>` and `determ send_anon <to> <amount> <privkey_hex>` take the private key as a POSITIONAL argument — `send_anon` is the one that SIGNS and submits — and the `--<name>-from` convention does not map onto a positional without changing the command grammar, which is a separate increment and would touch every shipped script that calls them. Each `--keyholder-sig <idx>:<priv_hex>` of `submit-param-change` still carries a KEYHOLDER private key on argv — it is one argument holding an index and a key and it can be repeated, so it needs its own list-valued source form. Section D covers three commands; the four `submit-*` parsers are covered by the equivalence, source-refusal and warning sections only, because reaching any window in them needs a live RPC peer. | `include/determ/util/secret_source.hpp`, `src/main.cpp` argument parsers | done (raw flags: owner) |
| S-116 | ⚠ OPEN | **Low/Op (two wallet `--out` emitters put key material on disk with NO framing, and the reference documents one of them as a different format taking flags it rejects; MEASURED 2026-09-18 against the shipped binary, not read off the source).** `keyfile-recover --out` writes the reconstructed secret as RAW BYTES: measured, a 16-byte file whose entire content is `aa bb cc dd ee ff 00 11 22 33 44 55 66 77 88 99` — the plaintext secret and nothing else, no magic, no length, no version, no algorithm tag. Every other container this binary writes carries a four-byte magic and is length- and version-checked by its own decoder (DAK1, DSS1, DNK1, DWE2, DBE1, DRS1), so this one output cannot be told apart from any other blob, cannot be checked for truncation, and carries nothing to say which backup or which curve it came from; an operator who finds it later has no way to know what they are holding. `keyfile-decrypt --out` writes 165 bytes of UNFRAMED JSON beginning `{` + newline + `  "priv_seed":` — no magic either. **PERMISSIONS ARE NOT THE ISSUE HERE:** both outputs go through `write_bytes_file_0600` and were measured at mode 0600, created restricted before their first byte; this row is about what the FILE SAYS ABOUT ITSELF, and it is separate from S-109. **NOT FIXED, and the reason is a named consumer in each case.** Framing `keyfile-decrypt --out` would break the daemon outright: its shape is `src/crypto/keys.cpp`'s `node_key.json` on purpose, the locus comment says so in as many words ("Do NOT binarize this output wallet-side or the daemon can't load its key"), and `load_node_key` reads exactly those two fields. `keyfile-recover --out` has no reader in this repo at all — nothing under `src/`, `wallet/`, `light/` or `tools/` decodes it, its consumer is the operator's own restore procedure — so framing it is mechanically safe but is still an on-disk format change to a shipped command, and it belongs in an increment that fixes its documentation at the same time, because that documentation is wrong today. **The documentation drift, measured and named rather than fixed here:** `docs/CLI-REFERENCE.md:629` documents `keyfile-recover` as taking `--shares` / `--envelopes` / `--passphrases` and as writing `{ed_priv_hex, ed_pub_hex, anon_address}` JSON "atomically (same `_tmp.json` + rename pattern as `keyfile-create` / `keyfile-decrypt`)". Against the shipped binary all four claims are false: the flags are `--backup-shares` / `--backup-envelopes` / `--keyholders` and the three documented ones are rejected with a usage error, the output is raw bytes rather than JSON, and there is no staging file and no rename — the write goes straight to `--out`. An operator following the reference cannot run the command at all. Correcting it means editing a cell of a CRLF file whose content is a documentation increment rather than a write-path one, so it is recorded here with file:line instead of being changed under cover of a permissions fix. **What would close this row:** a framed container for `keyfile-recover --out` (or a recorded decision that raw bytes are the contract) together with a `docs/CLI-REFERENCE.md` row that matches the binary, and a statement for `keyfile-decrypt --out` that its unframed JSON is the daemon's format by contract. | `wallet/main.cpp` `cmd_keyfile_recover` / `cmd_keyfile_decrypt` `--out` emitters; `docs/CLI-REFERENCE.md:629` | open |
| S-117 | ⚠ OPEN | **High (signed-transaction reinterpretation — `signing_bytes` is not injective when `to` may contain NUL; found and reproduced 2026-09-23).** `Transaction::signing_bytes` joins `from` and `to` with single 0x00 separators (`src/chain/block.cpp`), and no C++ rule rejects a NUL byte inside `to`. A signed TRANSFER to `bob` with nonce 0 and a payload starting 0x00 re-reads, under the same signature and hash, as a TRANSFER to `bob\0` moving 256× the amount with 256× the fee; replace-by-fee then prefers the altered copy. Reproduced on a three-node `single_test` chain (the sender lost 258,560 instead of 1,010). REGISTER does not check domain bytes, so under DOMAIN_INCLUSION `bob\0` may be registrable (not tested). The C99 `parser.c` NUL check has no production caller. A NUL-rejection rule briefly shipped inside the reverted D15 commit (2e22a643) without an owner entry. Fix options (owner decision; pre-genesis): reject NUL in `from`/`to` at the verifier with ingress and apply mirrors, or length-prefix the fields when D23 re-lands (see CanonicalSigningBytesParity.md T-6). | `src/chain/block.cpp::Transaction::signing_bytes`, `src/node/validator.cpp::check_transaction` | owner decision |

## 3. Critical findings (open)

### S-001 — RPC authentication missing

**Severity:** Critical • **Status:** Fully mitigated (localhost-only default + HMAC RPC auth both landed) • **Sources:** Audit 1.1, OV-#10

**Option 1 landed in-session.** Config field `rpc_localhost_only` defaults to `true`; the RPC acceptor now binds to `127.0.0.1` rather than `tcp::v4()` (any-interface) unless the operator explicitly sets the field to `false`. Legacy configs without the field get the secure default. External network clients can no longer reach the RPC port via the unauthenticated path that S-001 originally documented. Verified end-to-end in `tools/test_rpc_localhost_only.sh` (5/5 PASS).

**Option 3 landed in-session (HMAC RPC auth).** Config field `rpc_auth_secret` is a hex-encoded shared secret. When non-empty, the RPC server requires every request to carry an `auth` field that's `hex(HMAC-SHA-256(secret, method || "|" || params_canonical_json))`. The server computes the expected HMAC after JSON parse-round-trip (which canonicalizes object key order via nlohmann's deterministic dump) and compares constant-time to avoid timing side-channels. Mismatch → `{"error": "auth_failed"}`. Empty → `{"error": "auth_required: missing 'auth' field"}`.

Client side (CLI + `rpc_call`): if `DETERM_RPC_AUTH_SECRET` env var is set, every outgoing request automatically gets the auth field computed from the env-var secret. No code change needed for existing subcommands.

External-bind-without-auth warning. When `rpc_localhost_only=false` AND `rpc_auth_secret=""` at startup, the node logs `[WARNING: external bind without HMAC auth ...]`. Operators are explicitly nudged toward either keeping localhost-only or setting the auth secret.

**Threat model coverage with v2.16:**
- Network-reachable unauthenticated RPC: closed (option 1)
- Cross-tenant on the same host (multi-user box): closed (option 3 — attacker without secret cannot forge requests)
- Replay of authenticated requests by MITM: NOT addressed in v2.16. Replay protection (per-request nonce + sequence) is a follow-on; S-001's primary issue (unauthenticated requests) is fully closed.
- Secret distribution: operator's responsibility (env var, sealed config, secrets manager). The protocol's job ends at "supports shared-secret auth."

**Regression coverage:** `tools/test_rpc_hmac_auth.sh`, 5 assertions: unauth call when auth disabled, unauth call rejected when auth enabled, wrong-secret rejected, correct-secret authenticates, malformed-hex secret yields clear error.

**Original finding text** (preserved for audit-trail continuity). The RPC server (`src/rpc/rpc.cpp:13`) constructs its acceptor with `tcp::v4()` — bound to all IPv4 interfaces, port-only. Dispatch (`rpc.cpp:52-89`) executes `submit_tx`, `register`, `stake`, `unstake`, `submit_equivocation`, `snapshot`, `account` query, and so on with no authentication, no TLS, no rate limit, and no localhost-only restriction by default.

**Impact.** Any network-reachable client can:
- Submit transactions debiting the node's domain balance (with the node's own key implicitly via `rpc_send`)
- Register the node's domain or alter its stake
- Trigger `submit_equivocation` floods
- Pull a full state snapshot

**Resolution options.**

| # | Option | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Bind RPC to `127.0.0.1` by default**; require explicit config to expose externally. | Trivial. Constructor change in `rpc.cpp:13` to use `make_address("127.0.0.1")`. |
| 2 | **Per-method token bucket** in dispatch. Defaults: `snapshot` 1/min, `submit_tx`/`submit_equivocation` capped, query methods uncapped. | ~50 LOC. |
| 3 | **HMAC token in request envelope.** Client sends `{method, params, hmac}` where hmac is `HMAC-SHA256(shared_secret, canonical_request)`. Shared secret is config-pinned. | 1-2d. |
| 4 | **Mutual TLS** for any externally-exposed RPC. | 3d. Asio TLS layer + cert management. |

**Recommended.** Options 1 + 2 immediately (~1h total). Options 3 or 4 only if RPC must be exposed beyond loopback.

**Light-client trust model (determ-light).** The `determ-light` binary does NOT require the daemon to have HMAC RPC auth or rate limiting enabled to be safe. The operator's daemon is the data-serving party; the light-client is the verifier. Every fetched datum (headers, state-proofs, account replies) is verified locally against (a) the committee-signed `state_root`, (b) the chain-of-hashes from the pinned genesis, (c) Ed25519 sigs from the genesis-derived committee seed. Even a malicious daemon cannot mislead an honest light-client under the honest-majority-of-committee assumption — the worst the daemon can do is refuse to serve data or serve data that fails verification (which the light-client rejects with a clear diagnostic). The security property is *operational-equivalence with full-node operation* under that assumption. This is orthogonal to S-001: enabling HMAC auth on the daemon hardens against unauthenticated tx submission and state mutation; the light-client read path is already safe by construction.

---

### S-002 — Mempool accepts unverified-signature transactions

**Severity:** Critical • **Status:** Mitigated in-session (paired with binary-codec amount/fee/nonce fix) • **Sources:** Audit 1.4

**Mitigation landed in-session.** Both `Node::on_tx` (gossip-path admission) and `Node::rpc_submit_tx` (client RPC admission) now call `Node::verify_tx_signature_locked()` before accepting a tx into `tx_store_`. The helper mirrors `BlockValidator::check_transactions`'s per-tx signature check: it derives the sender's Ed25519 pubkey based on `tx.type` (REGISTER → payload bytes 0..31; anon TRANSFER → bearer address parse; otherwise → registry lookup) and verifies `tx.sig` over `tx.signing_bytes()`. On failure: gossip path silently drops (a forged-sig flood from any peer cannot amplify), RPC path throws with a diagnostic so the submitting client can retry.

This closure required a paired fix to `src/net/binary_codec.cpp::decode_tx_frame`: pre-fix, the decoder skipped the fixed-slot area at offsets 32–55 where the encoder writes `amount`, `fee`, and `nonce`, producing post-decode txs with zero values for these fields. The fields are now read explicitly, restoring the encode/decode round-trip property. The bug was latent because S-002's openness meant zero-field txs entered the mempool and were filtered later at apply (silent corruption). Closing S-002 surfaced the codec bug; both fix together. See `docs/proofs/S002-Mempool-Sig-Verify.md` for the full analysis trail.

**Verified post-fix:** bearer (anon TRANSFER) + governance (registered-domain PARAM_CHANGE) + equivocation_slashing regressions all PASS. The legit-tx code path through both admission sites (RPC and gossip) is exercised by existing tests; a dedicated forged-sig rejection regression is a follow-on once test-side Ed25519 forge tooling is available (currently requires PyNaCl not present in the test environment, or a `determ` CLI helper for offline forged-sig construction).

**Pre-fix description** (preserved for audit trail). `Node::on_tx` (`src/node/node.cpp:1353-1371`) accepted incoming transactions into `tx_store_` after only a stale-nonce check and a replace-by-fee check. **No `crypto::verify` was called** on this path. Signature verification happened only later in `BlockValidator::check_transactions` at apply time.

**Impact.**
- Mempool flood with valid-shape, invalid-signature transactions.
- Each fake tx is stored, gossiped, indexed by `(from, nonce)`, and iterated during block construction.
- Memory exhaustion + bandwidth amplification + producer time wasted resolving fake hashes.

**Resolution options.**

| # | Option | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Verify Ed25519 sig in `on_tx`** before insertion. For bearer addresses derive pubkey from the address; for registered domains lookup in `registry_`. | 1-2d. Hot path; benchmark. |
| 2 | **Verify hash + sig** as a cheap pre-filter (recompute `compute_hash()`, then verify). Same as `rpc_submit_tx` already does. | Subset of #1. |
| 3 | **Bounded mempool with sig-verify-on-eviction-attempt** (verify lazily but bound size first). | Cheaper at hot-path cost; still leaves the iteration risk. |

**Recommended.** Option 1. Same model `rpc_submit_tx` already uses — recomputes `tx.compute_hash()` and rejects mismatches with diagnostic "submitted tx hash mismatch" (grep `src/node/node.cpp::rpc_submit_tx`; current source at ~L2952). Aligning `on_tx` is mostly copy-paste.

---

### S-003 — Block timestamp window ±5s contradicts spec

**Severity:** Critical (for liveness) • **Status:** Mitigated in-session • **Sources:** Audit 1.2

**Mitigation landed in-session.** `BlockValidator::check_timestamp` window widened from ±5s to ±30s, matching the spec text (README §8 and PROTOCOL.md §4). The change is a one-line constant adjustment in `src/node/validator.cpp::check_timestamp`. PROTOCOL.md §4's inline comment on the `timestamp` field updated to "±30s window (S-003)". Spec, code, and documentation now agree on the canonical window.

The choice was option 1 from the resolution table below (the audit's recommended path). Option 2 (median-of-last-N-blocks, Bitcoin-style) is the v2 answer if drift becomes a measurement concern; today's wall-clock check is a sanity bound, not a consensus-defining property.

**Pre-fix description** (preserved for audit trail). `BlockValidator::check_timestamp` at `src/node/validator.cpp:559-564` rejected any block with `|b.timestamp - now()| > 5`. The README spec text referenced ±30s. Under normal cross-region NTP drift, legitimate blocks got rejected as out-of-window.

**Impact (pre-fix).** False-positive aborts → suspension → cascading liveness failure on globally-distributed deployments.

**Resolution options.**

| # | Option | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Widen window to ±30s** to match the spec. | One-line change. |
| 2 | **Median-of-last-N timestamps** (Bitcoin-style) instead of wall-clock comparison. | Medium. Adds chain history dependency to validator. |
| 3 | **Drop wall-clock check entirely**, rely on round timing for ordering. | Trivial. Loses the timestamp-as-monotonicity-signal. |

**Recommended.** Option 1 immediately + update PROTOCOL.md to nail the canonical window. Option 2 is the v2 answer.

---

### S-004 — Plaintext private key in `account create` output

**Severity:** Critical • **Status:** Fully mitigated (option 1 + option 2 both landed) • **Sources:** Audit 1.3

**Option 1 mitigation landed in-session.** `cmd_account_create` (`src/main.cpp`):

- **Refuses stdout output by default.** `determ account create` without `--out` exits 1 with a diagnostic naming the two acceptable paths (file output or explicit opt-in for legacy plaintext-stdout).
- **Requires `--out <file>`** for normal usage. The output file is written then immediately narrowed to owner read+write only via `std::filesystem::permissions(perms::owner_read | perms::owner_write, perm_options::replace)`. On Unix this is `chmod 0600`; on Windows the implementation does a best-effort owner-only ACL.
- **Opt-in `--allow-plaintext-stdout`** for the legacy stdout behavior (offline air-gapped key gen, controlled-shell scenarios). The flag's name makes the choice auditable in invoking scripts.

Test infrastructure updated: `tools/test_bearer.sh` and `tools/test_adversarial.sh` switched from `account create > file` to `account create --out file` (same effect, secure default).

**Option 2 mitigation landed in-session.** `account create --passphrase <pw>` (or `DETERM_PASSPHRASE` env var) wraps the keyfile in an AES-256-GCM envelope (16-byte salt, 96-bit nonce). The encrypted file format is a header line (`DETERM-ACCOUNT-V1 <address>`) followed by the canonical envelope blob. AAD binds the public address so a tampered envelope cannot be substituted with another account's encrypted blob. **(2026-08-12, D2 step 3):** the envelope's canonical form is now the **binary `DWE` container** and line 2 of this file is a single lowercase-hex rendering of those bytes — the old dot-separated field form is gone (pre-genesis, no shims; covered by `tools/test_account_encrypted.sh`). `DETERM-ACCOUNT-V1` itself is `src/`-owned and stays a two-line text file until the src-side D2 keyfile increment — an explicitly recorded D2 remainder, not an oversight (`docs/proofs/DECISION-LOG.md` 2026-08-12 §3).

**R58 KDF hardening (2026-07-04).** Fresh envelopes now key the AES-256-GCM AEAD via memory-hard **Argon2id** (RFC 9106; defaults `t_cost=3`, `m_cost=64 MiB`, `p=1` — above the OWASP Argon2id floor) instead of PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA-256. Argon2id forces an attacker to materialize `m_cost` memory per guess, defeating the cheap GPU/ASIC parallelism that pure-compute PBKDF2 admits — strictly raising the offline dictionary-attack cost at equal unlock latency. The change is a **versioned, back-compatible** wire migration: a 4-byte magic distinguishes `DWE2` (Argon2id) from `DWE1` (legacy PBKDF2), `decrypt` auto-selects the KDF, and every `DWE1` envelope already on disk still decrypts byte-for-byte (an unknown magic fails closed). Legacy keyfiles upgrade opportunistically on the next `keyfile-reencrypt`. The AEAD leg is unchanged, so the confidentiality/integrity theorems carry over verbatim; only the passphrase work-factor bound improves. Proof: `docs/proofs/KeyfileArgon2Migration.md` (KM-1..KM-5), composing `EnvelopeKeyfileCrypto.md` (KE-1..KE-4).

**D2 step 3 — the at-rest containers are canonical binary (2026-08-12).** The wallet/light at-rest estate was inventoried and the "already binary" assumption proved FALSE byte-wise: the `DWE` envelope serialized as dot-separated hex TEXT and the `DETERM-NODE-V1` keyfile was a two-line ASCII file whose *encrypted plaintext was itself JSON*. Seven containers shipped — `DWE` bytes, `DAK1`, `DAB1`, `DNK1`, `DSS1`, `DBE1`, `DRS1` (+ `DLS1` for the light anchor cache) — all magic-prefixed, explicit-LE, length-prefixed, EXACT-length on decode, refuse-never-clamp. Security-relevant consequences: (a) `DNK1` binds the **raw 32-byte pubkey as AAD** and stores the **raw 32-byte seed** as plaintext, so there is no inner JSON parser reachable from decrypted attacker-influenced bytes; (b) `DAK1`/`DNK1` verify **pubkey-derived-from-seed equality** on decode — a stronger, key-material-level check than the string address cross-check it replaces (see S-028); (c) `DSS1`/`DBE1` enforce DISTINCT share indices on decode; (d) the R58 KDF-cost caps are enforced inside `deserialize_bytes` **before any KDF runs**, so the unbounded-work DoS fix survives the rewrite. Gates: `determ-wallet selftest-envelope-bytes` (21), `selftest-keyfile-binary` (19), `selftest-backup-binary` (19), `determ-light state --selftest` (8) — each mutant-verified. Record: `docs/proofs/DECISION-LOG.md` 2026-08-12 §3.

The envelope crypto (`wallet/envelope.cpp`, originally for the wallet binary's recovery-share encryption) is now also linked into the main `determ` binary. The cross-binary scope is limited to the symmetric-crypto primitive — no wallet-state (recovery shares, decrypted seed material) crosses the boundary; the daemon's address space isolation from wallet-secret material is preserved.

Read-back: `determ account decrypt --in <file> --passphrase <pw>` (or `DETERM_PASSPHRASE` env var) decrypts and emits the plaintext JSON (privkey + address) to stdout. AEAD tag verification fails clean (clear error message) on wrong passphrase or tampered file.

**Regression coverage:** `tools/test_account_encrypted.sh`, 7 assertions: header format, no-plaintext-leak in encrypted file, correct-passphrase round-trip, wrong-passphrase rejection, env-var auth, plaintext-path backward compat, decrypted-address well-formedness.

**Pre-fix description** (preserved for audit trail). `cmd_account_create` in `src/main.cpp` emitted the raw `priv_seed` either to stdout or to an unencrypted file, with no `chmod`, no passphrase prompt, no warnings beyond a string in the JSON.

**Impact.** Standard key-leak vectors: terminal scrollback, shell history, world-readable file, accidental commit, accidental log capture.

**Resolution options.**

| # | Option | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Refuse to write to stdout.** Require `--out`. Set `0600` on the output file (Windows: deny inheritable read for non-owner). | Trivial. |
| 2 | **Encrypt with passphrase** (libsodium `crypto_secretbox_easy` or OpenSSL EVP). Prompt for passphrase interactively. | 1-2d. |
| 3 | **Hardware-wallet integration** (BIP32 derivation from a hardware seed). | Out of scope for v1. |

**Recommended.** Option 1 immediately. Option 2 for v1.x. Document key-handling in `docs/QUICKSTART.md`.
