- `src/chain/genesis.cpp:172, 193` — guarded by `j.contains("shard_address_salt")` / iterating `param_keyholders` array; the iterator context already names the surrounding field.

**Resolution options** (historical — Option 1 selected and shipped).

| # | Option | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Use `json_require<T>` / `json_require_hex` everywhere** with sensible defaults via `j.value()` for optional fields. Explicit `j.contains()` checks for required fields. | Shipped in-session. |
| 2 | **JSON Schema validator library** (`nlohmann::json_schema_validator`). | Medium. New dependency. Rejected — Option 1 is sufficient. |

---

### S-019 — Phase-2 timer R-arrival spoofing

**Severity:** Medium (was) • **Status:** ✅ Moot — see M-F. With delay-hash removed, `R` is computed instantly from local Phase-1 inputs; there's no expensive computation an attacker could spoof completion of. The piggyback optimization that depended on peer-`R`-arrival timing is no-op. • **Sources:** OV-#4, Gemini analysis

**What's open.** The Gemini analysis cites `min(local_delay_done_time, peer_R_arrival_time) + block_sig_ms` as the Phase-2 trigger formula and warns of forged-R injection. The current code may differ — needs an audit pass.

**Resolution options.**

| # | Option | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | **Audit first.** Determine whether the formula is still in the consensus event loop. | Free. Required first step. |
| 1 | **Cryptographically pre-validate `R`** before allowing it to influence the timer. | Low. |
| 2 | **Local-clock only.** Drop `peer_R_arrival_time`. Timer = `local_delay_done_time + block_sig_ms`. | Trivial. Loses the latency optimization. |
| 4 | **Bind `cumulative_rand` + current epoch's timestamp into the timer-trigger condition.** The Phase-2 timer fires only when an arrived `R` produces a `block_digest` whose creator-sig verifies against the *current* `cumulative_rand` AND the current second-bin. Adversaries can't prefabricate a future-`R` because `cumulative_rand` only crystallizes per round; binding the timestamp forces brute-force attempts to restart every second. | Low. ~50 LOC. Defeats forged-R injection without losing the latency optimization. |

**Recommended.** Option 0 → Option 4 (best of both worlds — keeps the optimization, defeats spoofing). ~1 day total.

---

### S-020 — Rejection sampling O(K²) at K/N → 1 — ✅ Mitigated in-session

**Severity:** Medium (was) • **Status:** ✅ Mitigated • **Sources:** OV-#3, Gemini analysis

**What changed.** `crypto/random.cpp::select_m_creators` and `select_after_abort_m` now branch on `2K vs N`:
- **`2K ≤ N`** — unchanged rejection sampling. Cheap, no allocation, expected O(K) hashes, and preserves rev.9 output for the K/N ≤ 0.5 regime so existing committee-index fixtures stay stable.
- **`2K > N`** — partial Fisher-Yates shuffle over an `[0..N)` index array. O(N) setup + exactly K hashes + K swaps, with no rejection spin. The worst case (K = N or K = N−1) is now bounded; rejection sampling on the same input expected ~N tries for the final pick.

`select_after_abort_m` pins `new_first` at slot 0 of the shuffle buffer (the abort-hash offset is part of the consensus contract) and Fisher-Yates the remaining `m−1` positions.

**Determinism.** Both `K` and `N` are inputs to the function; every node picks the same branch and the same indices. No fork-height management needed because no chain history sits on the K > N/2 path under the current regression suite: most tests use `single_test` (M=K=3, N_registered ≤ 3) which trips the partial-FY branch via `2K > N` when only 3 validators register; `web` / `web_test` (M=4 K=3) and `cluster_test` (M=K=3) similarly run with small N. Tests with explicitly larger pools (`regional_test` M=5 K=4, `global_test` M=7 K=5) exercise both branches depending on how many validators register at boot. All branches share the same SHA-256-derived randomness so committee outputs are deterministic across nodes regardless of which branch fired.

**Effort.** ~40 LOC including comments. Header doc updated in `include/determ/crypto/random.hpp`.

---

### S-034 — VDF allocates `EVP_MD_CTX` per iteration

**Severity:** Medium (was) • **Status:** ✅ Moot — see M-F. With the iteration removed, there's no inner loop allocating contexts. • **Sources:** Architectural Analysis §3.6

**What's open.** Nothing — the iteration was removed (M-F). The former `delay_hash_compute` (in the since-deleted `src/crypto/delay_hash.cpp`) looked like:
```cpp
Hash delay_hash_compute(const Hash& seed, uint64_t T) {
    Hash cur = seed;
    for (uint64_t i = 0; i < T; ++i) {
        cur = sha256(cur);   // each call: new SHA256Builder, new EVP_MD_CTX
    }
    return cur;
}
```

Each `sha256(cur)` constructs a `SHA256Builder` — which `new`s an `Impl` and calls `EVP_MD_CTX_new()` (heap allocation + OpenSSL context setup) — appends, finalizes, then destroys it. For T = 4,000,000, that's 4 million heap allocations and 4 million `EVP_DigestInit_ex` calls. The actual SHA-256 work is a tiny fraction of the wall-clock cost.

A reusable `EVP_MD_CTX` inside the inner loop, calling `EVP_DigestInit_ex` once and reusing it via `EVP_MD_CTX_reset`, is a roughly **100×-1000× speedup**.

**Why this matters beyond performance.** `delay_T` is calibrated to a specific consumer-CPU wall-clock target (`T_delay ≥ 2 × T_phase1`). The current implementation's wall-clock cost is dominated by allocation/setup overhead, not SHA-256 work. **A more efficient implementation would dramatically reduce the wall-clock cost**, breaking the calibration unless `T` is bumped proportionally. So this is also a security-relevant calibration risk: if some node operators run an optimized build and others don't, the network desynchronizes.

Compounds with S-031 because this 4M-iteration loop runs under `state_mutex_` on the piggyback path.

**Resolution options.**

| # | Option | Cost |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Reuse `EVP_MD_CTX`** inside the loop with `EVP_MD_CTX_reset` between iterations. | ~30 LOC. Requires bumping `delay_T` ~100-1000× to maintain the same wall-clock target. |
| 2 | **Direct SHA-256 block compression.** Skip OpenSSL's EVP layer entirely; call the SHA-256 transform on a 32-byte block in a tight loop. | Medium. ~50 LOC + understanding the transform interface. Fastest possible CPU implementation. |
| 3 | **Bench-and-recalibrate** before fix. Document the new per-iteration cost; coordinate a `delay_T` bump alongside the optimization. | 1d benchmark. |

**Recommended.** Options 1 + 3 together. The fix without recalibration would silently weaken the protocol's selective-abort defense (S-009 already weakens it; this would compound).

### S-110 — determ-light takes private seeds as command-line arguments — 🟠 Partially mitigated

**Severity:** Medium (local key disclosure; client tooling, no consensus path) • **Status:** 🟠 Partially mitigated 2026-09-17 — the safe path ships and the unsafe one is warned about but NOT removed.

**The defect, reproduced before the fix (2026-09-17, executed).** `determ-light` accepted every secret seed as a command-line ARGUMENT:

- `pq-sign-tx --mldsa-seed <hex32> [--ed-seed <hex32>]` — the ML-DSA (and hybrid Ed25519) signing seed
- `pq-address --mldsa-seed <hex32>` and `pq-transfer --mldsa-seed <hex32>` — the same seed
- `build-shield --blind-seed <hex>` and `build-unshield --blind-seed <hex>` — the note blinding seed (reuse or disclosure leaks the amount)

Three separate readers get it. While the command runs, `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` hands the full argument vector to **any local process**; `ps -ww -o args=` prints the same line to **every user on the host**; and the operator's interactive shell appends the command **verbatim to the history file**, where it persists after the process is gone. The reproduction started a `pq-sign-tx` whose `--out` was a FIFO (so it blocked in `open(2)` after signing), read the seed out of `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` and out of `ps` from a second process, showed the same seed in a `$HISTFILE`, and then rebuilt a valid `PQ_TRANSFER` from the leaked seed: for an ML-DSA seed the hex string IS the private key, so the reader signs as that identity.

**What shipped (additive only).** Every one of those flags gained a `--*-seed-from <source>` twin — `--mldsa-seed-from`, `--ed-seed-from`, `--blind-seed-from` — taking `file:<path>` (first line, whitespace-trimmed), `env:<NAME>`, or `prompt` (stdin with terminal echo disabled). This deliberately MIRRORS `determ-wallet keyfile-create --passphrase-from` (`wallet/main.cpp::passphrase_from_source`) rather than inventing a second convention: same three source forms, same diagnostic shape, same exit code 1 on a bad source. That helper lives in the wallet binary's own translation unit and is not linked into `determ-light`, so it is re-implemented in `light/seed_source.cpp` instead of dragging a dependency across the binary boundary. Resolved seed buffers (the hex string, the decoded 32 bytes, the blinding vector) are `determ_secure_zero`d on every exit path including the throwing ones, matching `light/keyfile.cpp`'s existing discipline.

**What this does NOT close.** The raw `--mldsa-seed` / `--ed-seed` / `--blind-seed` flags still exist and behave exactly as before — same accepted inputs, same output bytes, same exit codes. Removing them is an owner decision, not a track's: `tools/test_light_pq_sign.sh`, `tools/test_pq_transfer_e2e.sh` and `tools/test_light_ct_tx.sh` all drive them, and they are the regression lock that proves the old form is unchanged. An operator who keeps typing the raw flag keeps the full exposure. The only behavioural change on that path is a named `WARNING[seed-on-command-line]` line on stderr naming the process table, the shell history file and the `-from` alternative — a warning, not a refusal, and the exit code is unchanged. Passing both forms of one seed is refused (exit 1) rather than silently preferring one. `env:` is better than argv but not perfect: the value is still visible in `/proc/<pid>/environ` to the same UID, so `file:` with restrictive permissions is the strongest of the three.

**Gate.** `tools/test_light_seed_source.sh` (FAST), 28 assertions in five groups: (A) `--mldsa-seed-from file:` yields BYTE-IDENTICAL output to `--mldsa-seed` for `pq-address`, `pq-transfer` and hybrid `pq-sign-tx`, and the `-from`-signed tx verifies; (B) `env:` and `prompt` agree with the raw form, and a missing file, an empty file, an unset variable, an unknown source scheme, both-forms-at-once and a malformed (odd / short) hex value are each refused with a named diagnostic and exit 1; (C) the raw form emits the named warning and still exits 0, while the `-from` form writes nothing to stderr; (D) **the property itself, read from the kernel** — with `--mldsa-seed-from` the seed is ABSENT from the running child's `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` in a window that is strictly AFTER the seed has been read and used (the source is an ordinary file and the `--out` path is a FIFO, which `pq-sign-tx` opens only once the envelope is signed), while the control shows the raw flag DOES put it there in the SAME window; a third check covers `pq-address` in the pre-read window. The wait is on `/proc/<pid>/comm`, which no argv edit can forge, and the section prints `SKIP:` by name on non-Linux platforms or where `/proc/self/cmdline` is unreadable. The post-read window is load-bearing: sampling BEFORE the read left the "write the resolved seed back into argv" mutant GREEN when this gate was first written; (E) `--blind-seed-from` round-trips byte-identically for `build-shield` / `build-unshield` and the raw form warns. Five mutants, each alone and each RED against the rebuilt binary: `file:` silently falling back to raw hex; the warning dropped; a short/odd hex seed accepted; `env:` reading a fixed wrong variable; the `-from` path writing the resolved seed back into the process's own argv.

**Platform evidence (2026-09-22).** The `/proc`/FIFO observations in the S-110, S-114 and S-115 gates require Linux and readable `/proc/self/cmdline`. Other platforms report section D as a partial skip while retaining the functional source, refusal, equivalence and warning checks. An emulated `/proc` directory does not establish Linux kernel semantics for a native Windows executable; no Windows process-table secrecy proof is claimed.

**Not in scope at the time, and CLOSED on 2026-09-18 by S-114 / S-115 (kept here so the trail is readable).** `determ-wallet keyfile-create --priv <hex>` and `determ-wallet account-import --priv <hex>` took an Ed25519 private seed on the command line with the same three readers — the wallet binary is a separate surface and is NOT changed here. `determ-light verify-selection-offline --seed-hex <hex>` is the PUBLIC `cumulative_rand` beacon seed, not secret, and is left alone.

---

### S-035 — No unit tests, no CI, no deterministic simulation framework — 🟠 Partially mitigated

**Severity:** Operational • **Status:** 🟠 Partially mitigated (Option 3 path portability shipped in-session; Option 1 seeded with 155+ in-process `determ test-*` subcommands covering every cryptographic foundation; Option 2 implemented, its production dependency injection outstanding; CI committed 2026-07-03) • **Sources:** Architectural Analysis §4.1, §4.2

**Path portability shipped in-session (Option 3, ~1d).** Every test under `tools/test_*.sh` now sources `tools/common.sh` which detects the determ + determ-wallet binaries across platforms (Windows MSVC multi-config `build/Release/determ.exe`, Linux/Mac single-config `build/determ`, etc.) and resolves `PROJECT_ROOT` to a Windows-style absolute path on Git Bash via `pwd -W` (with `cygpath -m` fallback for Cygwin). Hard-coded `C:/sauromatae/...` references in test scripts are replaced with `$PROJECT_ROOT/...`; hard-coded `build/Release/determ.exe` is replaced with `$DETERM`. Override hooks: set `DETERM_BIN=/path/to/determ` or `DETERM_WALLET_BIN=/path/to/determ-wallet` to use a custom build layout (CI runners can point at any installed binary without editing tests).

| `determ test-block-binary-codec` | Canonical binary Block container — `Block::encode_frame` / `Block::decode_frame` (D2-inc5, commits `d33d410` + `76d7ea4`). Ships ALONGSIDE `to_json` / `from_json`; **no call site uses it yet** — the p2p wire (BLOCK / CHAIN_RESPONSE / BEACON_HEADER / SHARD_TIP / CROSS_SHARD_RECEIPT_BUNDLE) and chain storage switch over in later D2 increments, so this gate is currently the only thing standing between the container and a silent defect. THE THEOREM (BF-1) is INFORMATION EQUIVALENCE with the JSON container — `decode_frame(encode_frame(b))` == `from_json(to_json(b))` as JSON, on a block exercising all 37 fields — not field-for-field: `to_json` deliberately discards the six-key `creator_view_*` bundle under all-zero roots (and the shard-tip pair under its own gate) and `source_shard_id` under `eligible_count == 0`, and since neither is covered by `signing_bytes` or `compute_block_digest` a faithful frame would carry a relayer's injection and let the validator reject an otherwise-valid block — a remotely-triggerable rejection, i.e. a censorship / liveness vector. BF-10 (non-empty view LIST under all-zero roots) + BF-11 (`source_shard_id` under `eligible_count == 0`) pin that BOTH containers drop it. Also: BF-0 an empty frame is exactly 297 bytes (pins `kMinBlockFrame`, from which the Layer-1 witness cap is derived — caught a real double-counted u32 length prefix that made the cap reject legitimate one-witness frames); BF-2 `encode(decode(x)) == x` byte-for-byte; BF-3 `signing_bytes` + `compute_hash` unchanged across the round trip; BF-4 an all-default block resurrects none of the nine gated keys; BF-5 trailing-byte rejection (exact consumption); BF-6 every proper prefix rejected (2783 truncations, 0 accepted); BF-7 a 65535 count backed by 10 bytes rejected before any reserve; BF-8a/8b witness depth (one level decodes, a nested witness rejected `'must be a leaf block'`); **BF-12a/12b HOSTILE BYTES — 12,248 adversarial inputs** (every single-byte corruption of a valid frame × 3 XOR deltas, 0xFFFF stamps over the header region, 3000 pure-garbage buffers, 500 valid frames with hostile tails; measured 8315 decoded / 3933 rejected, zero crashes, ~0.6s, fixed PRNG seed so it is reproducible). Added after the mutant pass showed an encoder emitting one section FEWER than the decoder reads does not reject cleanly — it fail-fasts the process; BF-6 only truncates a VALID frame, whereas BF-12 corrupts counts and length prefixes IN PLACE, which is what desynchronises a parse. **RESIDUAL, stated by the gate itself:** BF-7 is STRUCTURAL, not measured — a mutant moving the cap after the reserve stays green, verified by building and running that mutant rather than assumed. Tolerable only because the u16 count width bounds the worst-case over-reserve to 65535 × 64 B ≈ 4 MB, which is also the BLOCK wire cap, so check placement is a performance question and not the unbounded WIRE-1 amplification class (16 MB → 831 MB, 51.9×). Had the counts been u32 this leg would HAVE to count allocations. | D2 / block container | 14 |

Falsify-on-mutant: deleting the clause reddens exactly the 6 rejection arms and leaves every acceptance arm green; narrowing the multiply to uint32 reddens exactly the no-wrap arm. | S-054 / consensus safety | 16 |

| `determ-dsso selftest-pid` | DSSO as a **wallet-relying party** (Reg. (EU) 2024/1183 Art. 5b): an EUDI Wallet **PID presentation** in **SD-JWT VC** form is verified and then bound to an account. 156 assertions. Nine separately-testable rules, each with an accept case and its own rejections — bounded base64url (canonical, unpadded) and bounded JSON (duplicate member names REJECTED, depth and element caps, no trailing data); the issuer key comes from a configured **PID Provider trust anchor list** and never from the token, with `jwk`/`jku`/`x5u`/`x5c`/`crit` headers refused (ARF OIA_12); ES256 over the wire signing input, with the `alg` pin checked BEFORE the signature is decoded so `alg: none` is refused on the algorithm; selective disclosure against `_sd` with duplicate, unbacked, reserved-name and shadowing disclosures rejected; holder binding by a KB-JWT under the credential's `cnf` key whose `sd_hash` covers exactly the presented material (ARF OIA_02); audience; the nonce DSSO issued for THIS request; freshness with bounded skew and age; **IETF Token Status List** trust-anchored and **FAIL-CLOSED** five ways; and assurance capped by the anchor. Plus the account-binding rules — a DSSO session ALONE never authorises binding another person's identity, no silent double-binding or re-binding without an explicit unbind, single-use TTL-bounded challenge — and a stored pseudonym that is `HMAC-SHA256(K, iss + id)`, never a raw national identifier. Fixtures come from an INDEPENDENT pure-Python P-256 anchored to the RFC 6979 A.2.5 vectors and cross-checked against OpenSSL; the C verifier is separately anchored to those same vectors. 8400 fuzz mutants over the presentation / JSON / base64url / zlib readers — every presentation mutant that differs is REJECTED. 17 mutants, each RED on its own arm. Wrapper `tools/test_dsso_pid.sh`; proof `docs/proofs/DssoPidVerification.md`. A: the possession primitive — two device seeds give two keys (the seed is load-bearing), the same seed reproduces its key, sign/verify round-trips, and a proof for another challenge / under another key / with a flipped proof or response byte / against a malformed public key is rejected. B: first enrolment is authorised ONLY by a verified PID presentation — fail-closed with no verifier (`DSSO_E_UNAVAILABLE`), refused with no presentation, and a presentation is single-use. C: the honest two-factor login accepted at SUBSTANTIAL; **password-only rejected**; **device-only rejected**; a replayed device response, one bound to another session, and one bound to another server set all rejected; a cluster whose set digest is not the deployment's bound one refused outright; stale and future-dated timestamps rejected; and the §2.2.1 adversary — a device key derived from `pk_dev` + the password-derived server secret + the server-set digest is NOT the enrolled key and CANNOT complete a login, while the honest holder still can. D: a second device is enrolled only with a two-factor session PLUS a fresh proof from the current device (password alone refused; a login-purpose proof refused for an enrolment); adding a device does not invalidate live sessions. E: a STOLEN SESSION TOKEN without the device revokes nothing; revocation succeeds with session + device and every concurrent session STOPS VERIFYING; the revoked device cannot authenticate; the survivor is unaffected. F: the loss/recovery state machine — `RESTORE_DEVICE`/`RESTORE_PASSWORD`/`RESTORE_BOTH` each refused without the second evidence, the downgrade to LOW visible to the assertion layer, a LOW session unable to enrol, a PID for another subject refused, a rejected verdict and an unreachable verifier both failing closed, a device id never recycled, a refusal leaving the account untouched. G: the aggregate limiter — full rotation over all ten 3-subsets yields exactly `floor(cap/t) = 4` attempts then `DSSO_E_RATELIMIT`, every server agrees the aggregate is the cap, another account is untouched, a stale view refuses to serve, a new window resets, a failed login still costs budget, a success clears it set-wide. H: the assurance report the assertion layer consumes — unknown account NONE, unknown/expired session refused, NOTHING reaches above SUBSTANTIAL, and the byte-identical login under two different server-side session secrets yields two different session ids (the bearer token is not a function of the request; an all-zero secret is refused at init). It replaced a fixed `sleep 0.1` on 2026-09-18 that measurably landed inside the daemon's Argon2id (5 runs of 5), and the proof is mutant M9 — an argv write AFTER the seal — which is RED against the synchronised window and GREEN against a replica of the timed one. A write after that `open(2)` returns is still outside the window and the gate says so. `SKIP:` by name on non-Linux platforms, where `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` cannot be read, or where the Linux kernel does not name the FIFO wait; A, B and C still assert there. Each runs with no network, no cluster and no daemon, and none is flaky; the TIER as a whole takes ~2-3 min. This sentence used to read "each runs in <5s" and had not been true for some time — measured 2026-09-18 on this 2-core box with two concurrent builds: `tools/test_light_outbox.sh` 9.4 s and `tools/test_light_outbox_flag_surface.sh` 13.7 s, and `tools/test_wallet_out_perms.sh` 7.5 s after growing from 50 to 101 assertions over nine commands under `strace` and `LD_PRELOAD`, both spending most of it on the real timing windows they measure (a ~1 s socket timeout per network verb) and on well over a hundred process launches each. The bound the suite enforces is the tier's total, not a per-wrapper one. Wrappers in `tools/test_*.sh`. `FAST=1 bash tools/run_all.sh` runs only this subset (**341 wrappers** in the `ONLY_PATTERN` tier since 2026-09-23) for fast iteration. That figure is DERIVED, not copied: the `ONLY_PATTERN` alternation in `tools/run_all.sh` carries 341 stems, each with a wrapper file (a stem without one now fails the run), and the pattern matches 341 of the 577 `tools/test_*.sh` wrappers on disk. On 2026-09-23 the tier went from 329 to 341: ten cited offline gates joined it, and the D15 and S-097 wrappers left with their reverted increments along with the wrapper-less `dsso_assertion` stem. The figures below are the 2026-09-18 record. It read "299" from 2026-08 until 2026-09-18, by which time it was 26 short. It moved 325 → 327 on 2026-09-18 when `test_light_wait_surface` and `test_light_negative_footing` were repaired and admitted to the tier: both are pure source analysis, need no binary and run sub-second, and both had been RED outside the tier for weeks precisely because nothing ran them; and 327 → 329 later the same day when two wrappers landed together: `test_light_outbox_flag_surface` with S-113, and `test_secret_on_argv` with S-114 / S-115 — the latter needs only the two already-built binaries and runs in a few seconds. **329 is the MEMBERSHIP of the tier, and since 2026-09-18 that is no longer the same figure as the number of wrappers that ASSERTED.** `tools/run_all.sh` has a third per-wrapper outcome, `PLATFORM-SKIP:`, for a wrapper that skipped ENTIRELY because the property it gates does not exist on the platform in hand. It is counted in its own column under the widened self-check `PASS + FAIL + PLATFORM-SKIP == RUN`, it is neither a pass nor a failure, and the suite still exits non-zero only when `FAIL > 0`. On **Linux and macOS that column is 0 and all 329 assert.** On **Windows it is 2**: `tools/test_wallet_out_perms.sh` and `tools/test_node_key_perms.sh` both gate the mode a key file ends at and Windows has no POSIX file mode, so **327 assert there** and the at-rest permissions of wallet key outputs (S-109) and of the node identity seed (S-091) have NO coverage on that platform. The suite now says so by name; before 2026-09-18 it said it by failing the `windows-2022` job against a product that is not broken. The next mechanical extension is to add unit tests for the remaining cryptographic surface — equivocation detection logic (FA6 producer-side — needs NodeRegistry fixture, now partially seeded via `test-node-registry`), bounded mempool (S-008 — needs partial Node fixture), AbortClaimMsg verification path. Then mid-level invariants (abort-tally semantics, fork resolution).

A comprehensive coverage map + extension guide for this seed is in `docs/UNIT-TESTS.md`.

Verified end-to-end with the converted tests:

- `test_bearer.sh` — 3-node gossip TRANSFER round-trip PASS
- `test_zero_trust_cross_chain.sh` — beacon + shard cross-chain PASS
- `test_state_proof.sh` — TABS-using snapshot path PASS (7/7)
- `test_atomic_scope.sh`, `test_s018_json_validation.sh` — in-process tests PASS

The change is mechanical (49 file `sed` + a 25-line `common.sh` helper) and zero-cost on Windows: the existing tests continue to find `build/Release/determ.exe` and resolve `$PROJECT_ROOT = C:/sauromatae` exactly as the prior hard-coded values did. The portability gain is the new ability to run the same scripts on Linux/Mac (binary discovery via `build/determ`, `pwd` returns the native path) and the new `DETERM_BIN` override hook for CI flexibility.

**What's still open.** No unit-test framework (no gtest/Catch2/doctest — the in-binary `test-*` subcommands serve instead), and the DSF's production dependency injection (DSF-SPEC.md §Q1/§Q2: generated scenarios drive a toy SimState, not the real Node/Validator/Producer). Corrected 2026-09-23 — this paragraph said no CI workflow and no simulation framework existed: CI has been committed since 2026-07-03 (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, 3dad221d; jobs linux, windows, c99, ubsan and tla as of 2026-09-23) and the DSF since 2026-07-04 (`sim/`, `determ-dsf`, increments 1-14; the in-process virtual clock and network also drive real Nodes in `test-fa-liveness-virtual` / `test-fa-partition-virtual`).

**Why this is operational, not a vulnerability.** A bug-free codebase doesn't strictly need unit tests, but their absence makes regression-prevention impossible. Edge cases (`ContribMsg` with invalid `aborts_gen` or same-generation duplicate, two same-height blocks in different orders, equivocation under network partition, V12/V13 cross-shard receipt fuzzing, S-022 per-message-type-cap boundary, S-014 token-bucket interactions across HELLO exemption) require targeted unit tests; the integration scripts can't drive them.

**Resolution options.**

| # | Option | Cost | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Add gtest/Catch2** for crypto, serialization, state transitions, validator rules. CMake + Linux CI. | 1-2w to seed + ongoing per-feature. | 🟡 seeded in-session — 155+ in-process `determ test-*` subcommands ship under `src/main.cpp` (covering atomic-scope, composable-batch, dapp-register, dapp-call, s018-json-validation, Merkle, committee-selection, shard-routing, Ed25519, SHA-256, anon-address, genesis-message, state-root, block-rand, rate-limiter, block-digest, block-hash, binary-codec, wire-types, transaction, merge-event-codec, consensus-msgs, tx-root, genesis, envelope, resolve-fork, shamir, random-state, snapshot-defense, encoding, chain-helpers, json-validate, block-roundtrip, config-roundtrip, tx-binary-codec, chain-append, state-types, validator-config, timing-profiles, params-constants, supply-invariant, enum-values, block-accessors, make-block-sig, domain-separation, tx-signing-bytes, merge-event-bytes, make-genesis-block, pending-param-changes, merge-state, chain-apply-block, snapshot-roundtrip, state-proof, abort-event-apply, equivocation-apply, unstake-deregister-apply, cross-shard-receipt-apply, param-change-apply, subsidy-distribution, merge-event-apply, cross-shard-outbound-apply, supply-lifecycle, dapp-state-transition, overflow-paths, state-root-namespaces, multi-tx-block, state-proof-namespaces, applied-receipt-restore, stake-accounting, fee-distribution-edge, equivocation-multi, cross-shard-multi-receipt, cross-shard-supply-invariant, multi-block-chain, tx-edge-cases, snapshot-then-apply, genesis-with-region, anon-routing, merge-event-apply-edge, block-event-composition, nef-pool-drain, tx-payload-bounds, empty-block-apply, account-create-on-credit, randomized-delay, block-timestamp, node-registry, tx-replay-protection, chain-save-load, block-validator-basic, genesis-sharded, cross-shard-atomicity, chain-ctor-bootstrap, snapshot-version-rejection, config-defaults, required-block-sigs, config-load-save, block-from-json-minimal, config-permissive, chain-shard-routing-config surfaces; Option 1 continues incrementally in parallel (add unit tests for new code as it lands; 155+ in-process subcommands already shipped). (2026-09-23: Option 2 and CI have both shipped, as above.)

### S-037 — `dapp_registry` snapshot serialize/restore gap — ✅ Mitigated in-session

**Severity:** Operational (Low) (was) • **Status:** ✅ Mitigated • **Source:** discovered during the PROTOCOL.md coherence sweep + closed in the same session

**Pre-fix description.** `Chain::build_state_leaves` (`src/chain/chain.cpp` ~line 265) emits a `d:` namespace leaf for every entry in `dapp_registry_` — the v2.18 DApp substrate ties the registry into the state Merkle commitment so light clients can prove DApp registration. But `Chain::serialize_state` and `Chain::restore_from_snapshot` did not include the map. Concretely (pre-fix):

1. Snapshot taken on a chain with one or more `DAPP_REGISTER` entries omitted `dapp_registry`.
2. Restore on a fresh node started with `dapp_registry_ = {}`.
3. `dapp-info` / `dapp-list` on the restored chain returned empty even for DApps that were live on the donor.
4. `Chain::compute_state_root()` over the restored state diverges from what the donor computed (the `d:` slice contributes no leaves on the restored side). The S-033 gate at `restore_from_snapshot` rejects the snapshot with `"state_root mismatch"` (now that S-038 also closed in this session, the gate actually fires on production blocks rather than being dormant).

**Why this was operational, not a security regression.** Fail-loud — restore would refuse the snapshot in the full S-033 case. No silent state corruption, no equivocation surface, no funds at risk. The pragmatic harm was "can't bootstrap a DApp-active chain via snapshot" — full chain replay still worked.

**Mitigation shipped.**

1. `snap["dapp_registry"]` emission added to `Chain::serialize_state` immediately after the `merge_state` block. Every field that contributes to the `d:` value-hash in `build_state_leaves` is persisted: `service_pubkey` (32B hex), `endpoint_url`, `topics[]`, `retention` (u8), `metadata` (hex bytes), `registered_at`, `active_from`, `inactive_from`. Plus the map key (`domain`).
2. Symmetric readback in `Chain::restore_from_snapshot` follows the `registrants_` restore pattern: `if (snap.contains("dapp_registry"))` guard ensures pre-v2.18 snapshots (no `dapp_registry` field) still load (the restored chain just gets `dapp_registry_ = {}`, matching the original donor state).
3. Regression test `tools/test_dapp_snapshot.sh` (11/11 PASS) exercises the full surface: 3-node donor chain advances, donor1 registers a DApp, snapshot taken from donor1, donors stopped, fresh receiver starts with `snapshot_path` set + no genesis + no peers. Asserts: (a) snapshot file contains the `dapp_registry` array with 1 entry, (b) receiver boots without `state_root mismatch` error, (c) `dapp-info(donor1)` on the receiver returns the same `endpoint_url`/`topics`/`metadata` as on the donor, (d) `dapp-list` reports the DApp post-restore. Backward-compat verified: existing `tools/test_snapshot_bootstrap.sh` (which produces a no-DApp snapshot) still passes — the guard correctly handles the absent-field case.

**Test coverage delta.** From "disjoint" (snapshot tests skipped DApps; DApp tests skipped snapshots) to "joint" — the new regression closes the specific co-surface that hid this bug.

### S-038 — S-033 state_root verification gate dormant on production blocks — ✅ Mitigated in-session

**Severity:** High (consensus-integrity gap; documented mitigation was non-functional) • **Status:** ✅ Mitigated • **Source:** discovered during the S-037 closure test (snapshot tail-head's `state_root` field was empty in JSON, exposing this latent gap; same session as the closure)

**Pre-fix description.** S-033 mitigation claims to bind the post-apply state Merkle root into the block via `Block.state_root`, with a verification gate in `Chain::apply_transactions` (~chain.cpp:1430) that rejects any block whose stored `state_root` doesn't match the locally-recomputed root. The gate has correct logic but is guarded by `if (b.state_root != zero) verify` — a backward-compat shim that skips the check on pre-S-033 blocks (zero state_root field).

**The latent gap.** `src/node/producer.cpp::build_body` does NOT populate `state_root` on the produced block. `Node::start_block_sig_phase` builds a *tentative* copy and populates state_root on it for the digest dry-run (`compute_block_digest` doesn't include state_root anyway, so this was just consistency), but the FINAL body broadcast by `Node::try_finalize_round` was built afresh via a second `build_body` call and left `state_root = Hash{}` (zero default). Result:

- Every gossiped block had `state_root = 0`.
- The S-033 gate at apply time short-circuited on every block.
- Peer nodes accepted any committee-signed body regardless of its state-after-apply.

This means the S-030 D1 "effective-closed via S-033" claim was actually unenforced in production. The gate's infrastructure was correct; the producer just wasn't feeding it.

**Why this isn't an attack surface today.** The K-of-K committee structure still prevents forks (every member must sign the same digest, and the digest covers the tx set deterministically). Two distinct state outcomes from the same tx set would require nondeterministic apply, which the protocol doesn't allow. So the gate's bypass didn't enable concrete attacks — but it did make the documented S-033 mitigation a no-op, which would silently degrade safety against any future bug introducing apply-time nondeterminism.

**Mitigation shipped.**

`Node::try_finalize_round` now populates `body.state_root` immediately after `build_body` and before `apply_block_locked`:

```cpp
chain::Block body = build_body(...);
body.creator_block_sigs = std::move(ordered_block_sigs);

// S-038 closure
{
    chain::Chain tentative_chain = chain_;
    tentative_chain.append(body);  // state_root still zero, verify short-circuits inside append
    body.state_root = tentative_chain.compute_state_root();
}

apply_block_locked(body);
gossip_.broadcast(net::make_block(body));
```

Notes on safety + compatibility:

- `compute_block_digest` (what committee members sign in Phase 2) **excludes** `state_root` per §4.3 of PROTOCOL.md. So the K-of-K signatures gathered before `try_finalize_round` runs are unaffected by populating the field afterward. No re-signing required.
- `compute_hash` (block_hash, via `signing_bytes`) **includes** `state_root` when non-zero. So the block_hash of post-fix blocks is different from what it would have been pre-fix. But: pre-fix blocks ALL had `state_root = 0`, so their block_hashes computed without the state_root contribution. Post-fix blocks have `state_root` populated and compute their hash WITH the contribution. Each block's hash is internally consistent with its own state_root field — no rolling break.
- Backward-compat: chains with pre-fix blocks already in their history retain their zero-state_root blocks unchanged. The gate skips zero per the existing backward-compat shim. Only blocks produced after the fix carry populated state_root.

**Test.** `tools/test_dapp_snapshot.sh` (12/12 PASS) now strictly verifies that the snapshot tail-head's stored `state_root` matches the receiver's freshly-computed `compute_state_root()` post-restore. Pre-fix, this assertion would have failed because the snapshot tail head's `state_root` field was empty in JSON. Post-fix, the field is populated and the comparison succeeds — proving the gate is wired through end-to-end.

**Effect on S-030 D1/D2.** S-030's apply-layer closure is now actually shipped end-to-end:

- D1 (resolved tx-payload mismatch): two committee members applying different tx sets reach different state_roots → different block_hashes → divergent stored state, but only one matches the locally-recomputed root at any honest peer → fork rejected at apply.
- D2 (non-tx-payload field mismatch): same mechanism — divergent abort_events/equivocation_events/inbound_receipts/etc. produce divergent post-apply state, caught at apply.

The S-033 apply-layer gate (rejection after some peers might have signed the divergent body) is now real and is retained as belt-and-suspenders. On top of it, the v2.7 F2 view reconciliation **shipped** the consensus-layer closure for the three pool-fed dimensions (inbound / equivocation / abort): divergent views now fail to gather signatures (`check_eqabort_reconciliation` + intersection enforcement in `validator.cpp`, digest binding in `producer.cpp`; commits `850d2c3` / `c16d6c3` / `a727cb2` / `48c4b45`). `partner_subset_hash` is bound too (commit `8585a50`), and `timestamp` is now bound via median reconciliation (commit `f99eeb8`, the §5 mechanism) — so D2 is now fully digest-closed (the residual "partial" tag tracks only the apply-layer belt-and-suspenders framing, not an uncovered digest field).

### S-039 — `compute_genesis_hash` does not bind operational params — ✅ Closed

**Severity:** Operational (Low) • **Status:** ✅ **Closed** — the operational params are now bound into `compute_genesis_hash` UNCONDITIONALLY. Shipped pre-launch as a wire-compat break (no installed base to migrate, so the only blocker — coordinated migration — was moot). • **Source:** Discovered while writing `tools/test_genesis.sh` (S-035 Option 1 seed)

**Pre-fix description.** `compute_genesis_hash` produces a 32-byte Hash from `chain_id + chain_role + shard_id + committee_region (when non-empty) + genesis_message (when non-default) + creators' ed_pubs + governance fields (when non-default) + suspension_slash/unstake_delay (when non-default) + merge thresholds (when non-default)`. It does NOT bind:

- `m_creators` (committee size K)
- `k_block_sigs` (Phase-2 quorum within committee)
- `block_subsidy` / `subsidy_pool_initial` / `subsidy_mode` (E1 economics)
- `min_stake`
- `initial_shard_count`
- `bft_enabled` / `bft_escalation_threshold`
- `epoch_blocks` (committee-selection epoch length)
- `shard_address_salt` (sharding routing salt)

**Diagnostic-UX impact.** Two operators running the same `chain_id` with different `m_creators` (or any other unbinded field) get matching `genesis_hash` but cryptic consensus failures: each side computes a different K-committee → signature gathering never converges → chain stalls. Operators see "no progress" rather than "your config doesn't match the chain's."

**Why this isn't a security regression.** The unbinded fields ARE consensus-critical, but the protocol fails LOUD when they diverge — chain stalls until the misconfigured operator notices. No silent state divergence, no equivocation surface, no funds at risk. The gap is purely operator-experience: faster + clearer diagnosis when configs drift.

**Closure (shipped).** `make_genesis_block` now binds all of the above into the genesis hash **unconditionally**, under a `"DTM-genesis-ops-v1"` domain-separator tag, immediately before `cumulative_rand` is finalized (`src/chain/genesis.cpp`). Two operators whose configs differ in any bound field now compute DIFFERENT `genesis_hash` values, so the mismatch is rejected at the HELLO genesis-hash handshake with a clear diagnostic — instead of the pre-fix silent non-convergence (cryptic "no progress" stalls). Shipped as a wire-compat break: pre-launch there is no installed base to migrate, so the only blocker (coordinated migration / flag-day) was moot; binding is unconditional rather than flag-gated for simplicity and to avoid default-value collision edge cases.

**Tests flipped.** The lock-in assertions that pinned the old no-effect behaviour were inverted to assert the new binding: `determ test-genesis-determinism` Scenario 7 (4 assertions — `m_creators` / `block_subsidy`+`subsidy_pool_initial` / `bft_enabled`+`bft_escalation_threshold`+`epoch_blocks` / `shard_address_salt`+`initial_shard_count` now each CHANGE the hash) and `determ test-genesis-sharded` (`initial_shard_count` now bound). **Closure follow-up:** the original `determ test-genesis` lock-in block (six "param NOT in hash" assertions) was MISSED by this flip — it fired exactly as designed when the binding shipped, but the failure was masked by the runner's then PASS-first marker detection and surfaced only when `tools/run_all.sh` flipped to FAIL-first detection. Those six lock-ins are now sensitivity assertions extended to all 12 `DTM-genesis-ops-v1` params (commit 31702c2). A "lock in the current behavior so we notice if it changes" test is only as good as the runner's ability to surface it firing.
