### S-040 — `merkle_verify` does not bind leaf_count — ✅ Closed

**Severity:** Operational (Low-Medium) • **Status:** ✅ **Closed** — `leaf_count` is now bound into the committed root via a root-wrapper hash, so a forged count is cryptographically rejected. Shipped pre-launch as a wire-compat break (no installed base to migrate; every `state_root` value changes). • **Source:** R21A5 audit while writing `test-merkle-proof-tampering`

**Pre-fix description.** `crypto::merkle_verify` accepts `leaf_count` as an input parameter but does NOT bind it into the leaf or inner hash. Two distinct `(target_index, leaf_count)` pairs that produce the same walk-shape (same `ceil(log2(N))` level count + same per-level parity given target_index) share an identical proof structure. Concrete failure case pinned by `test-merkle-proof-tampering` scenario #8: claiming `leaf_count=8` for a 5-leaf tree at index 2 verifies as TRUE because both yield identical 3-level walks consuming the same 3 siblings in the same left/right order.

**Why this is structurally a caller-trust invariant, not a hash-binding flaw.** The Merkle scheme commits to leaves + tree shape via `merkle_root`; the same shape with different leaves produces different roots. The gap is that `leaf_count` is metadata passed to verify, not data bound into the hash chain. A verifier-with-trusted-leaf_count is sound (the on-chain `state_proof` RPC returns leaf_count from canonical state — so a light-client verifying against a single trusted daemon is safe). A verifier with leaf_count from an untrusted source AND proof from another untrusted source can be tricked into accepting a proof against a different tree shape.

**Adversary model.** A v2.2 light-client fetches: (1) a state_root from a trusted committee-signed header; (2) a state-proof from an untrusted CDN / cache / relay; (3) a leaf_count from the same untrusted source. The attacker craft a proof + leaf_count pair that share walk-shape with the canonical (leaf_count, target_index) but claim a different (key, value_hash). T-040 holds against the proof-only attack (the canonical leaves are fixed; tamper detected at the leaf hash), but the attacker can pick leaf_count to make THEIR fake leaves walk-shape match.

**Closure (shipped) — root-wrapper binding.** Rather than the three originally-sketched paths (per-leaf / per-inner domain separation, or per-level is_pad bits), the shipped fix is a more surgical 4th variant: `merkle_root` now wraps the inner-tree root in a final hash that binds the leaf count — `root = SHA256(0x02 ‖ be_u32(leaf_count) ‖ inner_root)` (`src/crypto/merkle.cpp::merkle_root_wrap`, prefix `0x02` distinct from `0x00` leaf / `0x01` inner). `merkle_verify` re-derives the inner root from the proof exactly as before, then applies the same wrapper with the CALLER-supplied `leaf_count` and compares to the committed `root`. A forged count `M ≠ N` yields `SHA256(0x02‖M‖inner) ≠ SHA256(0x02‖N‖inner) = root` → rejected. This binds leaf_count without changing the `merkle_leaf_hash` / `merkle_inner_hash` / `merkle_proof` signatures (no direct-caller churn) — only `merkle_root` + `merkle_verify` internals change. The light-client (`light/verify.cpp`) calls `crypto::merkle_verify` directly, so it inherits the binding with no separate change.

**Wire-compat note.** Every chain's `state_root` value changes (the root is now the wrapped hash). Shipped pre-launch as a free break — no installed base to migrate. Determinism/round-trip invariants are preserved (only the value moved), so snapshot + state-root determinism tests stay green.

**Test flipped.** `determ test-merkle-proof-tampering` scenario (12) — which pinned the old "leaf_count=8 in a 5-leaf tree ACCEPTED (pinned limitation)" — was inverted to assert the forged count is now REJECTED. **Closure follow-up:** the root-SHAPE expectations in `determ test-merkle` (single-leaf == bare leaf_hash) and `determ test-merkle-tree-balanced` (assertions 2–5, unwrapped inner-root shapes) were MISSED by this flip — they fired when the wrap shipped, but the failures were masked by the runner's then PASS-first marker detection and surfaced only when `tools/run_all.sh` went FAIL-first. Both now recompute the `SHA256(0x02 ‖ be_u32(leaf_count) ‖ inner_root)` wrap inline (commit 31702c2).

---

### S-041 — merge-threshold scalars omitted from snapshot serialize/restore — ✅ Mitigated in-session

**Severity:** Operational (Low) • **Status:** ✅ Mitigated • **Source:** discovered while authoring `proofs/SnapshotDeterminismComposition.md` (the F5 composition proof flagged the `k:` namespace's merge-threshold sub-fields as the one set of state_root-contributing scalars not provably round-tripped); closed in the same session. S-037-class (same shape: a state-root-bound field omitted from the JSON snapshot path).

**Pre-fix description.** `Chain::build_state_leaves` (`src/chain/chain.cpp` ~line 392) emits three `k:` namespace constant leaves for the R7 merge-detection thresholds — `merge_threshold_blocks`, `revert_threshold_blocks`, `merge_grace_blocks`. These are genesis-pinned operational params (`GenesisConfig` defaults 100 / 200 / 10) that gate when an under-quorum shard triggers a merge. But `Chain::serialize_state` and `Chain::restore_from_snapshot` did not emit/read them (they handled the sibling `k:` scalars `min_stake` / `suspension_slash` / `unstake_delay` / `block_subsidy` / `subsidy_mode` and the `c:` supply counters, but skipped the three merge thresholds). Concretely (pre-fix), on a chain configured with **non-default** merge thresholds:

1. Snapshot taken on the donor serialized every `k:` scalar except the three merge thresholds.
2. Restore on a fresh node left `merge_threshold_blocks_` / `revert_threshold_blocks_` / `merge_grace_blocks_` at their member-initializer defaults (100 / 200 / 10).
3. `Chain::compute_state_root()` over the restored state diverged from the donor's (the three `k:` leaves carried different values). The S-033 gate at `restore_from_snapshot` rejected the snapshot with `"state_root mismatch"` (genuinely firing post-S-038).

**Why this shipped silently.** The in-memory `StateSnapshot` apply-rollback path (`create_state_snapshot` / `restore_state_snapshot`, chain.cpp ~560–631) DID round-trip all three fields, so consensus apply/rollback was never affected. Only the JSON `serialize_state` / `restore_from_snapshot` path (snapshot bootstrap) had the gap. And every existing test fixture used the **genesis defaults** (100 / 200 / 10) — with defaults, restore's fallback re-supplied the identical values, so `compute_state_root` matched trivially and no assertion tripped. The bug was only observable on a non-default-merge-threshold chain, which no test exercised. (`test-snapshot-roundtrip` case #10 and `test-snapshot-full-determinism` both used default thresholds pre-fix.)

**Why this was operational, not a security regression.** Fail-loud — restore refuses the snapshot under the S-033 gate. No silent state corruption, no equivocation surface, no funds at risk. The pragmatic harm was "can't bootstrap a non-default-merge-threshold chain via snapshot" — full chain replay still worked (replay reconstructs the thresholds from genesis).

**Mitigation shipped.**

1. `snap["merge_threshold_blocks"]` / `snap["revert_threshold_blocks"]` / `snap["merge_grace_blocks"]` emission added to `Chain::serialize_state` immediately after the `unstake_delay` block (keeping the A5-promoted `k:` group together).
2. Symmetric readback in `Chain::restore_from_snapshot` via `snap.value("merge_threshold_blocks", uint32_t{100})` (and 200 / 10 for the other two) — the genesis-default fallbacks make pre-fix snapshots (which lack these keys) load identically to before, preserving backward-compat.
3. Regression coverage tightened in two existing in-process tests: `test-snapshot-roundtrip` gained case #10b, which sets NON-default thresholds (137 / 311 / 29), round-trips, and asserts both field-for-field survival AND `compute_state_root` preservation — the assertion that would have failed pre-fix. `test-snapshot-full-determinism` (the all-10-namespace fixture) now carries the same non-default thresholds through its full splice → restore → re-serialize → state_root path, so the `k:` merge-threshold sub-namespace is exercised by the comprehensive guard alongside the other nine namespaces.

**Test coverage delta.** From "every snapshot fixture used default merge thresholds (gap invisible)" to "two fixtures carry non-default thresholds through the JSON round-trip and assert state_root identity." FAST=1 suite: 133/133 PASS post-fix.

---

### S-042 — Light-client trustless reads bound `state_root` to a daemon field — ✅ Mitigated in-session

**Severity:** High • **Status:** ✅ Mitigated (Closed) • **Source:** discovered while reconciling the F6 light-client soundness proofs (`proofs/StateRootAnchorSoundness.md §3.4 / §6.4`) against the shipped reader code — the proofs honestly flagged that the recompute-and-successor binding they specify was ABSENT in the readers, which trusted the daemon's reported `state_root`/`block_hash` fields. Confirmed HIGH, fixed in the same session.

**The gap.** The K-of-K committee Ed25519-signs `compute_block_digest` (`src/node/producer.cpp::compute_block_digest`), which **EXCLUDES** `state_root` (the exclusion is intentional and load-bearing for S-038 — it lets the producer populate `body.state_root` after the committee has gathered its signatures over the digest; §4.3). `state_root` is bound to the block ONLY via `Block::signing_bytes` (appended when non-zero, `src/chain/block.cpp:347`) → `block_hash = compute_hash() = SHA256(signing_bytes ‖ creator_block_sigs)` (`block.cpp:356-365`). But the `rpc_headers` RPC **strips** the heavy fields `signing_bytes` needs — `transactions`, `cross_shard_receipts`, `inbound_receipts`, `initial_state` (`src/node/node.cpp:2658-2661`) — so a light client **cannot** recompute `block_hash` from a stripped header. The trust-minimized readers therefore reported the daemon's `state_root` and `block_hash` FIELDS as "committee-verified" while in fact trusting them.

**The attack.** Because the committee digest does not cover `state_root`, a malicious daemon (or relay) can take a genuine header, **swap its `state_root` field** to a value reflecting a forged account/stake/governance state, and serve it with the genuine `creator_block_sigs` and a matching-but-fake `block_hash`. The committee-sig check (`light/verify.cpp::verify_block_sigs`, over the digest) still passes — the digest is unchanged. The trustless readers (`balance-trustless`, `nonce-trustless`, `verify-state-root`, and the stake / supply / account-history / receipt-inclusion / merge-state / param-change / registrant / dapp-registration / account verifiers) then anchored Merkle state-proofs against the forged root and reported the result as committee-verified → **forged balance / state passed off as cryptographically attested.**

**The fix (shipped).** New helper `light/trustless_read.cpp::committee_bound_state_root(rpc, committee_json, anchor_index)` (`:333-411`, declared `light/trustless_read.hpp:174-205`). For an anchor at index `H` it: (1) fetches the **FULL** block at `H` via the `"block"` RPC — `Node::rpc_block` returns the complete unstripped body (`src/node/node.cpp:2623-2627`), so `signing_bytes`/`compute_hash` are recomputable; (2) recomputes `b.compute_hash()` locally (binding `state_root` via `signing_bytes`); (3) fetches the committee-signed SUCCESSOR header at `H+1` and verifies its committee sigs (MD then BFT); (4) requires `successor.prev_hash == recomputed block_hash(H)` byte-for-byte (`:391-406`); (5) returns the anchor's `state_root` only after that match. A swapped `state_root` field changes the recomputed `block_hash`, which then fails the committee-signed `prev_hash` match (the committee never signed that state) → throws. The chain HEAD has no committee-signed successor, so the helper **fails closed** ("NO committee-signed successor yet … retry once the chain advances one block", `:366-375`) rather than reporting an unbound head root. **ALL trustless state readers now route their reported `state_root` through this helper** — `read_account_trustless` + `verify_state_root_at` plus the stake / supply / account-history / receipt-inclusion / merge-state / param-change / registrant / dapp-registration / account paths in `light/main.cpp`. This mechanizes exactly the transitive-forward binding `proofs/StateRootAnchorSoundness.md §3` describes (`state_root(H) ∈ signing_bytes(H) ∈ block_hash(H) = prev_hash(H+1) ∈ digest(H+1)`).

**Scope of the change.** The fix is **entirely light-client-side**. The consensus `compute_block_digest` (and its deliberate `state_root` exclusion) and `Block::signing_bytes` are **UNCHANGED** — no wire-format or consensus break. Tests: `tools/test_light_state_root_binding.sh` (offline mechanized witness — full-block recompute + successor binding; a swapped `state_root` field is REJECTED; the head fails closed); the live forgery-catch (daemon swaps `state_root` post-signing → reader throws) runs on CI. Proofs reconciled: `StateRootAnchorSoundness.md §3.4 / §6.3 / §6.4 / §8`, `CompositeStateReadSoundness.md` CR-1, `LightClientThreatModel.md` T-L3 mechanism note + §4.4.1 + L-5 — all updated from "mechanism specified but code trusts the field (residual/honest-daemon caveat)" to "mechanism IMPLEMENTED via `committee_bound_state_root`."

---

### S-043 — `on_contrib` verified Phase-1 contribs without the S-030-D2 `proposer_time` re-bind — ✅ Mitigated in-session

**Severity:** Critical (liveness; zero safety impact) • **Status:** ✅ Mitigated (Closed, fix `992cf2a`) • **Source:** discovered live — the first cluster boot attempted on this Windows box failed with every node logging `invalid Contrib sig` against every peer; tracing the verify site exposed the sender/receiver commitment-formula asymmetry. (The long-standing "clusters don't run on Windows" assumption was, in fact, THIS bug: daemons boot and gossip fine on Windows; no cluster anywhere could mint a block.)

**The gap.** Commit `f99eeb8` (S-030-D2 timestamp reconciliation) extended the Phase-1 contrib commitment with a `DTM-TS-v1 ‖ proposer_time` tail and shipped three of its four halves: the **sender** binds it (`producer.cpp::make_contrib` → `make_contrib_commitment(..., m.proposer_time)`, non-zero on every production contrib via `now_unix()` — `node.cpp::start_contrib_round`); the **wire** carries it (`ContribMsg::to_json`/`from_json`; the binary envelope wraps the per-type JSON verbatim, so both negotiated codecs preserve it); the **block validator** re-binds it (`validator.cpp::check_creator_commits`, `pt_at(...)`). The fourth half — the **gossip receiver's** recompute in `Node::on_contrib` — kept the pre-feature 7-argument call, so the defaulted `proposer_time = 0` took the helper's short-circuit and produced the pre-feature commitment shape. The recomputed digest therefore NEVER matched the signature on any production contrib.

**The impact.** Every honest contrib was rejected at every peer (`invalid Contrib sig`); Phase-1 could never gather K contribs; rounds spiraled through abort claims and re-rounds indefinitely. **Total block-production outage on every multi-node deployment** running at-or-after `f99eeb8`. No safety impact: the failure direction was reject-everything — no invalid message could be accepted. The regression shipped silently because the in-process suite either passes `proposer_time = 0` (short-circuit, byte-identical pre-feature commitments) or exercises the validator path; nothing exercised the sign → serialize → deserialize → receiver-recompute → verify chain, and no cluster test ran between `f99eeb8` and discovery.

**The fix (shipped, `992cf2a`).** Two arguments: `on_contrib`'s sig-verify recompute now passes `msg.proposer_time`, and the S-006 equivocation evidence's `digest_a` recompute passes `existing->second.proposer_time` (the evidence's `sig_a` verifies downstream only against the FULL commitment the equivocator signed). The core-only equivocation DETECTION comparison stays 4-arg per its documented S-016 rationale. **Commitment format, wire bytes, and signer behavior all UNCHANGED** — the receiver now verifies exactly what the f99eeb8-authorized sender already signs and what `proofs/TimestampReconciliationSoundness.md` T-3 documents. Legacy `proposer_time = 0` contribs keep the byte-identical pre-feature commitment via the helper's short-circuit.

**Validation + regression lock.** Live on a local 3-node cluster: pre-fix `tools/test_light_verify_block_sigs.sh` 1/5 (chain stuck at height 1); post-fix 5/5 PASS and `tools/test_light_verify_chain.sh` 4/4 PASS with 37 blocks minted + committee-verified; FAST=1 159/159 unchanged. New in-process lock `determ test-contrib-wire-verify` (`tools/test_contrib_wire_verify.sh`, in FAST=1): 9 assertions pinning the full wire+verify chain — including the regression class itself (the pre-fix 7-arg recompute MUST reject a production contrib) and the T-3 tamper authentication. **Process lesson:** a consensus feature whose commitment/digest formula has both a signer half and a verifier half needs a wire-roundtrip-verify test per message type at the moment it ships — sender-side and validator-side coverage does not substitute for the gossip receiver's recompute.

---

### F-7 — Light-client fail-closed on F2 / cross-shard blocks (capability gap) — ✅ Mitigated in-session

**Severity:** Low/Op (light-client-local **capability gap** — *not* a vulnerability) • **Status:** ✅ Mitigated (Closed, commit `dc7bb3e`) • **Source:** discovered while exercising `verify-receipt-inclusion` on a shard chain that had applied a cross-shard transfer — the trustless read threw on the F2 block that applies the inbound receipt.

**Classification (why this is not a deployment-blocking vulnerability).** The pre-fix verifier was **false-negative-safe**: `light_compute_block_digest` could not reproduce the committee-signed digest of an F2 block, so `verify_block_sigs` failed and `verify_chain_to_head` raised `std::runtime_error` *before reaching any verdict*. The only possible outcomes were therefore **error / UNVERIFIABLE — never a false `INCLUDED`** and never a forged balance/state passed off as verified. A capability gap (the client can't verify something it should be able to) rather than a soundness break (the client accepting something it shouldn't). No malicious-daemon leverage existed; the worst case was the honest client refusing to act.

**The gap.** `light/verify.cpp::light_compute_block_digest` was a deliberate PARTIAL mirror of `src/node/producer.cpp::compute_block_digest`. It bound the v1 core + `partner_subset_hash` + `timestamp` tail but OMITTED the three F2 view roots (`inbound_receipts` / `equivocation_events` / `abort_events` roots) — because those collections are STRIPPED out of the `rpc_headers` reply (`transactions`, `cross_shard_receipts`, `inbound_receipts`, `initial_state`), so from a stripped header the light client could not reconstruct them. Consequence: every committee sig over an F2 block (a cross-shard / reconciled block) failed to verify, and because the receiver shard applies an inbound cross-shard receipt IN an F2 block, `verify-receipt-inclusion` AND every trustless read (balance / stake / supply) were structurally unusable on any shard chain that had applied a cross-shard transfer — broken on its primary use case.

**The fix (shipped — user-chosen Path 3, "complete the light digest").**
1. **`light/verify.cpp::light_compute_block_digest` (`:124-191`)** is now a FULL byte-for-byte mirror of `producer.cpp::compute_block_digest`. It binds EVERY field the node does, including the three conditional F2 view roots, gated on the SAME data-driven conditions as the node (`:146-168`). The four F2 helpers (`hash_cross_shard_receipt` / `hash_equivocation_event` / `hash_abort_event` / `compute_view_root`, `:47-93`) are re-implemented as file-local statics because the light client deliberately does not link `producer.cpp`. A stripped header → empty collections / zero view roots → every F2 gate is false → the digest collapses to the v1 core, byte-identical to a non-F2 header (header-only sync of non-F2 blocks unchanged). A full block → collections populated → binds the same inbound/eq/abort roots the committee signed → reproduces the node digest exactly.
2. **`light/trustless_read.cpp::verify_chain_walk` (`:198-248`)** gained an F2 full-block fallback. When a stripped header's committee-sig check fails (after the MD→BFT retry), it re-fetches the FULL block via the `"block"` RPC and re-verifies against the now-F2-aware digest. **SOUNDNESS PIN (`:227`):** the full body's recomputed `compute_hash()` MUST equal the stripped header's chained `block_hash` (the value `verify_headers` already chained into `prev_anchor`), and committee sigs are unforgeable — so a doctored body (digest changes, K sigs fail) or a mis-positioned but validly-signed substitute body (block_hash pin mismatch) is rejected. This is the same full-block-recompute trust step `committee_bound_state_root` already relies on (S-042). The fallback fires only on F2 blocks.

**Scope of the change.** Entirely light-client-side. `src/node/producer.cpp` is **UNTOUCHED**; no wire-format or consensus break.

**Guard / tests.** The static parity guard `tools/test_block_digest_xbinary_parity.sh` was tightened from "light == producer MINUS the 3 F2 roots" to **"light == producer EXACTLY"** (full byte-parity — strictly STRONGER; `LIGHT_SEQ="$PRODUCER_SEQ"`); passes live + `SELFTEST=1` (the selftest now also asserts a light copy that DROPS an F2 root is flagged RED — the post-F-7 failure mode). Validation: `tools/test_light_verify_receipt_inclusion.sh` (applied receipt → INCLUDED at the F2 block via the fallback; anti-false-positive assertions intact), `tools/test_light_verify_block_sigs.sh`, `tools/test_light_verify_chain.sh`, `tools/test_light_stake_trustless.sh` all PASS; FAST=1 unchanged. Proofs reconciled: `proofs/LightClientThreatModel.md §7 F-7` + §3.2 + §8, `proofs/LightClientCompositionMap.md` (the L-2 digest note + the §3.1 lemma list).

---

### S-044 — K=2 committees wedge permanently under timing skew (abort-cascade) — ✅ Mitigated in-session

**Mechanism (historical).** At K=2 the abort-claim quorum was K−1 = 1 (`Node::on_abort_event` validated a "K−1 claim quorum"), so a SINGLE phase-1/phase-2 straggle by either committee member produced an immediate abort quorum against it — no corroboration. Each abort event transiently desyncs `aborts_gen` across peers (contribs whose `aborts_gen` doesn't match the local `current_aborts_.size()` are dropped, `node.cpp:2113-2116`), so the next round's selected member often appeared silent to its peers → another single-claim abort. `current_aborts_` cleared ONLY on block accept (`node.cpp:2454`) — no decay, no expiry, no retry — so exclusions accumulated until the eligible pool fell below K, at which point `check_if_selected` silently returns forever. (The later S-050 stall valve added a second, safety-neutral scratch clear on total stall — `RoundStallValveSoundness.md` C-1 — a full one-shot reset with its own re-sync, not the rejected F-b wall-clock decay.) BFT escalation could not rescue K=2: k_bft = ⌈2K/3⌉ = 2 = K (no committee shrink), and avail ≥ k_bft fails once the pool is down to 1.

**Evidence (live, 2026-06-11).** Three independent cluster tests at 2000 ms test timers: `test_weak_3node` (K=2-of-3 AND K=2-of-4), `test_web_hybrid` (K=2-of-3, wedged at height 3-4), `test_regional_shards` (K=2-of-3, wedge at height 3). Hybrid selection and finalization were themselves CORRECT — the defect was purely sustained liveness.

**Fix (F-a, shipped this session).** The abort-claim quorum is now `max(2, K−1)`, computed by the single shared helper `chain::abort_claim_quorum()` (`include/determ/chain/params.hpp`) and routed through by ALL three consensus sites in lockstep (the S-043 lesson — a producer/validator quorum asymmetry is a total-outage class): producer formation (`node.cpp::on_abort_claim`), gossip adoption (`node.cpp::on_abort_event`), and the validator's exact-count check (`validator.cpp::check_abort_certs`). For K≥3 the floor equals K−1 (bit-for-bit no change — every theorem, FA4/FA5/FA6 pipeline, and existing block unaffected). At K=2 it makes the 2-claim quorum **unsatisfiable** (only one eligible claimer exists against a given missing member, since `claimer != missing_creator`), so no single-claim abort event can ever form: the cascade is structurally eliminated. K=2 thereby becomes a crash-stop 2-of-2 unanimity system (a genuinely dead member halts the height with no abort-based reseat — but that is strictly rarer than, and more diagnosable than, the healthy-node timing-skew cascade; FB67 §4.1 argues halt-by-single-death is the better failure mode for K=2). **Additionally the `web` init-default profile was retuned M=3/K=2 → M=4/K=3** (`PROFILE_WEB` / `PROFILE_WEB_TEST`), so the default deployment is K≥3 with MD margin 1 and is not exposed at all.

**Residual (by design).** K=2 remains a crash-stop posture with no fault tolerance — operators wanting fault tolerance deploy K≥3 (the shipped default now does). The verify-genesis advisory prints a K=2 crash-stop NOTE.

**Regression lock.** `tools/test_s044_gate_surface.sh` was rewritten to pin the FIXED shape (C5/C6: quorum floor via the shared helper in both writers + its `max(2,K−1)` body; C9: web K≥3; SELFTEST 10/10 catches a revert). `tools/operator_liveness_posture.sh` still audits any genesis for residual K=2 exposure.

---

### S-045 — BFT escalation unreachable at the default threshold (counter freeze) — ✅ Mitigated in-session (was High, upgraded from Medium)

**Mechanism (historical).** `check_if_selected` escalates to a BFT round only when `avail < k_target && bft_enabled && total_aborts >= bft_escalation_threshold && avail >= k_bft`; otherwise, with `avail < k_use`, it **silently returns**. Rounds are what generate abort events — so the counter's ceiling at a stuck height was whatever the LAST runnable round produced, and with no abort decay or retry timer, once that ceiling was below the threshold, escalation was frozen out permanently. At the genesis-default `bft_escalation_threshold = 5`, M=K=3 with ONE dead member froze at `total_aborts ≤ K−1 = 2 < 5` (FB67 T-4) — **the common one-dead-node failure permanently halted M=K profiles at defaults.** Pure-distinct accumulation needs pool N ≥ θ + k_bft (T-2.1), which no shipped nominal pool satisfied.

**Fix (shipped this session).** The genesis-default `bft_escalation_threshold` is lowered from 5 to **1** (`include/determ/chain/genesis.hpp` GenesisConfig struct default, plus the four mirror defaults in `genesis.cpp` / `node.cpp` config loaders / `node.hpp` Config / `validator.hpp` — all moved in lockstep to keep a field-absent genesis producer/validator-symmetric). θ=1 is reached by the **first** abort event (which always forms — independent of the timestamp-collapse duplicate-adoption path FB67 F-2 flags), so the escalation counter can never freeze below the threshold. Escalation is therefore reachable exactly when it is *viable* — `avail ∈ [k_bft, K)` — and no earlier: gate 1 (`avail < k_target`) still returns MD-mode when MD margin exists (so a single fault in a margin-having pool like the new web 4/3 runs a full K-of-K MD round, never escalating), and the F-a `max(2,K−1)` claim floor means an abort event needs ≥2 honest observers, so a single Byzantine node cannot manufacture one to force BFT mode (the `S025BFTEscalationSoundness` `A_premature` forced-escalation attack stays closed — the validator's per-event `i >= threshold` recount over real `b.abort_events` is unchanged and still requires genuine certificates). Lower θ = escalate sooner when *genuinely* stuck = more FA5-conditional blocks, an analyzed-sound tuning trade (S025 §5.3; FB67 §4.3). This makes candidate fix F-c (a formation-failure certificate) unnecessary: the counter no longer starves.

**Residual (by design, not a defect).** The `avail < k_bft` permanent wedge — a pool that has lost more than its escalation headroom (`M − k_bft`) of members — is untouched, correctly: below `k_bft` the BFT committee itself is unstaffable and the designed recovery is R7 under-quorum merge (FA9 `UnderQuorumMerge.md`) or operator action, not automatic in-shard escalation.

**Validation + regression lock.** Live: `tools/test_bft_escalation.sh` (M=K=3, kill 1) at the θ=1 default mints MD blocks then escalates to a BFT round on node-kill (34 MD + BFT block, node-proposer). Static: `tools/test_s044_gate_surface.sh` C8 pins the θ=1 default (SELFTEST R8 catches a bump back to 5). `tools/operator_liveness_posture.sh` audits a genesis/config pair for any residual `θ > K−1` under-provisioned-pool exposure offline.

---

### S-046 — `determ start` silently ignored unknown arguments (CLI fail-open) — ✅ Mitigated in-session

`cmd_start` parsed only `--config`/`--data-dir` and dropped everything else on the floor: `determ start --listen-port X --rpc-port Y --genesis G --peer P` "started" a node with NONE of those applied — init-default ports (7777/7778), no genesis pin, no peers — and no diagnostic. Concretely, multiple such invocations race for the same default ports (losers FATAL with bind errors) and any survivor runs the legacy zeros-genesis path. This is precisely how `tools/test_tactical.sh` shipped broken and stayed CI-invisible. Every other subcommand guards its flags; the node *launcher* failed open. **Fix (`16f0a09`):** strict argv — any argument other than `--config <file>` / `--data-dir <dir>` prints a usage error pointing at config.json (where per-node settings live) and exits 1. Validated: unknown-flag invocation rejected; `--config` path unchanged (cluster tests boot + pass on the rebuilt binary); FAST=1 160/160.

---

### S-047 — one-shot consensus broadcasts wedge the chain on a single missed message — ✅ Mitigated in-session

**Mechanism (historical).** Every round message was broadcast exactly ONCE: abort claims at the single timer fire (the timers never re-armed — "timeout fires only emit a claim"), the assembled abort EVENT once at quorum formation (a receiver that missed it — e.g. during a height transient, `on_abort_event` line-1413 gate — could never adopt later events either, since they hash-chain and committee-validate against the receiver's `current_aborts_`), and contribs/sigs once at phase start. Three distinct permanent-wedge modes followed, all reproduced in-process by `test-fa-liveness-virtual`'s failover phase (kill 1 of 5): (1) a lost claim volley left both live committee members stuck in CONTRIB forever — never IDLE, so never re-selected, while off-committee nodes' `in_creators` guard drops the claims; (2) a missed abort event split `aborts_gen` generations that then mutually dropped each other's messages; (3) **asymmetric death** — the crashing member's last contrib/sig reached SOME peers only, splitting the two live members across phases so their claims landed in different `(round, missing)` buckets and the `max(2, K−1) = 2` quorum could never assemble from 2 divergent claimers. The live cluster suite never surfaced any of these because it never kills a node mid-run; `test_bft_escalation.sh`'s kill happened to produce the symmetric case.

**Fix (shipped this session).** `Node::rebroadcast_round_state_locked()` + re-arming timeout handlers: each phase-timer fire (now phase-gated against stale queued expiries and re-armed at `tx_commit_ms`/`block_sig_ms` cadence) relays the node's FULL stored round state — `current_aborts_` events in chain order (a generation-behind receiver adopts sequentially; each adoption re-derives the committee the next event's claimers were drawn from), then ALL stored contribs, then ALL stored sigs. Relaying others' messages is protocol-sound: every entry is signed by its AUTHOR and re-verified at the receiver — a relay can re-deliver but not forge — and this is precisely what heals asymmetric death: a peer holding the dead member's pre-death contrib re-seeds it to the peers that missed it, the phase-split committee converges, and the round either finalizes or forms a genuine abort quorum. All re-broadcasts are byte-identical stored originals, so every receiver dedup path (event_hash match, contrib core-commit match, sig digest match) drops repeats instead of reading S-006 equivocation. Zero wire-format and zero digest change — pure delivery-layer liveness.

**Validation.** Golden consensus vectors byte-identical; live `test_weak_3node` green; the failover loop went from 5/12 permanent wedges (one-shot) to 0 wedge-mode failures with the full relay (the residual failure mode is S-048, a different defect); survivor clusters now recover so fast they finalized 130+ blocks in the harness's 90-second window.

---

### S-048 — minority same-height fork cannot reorg (resolve_fork unwired; append-only sync) — ✅ CLOSED (owner-authorized head-reorg wired, A4, 2026-07-12)

**Mechanism.** The abort-vs-finalize race can produce two validly-signed blocks at the SAME height: one part of the committee completes phase 2 on generation g while another forms an abort quorum and re-runs the round at generation g+1 — both blocks carry legitimate committee signatures for their respective abort generations, and the validator accepts either. `Chain::resolve_fork` (S-029) defines the deterministic preference (heaviest sig set → fewer aborts → smallest hash) and is unit-tested — but (pre-fix) NO Node path called it: a node that already applied the minority block dropped the majority block (old index), and the sync path is append-only, so the forked node could never reorg.

**Fix (owner-authorized, pre-launch register A4; design `docs/proofs/BoundedReorgDesign.md`).** A depth-1 head reorg is now wired into the live accept path. `Chain::revert_head()` (A4.1) reverts exactly the current head — restoring the pre-head state from the A9 rollback snapshot retained on apply-success, dropping the block, clamping `persisted_count_` so the store rewrites the tail — bounded to one block (genesis is the finality floor; a second revert without an intervening apply is refused). `Node::maybe_reorg_to_locked` (A4.2), called from the same-height branch of `apply_block_locked`, gates structurally (same parent, distinct content, `resolve_fork` prefers the competitor, revertible head) BEFORE any mutation, then reverts → validates the competitor against H-1 → appends the winner, or re-appends the old head verbatim if the competitor fails validation (fail-closed against a Byzantine block that wins the structural tie-break with invalid sigs) or if its apply throws (S-102, closed 2026-09-16 — until then an apply throw escaped after the revert and the head was lost; `determ test-node-reorg-guard`). `resolve_fork` is a total order, so every honest peer converges on the same winner — no oscillation. Normal operation is byte-neutral (the reorg is a strict no-op on the constant gossip-duplicate stream; FAST 216/0 both platforms).

**Validation.** The live 5-node `test-fa-liveness-virtual` cluster is 10/10 with `[node] S-048 REORG … replaced head` firing (the fork still arises under the abort-race; the node now reorgs to converge) and ZERO "cannot reorg" markers — direct evidence the defect is closed. `test-node-reorg-s048` (A4.3) is the deterministic repro: a non-producing follower fed a producer block + a same-height competitor over the real gossip path, covering WINNER (reorg) / REPLAY (identical-seed byte-identical, via the `SeededRng` seam) / LOSER (head kept) / INVALID (rejected after revert, old head restored). Commit `e22d857` (wiring), `d661d05` (revert primitive), `05d612a` (RNG seam), `dcb49be` (A4.4 sync rejoiner). Adversarial review GO.

**A4.4 (SHIPPED `dcb49be`) — sync-path rejoiner.** `request_next_chunk` fetches from `height()-1` so the one-block replacement arrives as a candidate (was `prev_hash mismatch` forever), and `on_chain_response` routes a block at `height()-1` into `maybe_reorg_to_locked`. A `progressed` guard (head advanced OR head-hash changed) stops a Byzantine peer spinning the fetch loop; the pre-revert gate 3b (finding 4) rejects a competitor whose `creator_block_sigs` count mismatches its `creators` before any revert (the validator enforces the same size invariant — sound, and it caps the fail-closed revert churn a padded competitor can force). Gate: `test-node-reorg-s048` scenario 5 (winner delivered via CHAIN_RESPONSE, not gossip) + `test-fa-liveness-virtual` REJOIN 10/10 ("restarted node caught up … real sync path"); FAST 216/0 both platforms; soundness `docs/proofs/BoundedReorgSoundness.md` (REORG-1..7).

**A4.5 (SHIPPED `d8265c0`) — reorg-during-save crash-consistency.** A reorg clamps `persisted_count_` below the on-disk manifest height, so `save_incremental` rewrites the tail block file in place (no longer append-only); a crash between that rewrite and the manifest update would leave `manifest{N, OLD}` beside `<N-1>.json=WINNER` → `load()` bricks on a head_hash mismatch (a fail-closed store that cannot boot). Fix: track `persisted_manifest_height_` (the height the on-disk manifest claims, NOT clamped by `revert_head`); when it exceeds `persisted_count_`, `save_incremental` writes a shrink manifest to `persisted_count_` FIRST — the H-1 finality floor it then names is provably never reverted (depth-1 bound), so every subsequent crash window reloads a consistent shorter chain and re-syncs. Byte-neutral (the shrink fires only post-reorg; the `write_file_atomic` crash seam defaults disarmed). Gate: `test-chain-reorg-save-crash` — a DANGER PROOF reproducing the pre-fix brick + a FIX PROOF driving the real `save_incremental` through a crash-injection seam at every atomic file boundary (6 assertions) — + FAST 217/0 both platforms; soundness `docs/proofs/BoundedReorgSoundness.md` REORG-6; adversarial-review GO (zero surviving findings; closed-window enumeration independently re-derived). Documented non-blocking residuals: process-crash (not power-loss/`fsync`) durability — the pre-existing store-wide assumption, unchanged; and a benign short-chain legacy transient (a crash mid-reorg-save can reload H-1 while the sibling `chain.json` holds N — consistent, self-re-syncing, never a brick). **S-048 is now closed end to end.** Ridealong: `test-node-reorg-s048` was ~10% flaky (it generated node keys with OS entropy, so `resolve_fork`'s smallest-block-hash tie-break was random per run and `craft()`'s 30-delta grind sometimes failed); switched to fixed Ed25519 seeds → 30/30 deterministic on both platforms.

---

### S-021 through S-029 (quick-fix summary)

| ID | Title | Quick fix |
|---|---|---|
| S-021 | Chain file integrity not cryptographically verified | ✅ **Closed.** `chain.json` is now a wrapping JSON object `{head_hash, blocks}`. `head_hash` is hex of the latest block's `compute_hash()`, which transitively covers every prior block via the `prev_hash` chain + committee signatures. On load, after parsing + replay, the recomputed head digest is compared to the stored `head_hash`; mismatch → throw `"chain file: head_hash mismatch (tampering or corruption?)"`. Legacy array-form chain.json is still accepted (no-op fallback); the next save() upgrades the format. Test: `tools/test_chain_integrity.sh` 4/4 PASS. |
| S-022 | 16 MB message limit too permissive — but snapshots use it | ✅ **Closed.** `Peer::read_body` retains the 16 MB framing-layer ceiling (`kMaxFrameBytes` — needed for SNAPSHOT_RESPONSE / CHAIN_RESPONSE), then applies a per-message-type cap from `max_message_bytes(MsgType)` after deserialization. Caps: 1 MB for consensus chatter (CONTRIB / BLOCK_SIG / ABORT_CLAIM / ABORT_EVENT / EQUIVOCATION_EVIDENCE / HELLO / STATUS_* / TRANSACTION / GET_CHAIN / SNAPSHOT_REQUEST); 4 MB for BLOCK / BEACON_HEADER / SHARD_TIP / CROSS_SHARD_RECEIPT_BUNDLE; 16 MB only for SNAPSHOT_RESPONSE / CHAIN_RESPONSE. Oversize messages drop + close the connection (same disposition as framing-layer overflow). The default branch in the switch keeps the cap tight at 1 MB so a future MsgType variant added without explicit categorisation cannot slip through unbounded. |
| S-023 | RPC `send`/`stake` skip balance check | Pre-check balance before queueing. ~1h. |
| S-024 | Deregistration timing predictability (1-10 block grind window) | ✅ **Accepted.** Auditor's own reclassification ("acceptable per auditor's own re-classification"). The 1-10 block grind window matters only at the brief moment between a `DEREGISTER` tx landing and `inactive_from` taking effect; the window is bounded and offers no leverage against finalised chain state. A v2.X enhancement could mix a future block hash into `derive_delay` to remove even this bounded predictability if a deployment's threat model requires it. No code change in v1.x. |
| S-025 | `compute_tx_root_intersection` is dead code | Delete it or guard under `#ifdef DETERM_INTERSECTION_MODE`. 5 minutes. |
| S-026 | No connection timeout / keepalive | ✅ **Closed.** `Peer::Peer` constructor now flips `SO_KEEPALIVE` on every accepted/initiated socket via asio's `keep_alive(true)` option. Dead connections (network partition, peer crash without FIN, NAT-rebind timeout) are detected by the kernel at OS-default keepalive intervals and surface through the existing on_close path (which evicts the peer from `GossipNet::peers_`). Detection latency follows the OS defaults — Linux ≈ 11 min, Windows ≈ 2 hr — bounded but slow; operators wanting faster detection tune the system-level knobs (`net.ipv4.tcp_keepalive_*` on Linux, `Tcpip\Parameters\Keep*` registry keys on Windows). Per-socket override of the interval is non-portable so we deliberately use the OS-level knob rather than couple the protocol to platform APIs. |
| S-027 | Info leakage in logs / error messages reveal state | ✅ **Closed.** Two-part closure: (1) **Audit pass** over every `std::cerr` / `std::cout` site in `src/node/node.cpp`, `src/rpc/rpc.cpp`, `src/net/*.cpp`, `src/chain/*.cpp` — no secret material (privkey, passphrase, HMAC auth token, recovery envelope) reaches the logs. Wallet CLI flows (`determ-wallet recover`, `determ-wallet shamir combine`) print user-requested recovery secrets to stdout by design (the user is asking for their own secret). Node/RPC paths log only chain-public state (block heights, hashes, domain names), peer addresses, and timing markers. (2) **Operator quiet-mode flag** — `Config::log_quiet` (default `false`). When `true`, the chatty per-block `[node] accepted block #N creators=K` line is suppressed; WARN/ERROR diagnostics continue to surface. Production operators wanting fewer logs set `log_quiet = true` in `config.json`. Additional `log_quiet`-gated suppressions can be added per-line as operators report log-volume issues. |
| S-028 | Hex parsing only accepts lowercase | ✅ **Closed.** Three-part fix per the deferred-closure plan: (a) `is_anon_address` now accepts either case (still rejects malformed shapes), (b) new `normalize_anon_address(s)` helper returns lowercase canonical form for anon-shaped inputs (domain names pass through unchanged), (c) RPC read paths apply normalize at input — `rpc_balance` and `rpc_send` both lowercase the address before storage/lookup so "0xABC..." and "0xabc..." resolve to the same account. `rpc_submit_tx` takes a different approach: it REJECTS non-canonical addresses with a clear diagnostic rather than mutating, because the client's Ed25519 signature is over `signing_bytes` which embeds `tx.from` / `tx.to` byte-for-byte — server-side mutation would invalidate the signature. The strict-input rule keeps store-keys unambiguous without forcing the client to resign. Test: `tools/test_anon_address_case.sh` 3/3 PASS (balance case-insensitive query, send-to-uppercase credits canonical slot, submit_tx rejects non-canonical with diagnostic). |
| S-029 | BFT-mode multi-proposer fork-choice undefined | ✅ **Closed.** `Chain::resolve_fork` ranks by `(heaviest sig set, fewer abort_events, smallest block hash)`. Deterministic across peers — every node picks the same head when two same-height blocks arrive. **Re-derived 2026-09-17 (step 3c):** the determinism/confluence result that closes THIS finding holds unconditionally. Separately, the Level-3 smallest-hash tiebreak is grindable at one Ed25519 sign per trial by the trailing signer AND at one SHA-256 per trial by an unauthenticated relayer (`initial_state` is in `signing_bytes`, outside the digest, ignored by apply above genesis, read by no validator rule — the S-102 adjudication entry); the forfeiture that used to be cited as its price was removed by D4. That hole is OPEN, has no row of its own, and its fix is an unwritten accept rule. Harm is bounded to liveness griefing because nothing security-relevant is seeded from the block hash. See `docs/proofs/S029ForkChoiceSoundness.md` §5.3 + F-1. |

---

## 6.5. Regional sharding posture (informational, `EXTENDED` mode only)

These are not bugs — they are **inherent trade-offs** an operator accepts when choosing `sharding_mode = EXTENDED` over `CURRENT`. Each is documented so deployment specs can name the threat model explicitly. Operators choosing `CURRENT` or `NONE` are unaffected.

### T-001 — Regional capture (informational)

**Posture.** Under `EXTENDED` sharding, a shard's K-committee is restricted to validators whose registered region matches the shard's `committee_region`. Censorship resistance becomes **regional** rather than global: capturing every validator in region `R` lets that adversary produce blocks for shards pinned to `R` without input from other regions.

**What changes vs. global sharding.**
- Censor-probability per round shifts from `(f_global / N_global)^K` to `(f_in_R / N_in_R)^K`. The numerator and denominator both shrink; capture is easier in proportion to how concentrated the regional pool is.
- A captured region cannot corrupt cross-shard credits because destination shards re-derive the source committee from their own beacon view and reject signatures from unknown validators.

**Mitigation guidance.**
- Deployments that prioritize global censorship resistance should use `CURRENT`, not `EXTENDED`.
- Deployments that use `EXTENDED` should disclose the regional trust assumption in their deployment spec (which shard maps to which region, what jurisdictions cover which validators).

### T-002 — Jurisdictional / regulatory risk (informational)

**Posture.** If region `R` corresponds geographically to a single jurisdiction, a government order to all validators in that jurisdiction can force censorship or transaction reversal on shards pinned to `R`. The beacon and shards in other regions are unaffected; cross-shard receipts from compelled shards are still validated by destination committees per the protocol.

**Mitigation guidance.**
- Choose region boundaries that span multiple jurisdictions where possible (`eu-west` rather than `de-frankfurt`).
- Use the recommended geographic taxonomy from `README.md §16.5` to avoid jurisdiction-aligned regions for cross-border deployments.
- For consortium chains where regional alignment with a regulator is desirable (e.g., a national banking settlement layer), this is a feature, not a bug.

### T-003 — Network partition during `EXTENDED` deployment (informational)

**Posture.** A region losing connectivity to the rest of the network stalls cross-shard receipts for shards pinned to that region: those shards continue producing blocks internally, but their `SHARD_TIP` messages don't reach the beacon, and outbound `CROSS_SHARD_RECEIPT_BUNDLE` messages don't reach other regions.

**Effects:**
- In-shard transactions in the partitioned region continue to finalize.
- Cross-shard transactions from / to the partitioned region stall until connectivity restores.
- After the partition heals, queued receipts flow through normally (idempotency via `applied_inbound_receipts_` dedup catches anything that gets re-delivered).
- No global stall — other regions' shards keep operating normally.

**Mitigation guidance.**
- Operators of `EXTENDED` deployments should monitor cross-region connectivity (e.g., heartbeat metrics between regional beacon peers).
- Applications that span regions should expect cross-region tx latency to spike during partitions; design for eventual consistency.

### T-004 — Stake concentration feedback loop (informational)

**Posture.** Validators earn block subsidy + transaction fees from the shards they're on. If one region has materially higher transaction volume, validators concentrate there for fee revenue. Over time, that region's stake share grows; other regions thin out. A thin region becomes more vulnerable to T-001 (regional capture) and may eventually trigger under-quorum recovery (R4 — pending).

**Mitigation guidance.**
- Operators monitoring `EXTENDED` deployments should watch per-region validator counts and stake totals.
- Possible interventions: rebalance shard regions at epoch boundaries, cap per-region validator counts at registration, redistribute economic activity (genesis allocations, dApp routing).
- The S-038 invariant (`num_shards >= 3` under `EXTENDED`) bounds the worst case — even a fully-concentrated single region can't drive cascading merges across the whole deployment.

---

## 7. Mitigated since rev.7 audit baseline (or in this session)

These findings have been addressed in current code. Listed for completeness so a reader of prior audits knows what's done.

### M-A — Block-level equivocation closed-loop (was Audit 2.4 partial)

**rev.8 added** `EquivocationEvent` (two valid Ed25519 sigs over distinct digests at the same height by the same key) → `pending_equivocation_evidence_` → baked into next block → `apply_transactions` zeroed the equivocator's stake AND set `inactive_from = h+1` — **that apply consequence was removed 2026-09-16 (D4, O-1 step 3a): the event is now an evidence record only.** External-submission path via `submit_equivocation` RPC. The closed loop (submit → bake → node keeps its stake and registry entry, chain keeps producing) is verified end-to-end in [`tools/test_equivocation_slashing.sh`](../tools/test_equivocation_slashing.sh).

The narrower ContribMsg-level case is now also closed (S-006 closure in-session): `on_contrib` detects a same-generation duplicate with a different commitment, builds an `EquivocationEvent` from the two contrib commitments + their Ed25519 sigs, and routes it through the same `pending_equivocation_evidence_` buffer the block-level path uses. No new wire format or validator rule was needed — the existing `EquivocationEvent` struct and `check_equivocation_events` validator are digest-agnostic ("two distinct digests, both sigs verify under the equivocator's registered key"), so the contrib commitments slot in cleanly.

### M-B — Hybrid-mode K-of-M liveness gap → BFT escalation (was Audit 2.1)

**rev.8 added** per-height BFT escalation: after `bft_escalation_threshold` (default 5) aborts at the same height AND `bft_enabled = true` AND `pool < K` AND `pool ≥ ceil(2K/3)`, the next round produces a `consensus_mode = BFT` block. The block's committee shrinks to `k_bft = ceil(2K/3)` slots and requires the within-committee 2/3 quorum `Q = ceil(2·k_bft/3)` signatures, with a deterministic `bft_proposer` mandatorily signing. The auditor's recommendation ("either disable hybrid mode or implement proper proposer rotation") was implemented as the second option. Verified in [`tools/test_bft_escalation.sh`](../tools/test_bft_escalation.sh).

### M-C — Linear-time sync barrier → snapshot bootstrap (was Gemini "fatal architectural flaw")

**B6.basic added** `Chain::serialize_state` + `restore_from_snapshot` + `determ snapshot {create,inspect,fetch}` + `Config::snapshot_path` for fast-bootstrap. Receiver skips per-block replay; restores state directly. The cryptographic-zero-trust version still requires a state Merkle root in the block format (deferred to v2 — see S-012). Verified in [`tools/test_snapshot_bootstrap.sh`](../tools/test_snapshot_bootstrap.sh).

### M-D — Blind-abort liveness exploit → BFT escalation

The Gemini analysis's headline conclusion — "blind abort = systemic liveness problem; capitalized adversary can stall any specific transaction by burning suspension penalty" — is **substantially mitigated by M-B**. A single blind-aborting adversary can stall a transaction for ~5 rounds, then BFT mode kicks in and the chain proceeds without their signature. The exponential-suspension formula still applies but is no longer the only liveness guarantee.

### M-F — Iterated delay-hash removed (S-009 closure)

**Was Architectural Analysis §3.6 + S-009 + S-019 + S-034.**

The SHA-256^T delay-hash function has been removed from the protocol. The selective-abort defense is now a Phase-1/Phase-2 commit-reveal protocol: each committee member's `ContribMsg.dh_input = SHA256(secret_i ‖ pubkey_i)` is a Phase-1 commitment to a per-round 32-byte secret; `BlockSigMsg.dh_secret` reveals it in Phase 2; validators enforce the bind. The block's randomness `delay_output = SHA256(delay_seed ‖ ordered_secrets)` is computed once K reveals gather (commit `14bf3d6`).

**Consequences:**
- S-005 (`delay_T` not in genesis) is closed: the `delay_T` field has been removed entirely from `GenesisConfig`, `Node Config`, `TimingProfile`, and the validator (commit `1b9b086`). There's no parameter left to misconfigure.
- S-009 (constant-T / SHA-256 ASIC fallacy) is closed: the defense is now information-theoretic (preimage resistance), not time-bound. ASIC asymmetry no longer matters.
- S-019 (Phase-2 timer R-arrival spoof) is closed: `R` is computed inline once K Phase-1 contribs gather; there's no expensive computation an attacker could spoof completion of.
- S-034 (VDF `EVP_MD_CTX` allocation) is closed: no inner loop.
- S-031 (global mutex serialization) is partially mitigated — the worst case (`delay_hash_verify` with 4M iterations under the lock during piggyback) is gone.

**Trade-off accepted:** the original VDF-style selective-abort defense — "an attacker can't predict block randomness during the Phase-1 commit window because computing it requires T sequential SHA-256 ops > Phase-1 budget" — is replaced by a commit-reveal-based defense backed by the existing BFT escalation + equivocation slashing machinery. An attacker without all K committee members' Phase-2-revealed secrets still cannot predict block randomness (under SHA-256 preimage resistance); the security argument shifts from "compute-time" to "information-theoretic." **Correction 2026-09-15 (S-077):** the LAST revealer does hold all K secrets before it reveals, and a Phase-2 abort carries no slash and no suspension, so it can reject one sample per height at the cost of its seat — rejection sampling, not choice; the "backed by … slashing machinery" clause does not hold for Phase-2 withholding.

**Cleanup landed.** `crypto/delay_hash.{hpp,cpp}` files are deleted. The `RUNNING_DELAY` consensus phase, the `delay_worker_` thread + `delay_cancel_` / `delay_done_` flags, the validator's `delay_T_` field + `set_delay_T`, and the test scripts' `c['delay_T'] = 200000` config writes are all gone (commit `1b9b086`). The M=K=1 single-validator chain recursion that the worker thread's `asio::post` used to break is now broken by an inline `asio::post` inside `enter_block_sig_phase`.

### M-E — Outbound HELLO mistagging in cross-chain peering

**Was Architectural Analysis §3.5.** Outbound `GossipNet::connect()` at `gossip.cpp:45` was sending `make_hello(domain, port)` — the 2-arg overload defaulting `role = SINGLE, shard_id = 0`. Inbound (accept-loop) at `gossip.cpp:33` correctly sent `make_hello(domain, port, our_role_, our_shard_id_)`. The mismatch silently broke `SHARD_TIP` and `BEACON_HEADER` propagation for outbound-initiated cross-chain peering: the receiving end's role-based gossip filter (`peer_message_allowed`) dropped messages from peers it had stamped as SINGLE.

**Fixed in this session.** `gossip.cpp:45` now passes the full 4-argument form. All 8 regression tests pass.

### M-G — Governance mode + PARAM_CHANGE shipped (A5)

**New in this session.** Genesis-pinned `governance_mode = 0|1` selector with `param_keyholders` + `param_threshold`. Validator enforces a whitelist of 9 mutable parameters (MIN_STAKE, SUSPENSION_SLASH, UNSTAKE_DELAY, bft_escalation_threshold, tx_commit_ms, block_sig_ms, abort_claim_ms, param_keyholders, param_threshold) with N-of-N multisig. Off-list parameters require a new chain identity.

**Soundness proof:** `docs/proofs/Governance.md` (FA10). Cumulative false-positive bound: `≤ 2⁻⁴⁵²` for N=5 keyholders + Q=2⁶⁰ adversary budget.

**Integration test:** `tools/test_governance_param_change.sh` — 3-of-3 keyholder genesis, MIN_STAKE 1000 → 2000 mid-chain, verified via snapshot inspect.

### M-H — Under-quorum merge shipped (R4)

**New in this session.** `MERGE_EVENT` tx type (TxType=7) with canonical 26+region_len byte payload. Genesis-pinned thresholds (`merge_threshold_blocks=100`, `revert_threshold_blocks=200` for 2:1 hysteresis, `merge_grace_blocks=10`). Eligibility stress branch in producer + validator extends the committee pool with refugee-region validators when this shard is absorbing. Snapshot save/restore preserves merge state.

**Soundness proof:** `docs/proofs/UnderQuorumMerge.md` (FA9). Demonstrates FA1 (safety) and FA7 (cross-shard atomicity) preservation across BEGIN/END transitions.

**S-036 — STRONGLY MITIGATED (v2.11, the D3 SHARD_TIP arc; not CLOSED — trustless closure is owner-gated Layer 2 / D3.5e).** The reachable exploit is **fail-closed**: MERGE_EVENT is rejected on every non-BEACON chain at both the mempool-admission and block-validation layers (D3.6, `1fda852`), so a fabricated-distress MERGE_BEGIN submitted to a shard can no longer dilute the partner committee (this reconciles the code to the long-standing `block.hpp` "MERGE_EVENT valid only under BEACON chain role" contract). The distress substrate is on-chain and committee-verified: source shards emit a digest-bound, K-of-K-signed `eligible_count` (D3.4); a BEACON folds contemporaneously-verified `ShardTipRecord`s into the `t:` state namespace, made deterministic across the K co-signers by an F2-style full-K Phase-1 reconciliation (D3.5a–d-ii); and the BEACON MERGE_BEGIN branch admits a window only on contiguous sub-2K source-attested `t:` coverage, uniform fail-closed on any absent in-window record (`A_beacon_omit`), pre-ring, or `threshold==0` (D3.6 historical-witness). The witness is proven by the D3.7 falsifier (`test-s036-merge-witness`: genuine distress accepted, every fabrication rejected, shard fail-closed, deterministic). **Residual (why not CLOSED):** the BEACON witness path has no emitter yet and no beacon→shard merge propagation — a beacon-coordinated merge FLOW plus a trustless source-committee anchor (the `sc:` cross-chain transport + beacon-genesis per-shard `K_0^s`) is the owner-authorized Layer-2 piece. See `docs/proofs/ShardTipMergeDesign.md` + `docs/proofs/ShardTipMergeSoundness.md`.

**Integration test:** `tools/test_under_quorum_merge.sh` — BEGIN inserts state, END erases, snapshot persists.

### M-I — Wallet recovery primitive shipped (A2)

**New in this session.** Greenfield `determ-wallet` binary providing distributed seed recovery via T-of-N Shamir SSS layered with AES-256-GCM AEAD envelopes. Single scheme: `passphrase` (`shamir-aead-passphrase`) — each share envelope's unwrap key is derived from the user's passphrase via PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA-256 with a per-envelope random salt, sealed under AES-256-GCM with a guardian-bound AAD. All wallet crypto runs on `determ-crypto-c99` (the libsodium-free C99 primitive layer) with OpenSSL EVP for the AEAD/KDF envelope; the wallet links **no libsodium** — like the daemon and `determ-light`, it links OpenSSL + `determ-crypto-c99` only. Pubkey-checksum gate prevents silent reconstruction corruption. (An earlier `--scheme opaque` OPAQUE-adapter branch — non-production, self-flagged as an offline-grindable stub — was de-scoped and deleted per `DECISION-LOG.md` 2026-07-03, along with the `oprf`/`opaque` CLI commands; recovery is now passphrase-only.)

**Binary isolation:** the wallet handles secret material; the chain daemon never has access to user seeds.

**Soundness proof:** `docs/proofs/WalletRecovery.md` (FA12). The dominant attack path is offline passphrase grind, bounded by `Q · 2^-bits_password / KDF_cost`; the PBKDF2 iteration count inflates per-guess cost and passphrase min-entropy is the sole gate (no online guardian rate-limit backstop) — operators must provision passphrase entropy accordingly.

**Integration tests:** 3 wallet recovery test suites (shamir, envelope, recovery) plus the broader wallet suite.

### M-J — Full formal-verification track shipped

**New in this session.** Every v1.x safety-critical mechanism has both an analytic proof (FA-track) and a TLA+ state-machine specification (FB-track):

- F0 Preliminaries + FA1–FA12: safety, censorship, selective-abort, liveness, BFT-mode safety, slashing soundness, cross-shard atomicity, regional sharding, under-quorum merge, governance, economic soundness, wallet recovery.
- FB1 Consensus.tla + FB2 Sharding.tla + FB3 Receipts.tla + FB4 CHECK-RESULTS.md (TLC is wired into CI as of 2026-09-23 — the `tla` job).

Every theorem cites its source-code enforcement points. A reviewer can trace any property end-to-end: theorem → state-machine model → implementation. Concrete bounds tabulated in `docs/proofs/README.md` §"Concrete-security summary".

The original "5-6 weeks of engineering" estimate has been substantially absorbed in-session. Remaining gates to permissionless-deployment-ready posture:

3. ~~v2.10 threshold randomness aggregation~~ — **block-beacon application de-scoped** (MPDH commit-reveal retained; rationale in `docs/proofs/FROST_DEVIATION_NOTICE.md` §9). No longer a permissionless-readiness gate: the residual selective-abort is accepted under MPDH (re-roll + suspension; the round-1 stake deduction was retired 2026-09-16, D13). FROST is removed from the chain path entirely (`docs/proofs/FROST_DEVIATION_NOTICE.md`); the C99 code is library-only.

Total remaining: the v2.7 F2 consensus closure is shipped and v2.10 is de-scoped, so no multi-day architectural gate remains — and with `timestamp` now bound via median reconciliation (commit `f99eeb8`, on top of `partner_subset_hash` in commit `8585a50`), S-030 is now fully digest-closed with no remaining residual. Beyond that (v2.X), Theme 8 (privacy + interop) + Theme 9 (DSSO) extend the design space toward god-protocol completeness for Determ's lane. FROST is **removed from the chain consensus path entirely** (`docs/proofs/FROST_DEVIATION_NOTICE.md`); the FROST C99 code is library-only. v2.25 DSSO rides the profile-agnostic NIST P-256 OPRF stack (Ed25519 for committee + user-credential keys; X25519 for share-envelope transport only); v2.22 confidential transactions ride the profile-agnostic NIST P-256 shielded pool — neither uses FROST (nor ristretto255, which was never adopted); Beaconless-v2 cross-shard randomness uses commit-reveal aggregation; DSSO uses threshold-OPAQUE (t-of-n P-256 T-OPRF, `docs/proofs/v2.25-DSSO-DAPP-SPEC.md`).

## 11. Versioning

This document tracks current state at HEAD. As findings are mitigated, they move from §3-§6 to §7 with a brief note describing the fix. New findings are added with the next available `S-NNN` ID. The triage table in §2 is the canonical entry point for any review.
